the docstring in `corpus/emitAudits.ts` still describes the x64 population as live. **Reaching 0 is nevertheless the WRONG target, and that stays true after g186**, because the flattering number is reachable by the opposite change, and that is measured rather than argued: a variant naming all 35 takes it to 0/0/0/0 and moves **no other number in the report**, including `offsetof` fields and distinct definitions, while printing `void sub_1400027C8(int64_t arg_0, …) { arg_0 = rbx; arg_1 = rsi; arg_2 = rdi; arg_3 = r12; }` — four declared parameters immediately overwritten by the callee-saved registers being parked in them. g186 reaches the same 0 by declaring **no** parameter there, so the row cannot tell the two apart and the instrument that can is the emitted parameter list read against `objdump`. Nothing else here sees the class: an offset-named argument is a well-typed name gcc compiles, it states nothing false so polarity/`staleGuards`/`staleReads` are indifferent, it is not an admission so the unrecovered count is unmoved, and `offsetof` only checks layouts it was given — the whole point being that such a slot never reaches struct synthesis as a parameter. Negative-controlled: run over the base's own emitted C it reports exactly 156/147/141/140, agreeing figure for figure with an independently written census (`peek-a-bin-emlv`).
- **ARM64X carries machine 0xAA64 and is refused by decode rate. ARM64EC does NOT carry 0xAA64 — it is marked x64 — and this bullet asserted the opposite for months.** Settled against Microsoft's own documentation, since no such binary exists here (`peek-a-bin-3ucw`): a **final ARM64EC image carries `IMAGE_FILE_MACHINE_AMD64` (0x8664)**, per [Microsoft's Arm64EC overview](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/arm/arm64ec), which shows `link /dump /headers` reporting `8664 machine (x64) (ARM64X)` and states that only the linked EXE or DLL carries "the `8664 (x64) (ARM64X)` or `AA64 (ARM64) (ARM64X)` machine value", and per lld (`case ARM64EC: coff->Machine = AMD64`, [D149086](https://reviews.llvm.org/D149086)); an **ARM64X image carries 0xAA64 by default** — "By default, Arm64X binaries appear to be Arm64 binaries", so Windows 10 on Arm can still load one ([Arm64X PE Files](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/arm/arm64x-pe)) — and may instead be marked 0x8664, which is what makes it launch as emulated x64; and **0xA641 / 0xA64E never appear in a linked image's machine field**, 0xA641 being an object/lib marker Microsoft calls "not a valid final PE machine type" (`dumpbin`'s `(ARM64X)` annotation comes from the hybrid metadata, not from this word). Three consequences, and the second is the one to carry. **The refusal's real population is an ARM64X image marked 0xAA64** plus an image whose machine word is not describing its bytes — it never sees an ARM64EC one, which is a narrower claim than this bullet used to make. **An ARM64EC image, and an ARM64X marked 0x8664, take the `"x86"` arm and are disassembled as x64 with nothing said**, which is a real misclassification and not a benign one: Arm64EC code is *native A64* following x64 software conventions, so the x64 decoder reads it as fiction and an x86 linear sweep decodes essentially any byte string — the same silent failure `arch.ts` records for ARMNT. It is **knowingly left alone**, because routing on CHPE there would be a *decision* taken on a field that has never once been read on an x64 image here: measured at `11408ac`, `t64.exe` and `w64.exe` have **no load-config data directory at all**, so both read `undefined`, and the only two images on this machine that read the field are the genuine ARM64 pair, which read 0. And an ARM64EC image's `.pdata` is read with the **x64** schema, which `peek-a-bin-c71x` has since settled as the **right** reading of the directory the header names and an **incomplete** reading of the image — see the two-exception-tables gotcha below, which is also why `pe/pdata.ts` no longer accepts 0xA641/0xA64E as a superset. The evidence actually used is the bytes: sweeping a code section with an A64 handle decodes 97.4%/97.7% of the two real ARM64 binaries against 21.8–27.9% of four x86/x64 ones. **~A quarter of arbitrary x86 bytes decode as *something* in A64** — that density is exactly why the failure was silent, and why the floor is 50% rather than anything near 100%. `disassembleArm64` throws `Arm64DecodeRateError` below it on sections of 256+ words; `detectArm64Functions` catches and degrades via `DetectResult.omitted`, because `.pdata`, the exports and the unwind handlers are the linker's own record and stay true. **The calibration is now a GATE in both directions** (`npm run corpus:arm64`): `coffHeader.machine` is the oracle, so an accepted 0xAA64 image must be above the floor and every 0x014C/0x8664 image must be below it, and moving the floor either way turns a row red — negative-controlled at 0.2 (the four x86 binaries pass, red) and at 0.99 (both ARM64 images refused, red). The printed table re-derives this docstring's figures exactly and adds w32 at 12288/2743. Stated limitation, unchanged and **not** narrowed by the gate: an ARM64X image is a good share genuine A64 and may pass the floor, and there is none on this machine (`peek-a-bin-2t1`).
- **A HYBRID IMAGE HAS TWO EXCEPTION TABLES, `pe/pdata.ts` READS ONE OF THEM, AND THE ONE IT READS IS THE ONE THE MACHINE WORD DESCRIBES — so machine-keying is right here and could never be right for the other.** Settled against documentation at `peek-a-bin-c71x`, unobserved for the usual reason: there is no ARM64EC or ARM64X binary on this machine. An ARM64EC image holds native A64 code following x64 software conventions **and** genuine x64 code, and each half has its own function table in its own architecture's format:

  | image, as it lies on disk | `IMAGE_DIRECTORY_ENTRY_EXCEPTION` | CHPE `ExtraRFETable` |
  |---|---|---|
  | ARM64EC (marked 0x8664) | **x64**, 12-byte entries | **ARM64**, 8-byte entries |
  | ARM64X (marked 0xAA64, default) | **ARM64**, 8-byte entries | **x64**, 12-byte entries |

  The invariant is that the exception directory always holds the table of the architecture the image *presents itself as*, and `ExtraRFETable` — a field of `IMAGE_ARM64EC_METADATA`, reached through `loadConfig.chpeMetadataPointer` — always holds the other one. For ARM64X the **ARM64X dynamic-value relocations swap both** when the image is loaded into an x64/EC process, so its hybrid view is byte-for-byte an ARM64EC image's layout and its on-disk view — what a static reader sees — is the ARM64 one. Six things:
  - **Three independent sources, none of them a secondary account, and all three read directly.** lld's COFF writer says it outright — `// ARM64EC (but not ARM64X) contains x86_64 exception table in data directory` over `machine == ARM64EC ? hybridPdata : pdata`, where `hybridPdata` is declared `// x86_64 .pdata sections on ARM64EC/ARM64X targets` and `mergeSections` splits `.pdata` in two by `chunk->getMachine() == AMD64`; the same file points `__arm64x_extra_rfe_table` at the *complement* (`machine == ARM64X ? hybridPdata : pdata`) and, for ARM64X, emits `IMAGE_DVRT_ARM64X_FIXUP_TYPE_VALUE` relocations over both the `EXCEPTION_TABLE` data directory and `offsetof(chpe_metadata, ExtraRFETable)` ([Writer.cpp](https://github.com/llvm/llvm-project/blob/main/lld/COFF/Writer.cpp)). Wine's loader routes by **code range** rather than by image: `RtlLookupFunctionTable` answers `ExtraRFETable`/`ExtraRFETableSize` when `RtlIsEcCode(pc)` and `IMAGE_DIRECTORY_ENTRY_EXCEPTION` otherwise, and the x64 `RtlLookupFunctionEntry` delegates an EC pc to the ARM64 one, which searches `ARM64_RUNTIME_FUNCTION` `{BeginAddress, Flag, FunctionLength, UnwindData}` — the 8-byte form `parseArm64Pdata` decodes ([unwind.c](https://github.com/wine-mirror/wine/blob/master/dlls/ntdll/unwind.c)). And Microsoft's [Arm64EC ABI page](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/arm/arm64ec-abi) requires *dynamically* added EC unwind entries to "be in Arm64 format", noting `RUNTIME_FUNCTION` is the x64 shape when compiling EC so `ARM64_RUNTIME_FUNCTION` must be named explicitly; its worked entry-thunk listing is ARM64 unwind codes (`E7 save_any_reg`, `E6 save_next_pair`, `E1`, `E4 end`).
  - **So `parsePdata`'s answer is RIGHT for every hybrid case and INCOMPLETE for all of them.** The directory really does hold what the machine word says — including for both hybrids, in both of ARM64X's views — and the other half of the image's functions is in a table nothing here reads. That is a narrower and more useful statement than `3ucw` could make, which was "whether the x64 path is right is not established".
  - **Reading the second table was deliberately NOT attempted, and that is the same refusal `3ucw` recorded.** `chpeMetadataPointer` has never once been observed non-zero on any file here — measured at `11408ac`, `t64`/`w64` have no load-config directory at all, the ARM64 pair read 0, and the PE32 pair declare a structure too short to reach the field — so a consumer of it would be unverifiable in both directions. What a future reader needs is written down instead of half-built: `ExtraRFETable` is at **0x40** and `ExtraRFETableSize` at **0x44** of `IMAGE_ARM64EC_METADATA` (all-`ULONG` fields, `Version` first), both RVAs, and the schema is the **complement** of the machine word.
  - **The two hybrid machine constants are GONE, and one of them was WRONG rather than merely unreachable.** `isArm64Machine` sent `IMAGE_FILE_MACHINE_ARM64EC` (0xA641) and `ARM64X` (0xA64E) down the ARM64 path as a "deliberate superset", on the reasoning that it would be right if either ever reached a `coffHeader`. The table refutes half of that: 0xA641 means an ARM64EC image, whose exception directory is the **x64** table, so the arm would have read 12-byte entries at an 8-byte stride — the `peek-a-bin-kwc` desynchronisation `parsePdata`'s own docstring opens by warning about, arrived at from the other direction. 0xA64E's intention was right and is already served by 0xAA64, the word an ARM64X image actually carries; keeping an unreachable duplicate of a correct answer beside a refuted one only invites re-adding both. **Do not re-add either.**
  - **The deletion is provably output-neutral and is pinned by a test rather than by a gate**, since neither constant reaches a linked image: `npm run corpus` byte-identical, `corpus:arm64` 51/51. `pdata.test.ts`'s new cases use a well-formed **x64** fixture so they show what the withdrawn arm *would have done* — at an 8-byte stride those 24 bytes read as one packed entry claiming a 0x1054-byte function at 0x1000, fiction and longer than the whole table describes — and the negative control (restore the arm) fails exactly those two tests and nothing else.
  - **NOT VERIFIED, and it must be said as plainly as `3ucw` said it: this rests entirely on documentation.** No ARM64EC or ARM64X binary exists on this machine, so neither table has been seen, no `ExtraRFETable` value has been read, and the ARM64X fixup swap has never been observed. Two things are inferences rather than quotes even so: that a *static* reader of an ARM64X file sees the un-fixed-up ARM64 view follows from the relocations being *dynamic*, and the ARM64EC/ARM64X split of `pdata`/`hybridPdata` is read off lld's code rather than stated in prose by Microsoft.
- **…and where the image DECLARES CHPE metadata the refusal says so, instead of inferring hybrid-ness from the rate.** `PEFile.loadConfig.chpeMetadataPointer` (`peek-a-bin-7p5t`) is the format's only declaration of hybrid-ness, and it now reaches `Arm64DecodeRateError` through `App` → `disasmClient.configure` → `WorkerState` → `Arm64Context`. Six things (`peek-a-bin-3ucw`):
  - **It is consumed for PROSE ONLY, and that is what makes it safe rather than what makes it cheap.** `peek-a-bin-7p5t` deliberately left the field with no consumer because "a wrong CHPE reading that starts rejecting genuine ARM64 is worse than the current heuristic, and it cannot be tested here". That reason does not apply to a message: the throw condition, the `decoded`/`words` fields and every caller's behaviour are untouched, so the failure mode it declined to risk is **structurally impossible** here. Pinned by a test that refuses and accepts the identical section under all three states of the field.
  - **It is OPTIONAL, not a dependency.** An image with no load-config directory, a structure too short to reach the field, or a caller that was never threaded produces exactly the message this threw before. Three states, and only a **non-zero** value is evidence: `0` means present-and-not-hybrid, which says *not* hybrid, and `undefined` means unreadable — collapsing those two is what would make the field useless.
  - **It names ARM64X rather than "ARM64EC or ARM64X"**, which is sharper than `peek-a-bin-3ucw` anticipated and follows from the machine-type finding above: this sweep is only ever reached for an image marked ARM64, and a final ARM64EC image is marked x64.
  - **Session state on the worker, not a per-request field** — the opposite of `machine`, and for a stated reason: `peek-a-bin-x4o2`'s hazard is that session state cannot decide the architecture of a message posted before the `configure` that set it, and this decides nothing. A decode serviced early throws the pre-existing message. It is keyed on **`machine` being declared** rather than on its own presence, because a declared machine type is the load handshake: guarding on its own presence instead leaves one file's CHPE pointer attached to the next file's refusal, which is a test.
  - **Not in any cache key.** `Arm64SweepCache`'s answer is a function of the bytes, the load address and the decoder, so a fourth argument for a message must not be able to cause a miss — that would make one file's prose cost another file's sweep. Tested by identity of the returned array plus the Capstone call count.
  - **`mcp/disasm.ts` is deliberately NOT threaded**, and that is a proportionality judgement rather than an oversight: its three entry points are 7-to-10-parameter functions, the consumer there is a tool client rather than a person, and the field's own contract already says an untreaded caller gets the previous message. `mcp/session.ts` holds the `PEFile`, so the value is one field access away if that ever changes.

  **NOT VERIFIED against a real file, and this cannot be fixed here**: there is no ARM64EC or ARM64X binary on this machine, so the non-zero branch is fixture-only. Measured at `11408ac`, the only two images here that read the field at all are `t64-arm.exe` and `w64-arm.exe`, both reading **0** — so the hybrid sentence cannot fire on any file available to test — while `t64.exe` and `w64.exe` have **no load-config directory** and the two PE32 binaries declare 0x48 bytes where the 32-bit field needs 0x80. Six negative controls, each failing 1–3 tests and only its own: the throw dropping the argument, the cache keying on it, `0` treated as evidence, `armCtx` not forwarding it, `configure` guarding on its own presence, and the client dropping it from the message.
- **`.pdata` is authoritative for x64 function boundaries and beats prologue scanning.** Where a `.pdata` range exists, a prologue-byte or padding-heuristic candidate strictly inside it is a re-detection of a function already known exactly, not a new function: t64 went 511 → 279 functions, 232 overlapping pairs → 0, 93 fully-contained → 0, 53 empty decompiled bodies → 16. Evidence about an *entry point* — call target, jump-table target, export, entry point, unwind handler, `.pdata` begin — still wins inside a range. PE32 has no `.pdata` to arbitrate and still over-produces (`peek-a-bin-abv`).
- **An MSVC x86 `__finally` funclet is not a function, and the SEH scope table must not be used to PROTECT one.** The funclet is emitted *inside* its parent, reached by a `call` from it, ends in `ret`, and the parent resumes on the next byte — so it is a call target, hence a function start by every other rule, and since sizes are distance-to-next-start it cuts its parent in half and the parent loses every `jcc` aiming past the new end. `interiorBranchedOverStarts` (`functionDetect.ts`) withdraws such a start when nothing outside the previous function calls it *and* a conditional jump that function can actually execute crosses it; that is what took t32 from 293 to 288 functions at `95229d3`, folding five funclet bodies into the five parents, with the instruction stream byte-identical either way. The trap for later: **the file does name two of those five** — 0x4058A6 and 0x4063B8 are handler entries in their parents' scope tables in `.rdata` (`{EnclosingLevel -2, Filter NULL, Handler …}`, and a NULL filter means `__finally`) — so `strong` means "named by a table the parser reads", not "named by the file". Parsing that table and feeding its handlers into `strong` would re-break `sub_4031A4`, whose funclet the table names at 0x403270 with the withdrawn 0x403276 six bytes inside it. The table's real content is *funclet-of-parent*, which belongs in a relation, not in a set that protects a start (`peek-a-bin-sysf`, `peek-a-bin-gtlf`). **That relation has since landed — `src/disasm/seh32.ts` and the fifth admission, `peek-a-bin-d827` — and the warning above is now MEASURED rather than reasoned**: feeding the tables' named addresses into `strong` instead re-introduces **9 withdrawn starts on t32 and 7 on w32** at `f3b89ec`, 0x4058A6 and 0x4063B8 among them, each cutting its parent in half again. Two corrections to the sentence above, both checked against the bytes: the records are at 0x411228 and 0x4112B8 and it is their *handler field* that sits at 0x411230 and 0x4112C0, and the layout is a 16-byte header (`GSCookieOffset`, `GSCookieXOROffset`, `EHCookieOffset`, `EHCookieXOROffset`) before the first 12-byte record.
- **…and `interiorBranchedOverStarts` now has THREE admissions, not one, because a tail funclet is not branched over at all.** The rule above needs the parent to have code *past* the funclet, and most of MSVC's x86 funclets sit at the parent's **tail**, after its last `ret`. So 8 starts on t32 and 6 on w32 survived it (at `99203fb`, which is also where the function counts below were taken) — every one a `push dword ptr [ebp + N]; call; pop ecx; ret` or the 46-byte pair at t32 0x40a631 / w32 0x40921c — and each was emitted as a standalone function reading `*(int32_t*)(ebp + 8)` with **nothing in it assigning `ebp`**. The third admission is a property of the candidate rather than of its surroundings: **`[ebp + N]` in the FIRST instruction at an address is a read of a frame some other function established**, because nothing has run there yet to set one up and no x86 calling convention passes EBP. Withdrawn, the same read is `arg_0` — the parent's own slot, nameable because the frame is now the frame of the function the read sits in, and `t32!sub_401DB3` overwrites `[ebp+8]` at 0x401e05 so the funclet reads the *updated* argument. Four things (`peek-a-bin-qe8z`):
  - **At least one caller is REQUIRED, and it is the whole soundness argument.** The second admission (below) is about a start nothing reaches; this one is the opposite. A candidate nothing calls is not a funclet, and there is then no independent evidence the address is even an instruction boundary — the "first instruction" would be whatever a byte pattern happened to decode as.
  - **THE WRONG VERSION IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY GATE AND THE FUNCTION COUNT REWARDS IT.** Drop the pre-existing "no caller outside the previous function" test and the rule also swallows `__SEH_epilog4` (`t32!sub_4041B5` / `w32!sub_404415`), which opens `mov ecx, dword ptr [ebp - 0x10]` — reading its caller's frame **by design** — and is called from **31 and 29 sites**; its body ends up as `loc_4041B5:` inside `__SEH_prolog4` while all 31 call sites still print `sub_4041B5()` for a function that no longer exists in the output. `npm run corpus` reports that as clean, with `functions` 280 → 279 and 278 → 277 — *toward* the target — and the whole tree at `99203fb` + this change moving 288/279/275/285 → **280/279/275/278**, and `distinct callees lost` **0**, because that audit counts callees the emitted C loses and the name is still on the page. Implemented and measured deliberately; pinned by a test.
  - **The first instruction is decoded from the boundary, not read off the linear sweep.** The sweep's grid is whatever the bytes in front of it produced, so its instruction at a given address can be a misalignment; the address here is one a `call` names, so it provably *is* a boundary. `firstInstructionOperands` uses `decodeOne`.
  - **`push 0xa; call; pop ecx; ret` funclets are not touched by THIS admission** — the *family* is 23 per 32-bit binary, not the 9 recorded here, and the count is corrected in the two bullets below — because an immediate says nothing about whose frame is in scope, and the only evidence left would be the sole caller, which a helper laid out right after its one caller also has. `.pdata` still outranks all of this (`pdataEndMap.has(prev)` is checked first), so it is a PE32 rule in practice.
- **…and an unconditional `jmp` over a start NOTHING REACHES withdraws the start.** `interiorBranchedOverStarts` refused an unconditional jump as evidence, correctly, because a tail call and a shared epilogue are both spelled that way — but that ambiguity is entirely about the far side of the jump, and both of those alternatives are *reached*. Where the image holds **no direct transfer to the boundary at all** — no `call`, no `jmp`, no `jcc`, no jump-table case — the readings are gone. One candidate corpus-wide, and its harm was an **invented call** rather than a lost `jcc`: `w32!sub_401981` runs to 0x401b4b, the `6a 0c 68` at 0x4019e0 (`push 0xc; push 0x40ece4`, mid-body) cut it to 95 bytes, and its `jmp 0x401aaa` then aimed outside its own range, so the lifter read it as a tail call and emitted `eax = sub_401AAA(); return eax;` for an instruction that never leaves the function — while the 363 bytes past the cut became `sub_4019E0`, reading `[ebp + 0xC]` and `ebx` off a frame it never establishes and calling `sub_4024AE()` with **no arguments at all**. Three things (`peek-a-bin-qe8z`):
  - **`reached` must be a union over every transfer kind**, not the call sites alone. `t32!sub_403A88` and `w32!sub_403CDC` are `mainCRTStartup`'s body, reached by the entry point's own `jmp` and by nothing else and called from nowhere; a call-site-only test swallows both.
  - **The reachability walk still applies.** `.text` carries data and a linear decode turns it into plausible jumps, which is why `reachableCrossings` (renamed from `reachableCondJumpCrosses`, now returning *which kinds* crossed) decodes forward from the previous function rather than reading the sweep's answer.
  - **No standing instrument saw the defect.** `wildBranches` judges branches outside the *image* and 0x401aaa is inside it; the statement-drop audit counts drops rather than inventions; `gcc -std=gnu89` accepts a call to an implicit declaration at any arity; the guard ledger was `CHANGED 0` because the guards were correct on both sides. It was found by censusing the admitting evidence for every detected start, which is the instrument this bead produced — and the literal 0x4019e0 occurs nowhere in the image, so nothing takes its address either.
- **…and the funclet has TWO entries, which is why the first three admissions kept missing most of the family.** MSVC x86 emits the register reloads only the *unwinder* needs and then the funclet body; the parent's own `call` names the **body**, past the reloads, because the parent already has the register loaded. So the body is what gets detected and the reload falls to the previous function, where it is a dead assignment whose only consumer is now in another function — `t32!sub_40388B` ended `loc_4038F4: esi = arg_0;` and nothing else, while `sub_4038F7` called `sub_4023CD()` with **no argument at all**. Adjudicated against `objdump -d -M intel`; the bead had recorded this as "two of them are SPLIT", which is a 10× undercount. The **fourth admission** is the second and third composed and asked one instruction earlier: the instruction ending *at* the boundary reads memory through the frame register, so nothing there established a frame, and it falls into a body every caller of which is inside the same function. Withdrawn, `loc_4038F4: esi = arg_0;` becomes `eax = sub_4023CD(arg_0); return eax;`, and t32 0x405f1a's trailing `loc_405F23: esi = *(int32_t*)(ebp + 8)` — a frame read in a function that never assigns `ebp`, the exact defect the third admission exists to fix — disappears. Four things (`peek-a-bin-qe8z`):
  - **THE FRAME TEST IS THE WHOLE RESTRICTION, AND ONLY THE EMITTED C CAN SEE IT.** Relaxed to "the predecessor is not a terminator", the rule additionally takes `t32!0x4037b2` / `w32!0x403a06`, whose predecessor is `call _invalid_parameter_noinfo` — a call that does not return — and the merge appends `eax = sub_406CC0(7); return eax;` to the arm containing it, **stating that control flows out of a `noreturn` call**; it takes five more per binary besides, for which there is no evidence at all. `npm run corpus` reports the relaxed version as **clean, exit 0**.
  - **Reachability is deliberately NOT tested, and that is measured.** All 12 predecessors per binary are unreached — only the unwinder enters them — so the conjunct would contribute nothing, and it is not kept as a bound because the case it would exclude is *stronger*: a reachable instruction falling into the boundary means the previous function's own execution crosses it.
  - **It is the one place here that reads an instruction it cannot decode *from*.** Nothing names the predecessor's address, so `precedingOperands` walks the linear grid from `prev` — itself a detected start — and takes whatever ends exactly on `boundary`. Landing precisely on an address a `call` *does* name is the self-consistency check: a misaligned grid would have to survive the whole previous function and then come down on a known boundary.
  - **Measured at `cc45263`, both sides pinned: 12 starts per 32-bit binary and all 24 are funclet bodies — no false positive over 558 detected starts.** `functions` 280 → **268** and 278 → **266** (t64/w64 **byte-identical**), instructions unmoved at 18025/16601, 11 surviving functions changed per binary. Every gate figure in the report is identical except the denominators that move with `functions`: gcc 268/268 and 266/266 clean, `offsetof` 346/346 and 354/354 fields over 55/55 and 59/59 definitions at ratio 1.00, polarity anchor-A 517/517 and 441/441 with 0 inverted and 0 mismatch, `guards audited` exactly flat at 919/824/821/729 with `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0`, **arity completely unmoved** (79/105 and 85/111 exact, under 26, over 0), unrecovered values 36/12/11/33, and 0 on staleReads, staleGuards, popReads, lostDefs, armExits, unencodableNames, wildBranches, callees lost, loops short, throws. `distinct callees lost` stays 0 with its denominator 970 → 957 and 935 → 922 (the merge dedups callee sets), and statements lifted falls by exactly 6 per binary — the six reloads that fold into the call they feed.
- **…and the REST of the funclet family is refused on evidence, with each refusal pinned by a test.** The census at `cc45263` corrects two figures that have been quoted at people: the family is **23 per 32-bit binary**, not the bead's "~40" nor CLAUDE.md's former "9", and it is **two shapes, not one** — 17 `push <imm>; call; pop ecx; ret` and 6 `push <callee-saved reg>; call; pop ecx; ret`, the second of which the record never described. After the fourth admission, **10 family members per binary are still detected — re-measured at `f3b89ec`, and 11 was already wrong at `cc45263` — and only 7 of those are even withdrawal candidates** — the other four have a caller outside the previous function, three of them because MSVC shares one funclet body between two parents' funclets (t32 0x40618d, 0x406196, 0x406c16) and the fourth because `t32!0x40660a` / `w32!0x4054a1` is a real four-caller helper. Anyone quoting 16, 23 or ~40 as the residue is quoting a figure taken before the fourth admission. **All 7 candidates per binary have since been withdrawn on the scope table (`peek-a-bin-d827`, below), so what follows is the record of what was refused BEFORE that evidence was available, and it is still the rule wherever the table is silent or unread.** The family size itself is instrument-dependent and should not be quoted as a fact: a shape scan over `objdump`'s whole linear grid at `f3b89ec` finds **26 per binary (20 `push <imm>` + 6 `push <callee-saved reg>`) plus one 12-byte `pop ecx; mov esi, [ebp - 0x1c]; ret` variant**, against this census's 23/17/6 — the scans differ over genuine one-line helpers of the same shape. The DETECTED subset is the number that means something. Refused, in order of how tempting they are:
  - **`push <imm>` with a `ret` predecessor: no candidate-internal evidence, and none external either.** `push 0xd; call _unlock; pop ecx; ret` is byte-for-byte what a legitimate one-line helper is, and the callee really is one — t32's `_unlock` at 0x406cc0 has 21 call sites of its own.
  - **`push <callee-saved reg>`: the tree already reads that instruction the opposite way.** No x86 convention passes EBX, so an entry point cannot mean anything by reading one — but `peek-a-bin-6lmh` established, against this same corpus, that a `push` of a callee-saved register the function has not yet written is a register **SAVE**, which is what stops `collectArgs32` walking into a prologue. Withdrawing on the value reading would put two contradictory readings of one instruction in the tree; separating them needs the whole body (there is no `pop ebx`, so the `pop ecx` balances the stack and the push was an argument), which is not a kind of reasoning this pass does. Note the fourth admission reaches all 6 of them anyway, by the predecessor — so this refusal costs nothing today and is the guard against reaching for the register instead.
  - **"the boundary is reached by fallthrough from the previous function's reachable code": 1 site per binary, measured**, and that site's predecessor is the `noreturn` `call` above. Refused on measurement.
  - **"the predecessor is unreached", with no test of what it does: 8 per binary, and ONE IS A REAL FUNCTION** — `t32!sub_40E1D8`, which opens with a full `mov edi, edi; push ebp; mov ebp, esp; push ebx` hot-patch prologue, has two callers, and is named by no scope table in the image; w32 has the same shape at 0x40c7f8. Withdrawing it deletes real code with nothing said. **The second counterexample this bullet used to name is REFUTED, by the linker's own record**: the "six-byte thunk `t32!sub_40BEBA` (`call 0x406196; ret`)" and its w32 counterpart 0x4050f4 are the `__finally` handlers of `sub_40BE84` / `sub_4050BE`, named as such by scope table 0x4113f0 / 0x40f2f8, each called exactly once from inside that parent (0x40beac / 0x4050e6), and each a funclet whose body calls the SHARED funclet body. The fifth admission withdraws both. Do not quote "two are real functions" again (`peek-a-bin-d827`).
  - **Moving a start BACKWARDS onto the unwinder entry is confirmed unjustifiable.** The true entries (t32 0x405f17, 0x4038f4, 0x40316f, …) are admitted by nothing and reached by nothing — they are not in the candidate set at all.
  - **The only sound rule left was the SEH scope table read as a funclet-of-parent RELATION, and it HAS NOW LANDED** — `src/disasm/seh32.ts` plus the fifth admission; see the bullet below. Two things this note predicted turned out not to hold. The relation did **not** need "every start inside `[handler, its ret]` is interior to P": at `f3b89ec` no detected start lies strictly inside any funclet's `[handler, ret]` on either binary — the fourth admission had already taken every start that sat past an unwinder entry — so the address-exact form and the range form are indistinguishable here and the range walk was not written. And the capability was in `src/disasm/`, not `src/pe/`: nothing in the PE directories points at the table, only the `push` in the parent's own prologue does. **Never put its handlers in `strong`** still stands, and is now measured — see the sysf bullet.
  - **AND THE COUNT-CHASING RULE IS INVISIBLE TO EVERY GATE, worse than qe8z's own trap.** Withdrawing the whole family on body shape (`push X; call <imm>; pop ecx; ret`, every caller inside prev) takes t32 280 → **262** and w32 278 → **260** — much further "toward" the target — with `npm run corpus` **exit 0**, gcc 262/262 clean, `offsetof` ratio 1.00, polarity 0 inverted, statements dropped 0, unrecovered values unmoved, `distinct callees lost` still **0** (denominator 970 → 949), and `compare.mjs` flagging **exactly the same 2 rows the correct change flags** — `functions scanned`. So no instrument in this repo distinguishes a right withdrawal from a wrong one in this population; the oracle is reading the parent's emitted C at the join, and it is what caught the `noreturn` claim above.
- **…and the FIFTH admission is not an inference at all: the previous function's own SEH scope table names the boundary.** An MSVC x86 function using `__try` opens `push <framesize>; push <scopetable>; call __SEH_prolog4`, and the `_EH4_SCOPETABLE` in `.rdata` names the filter and the handler of every scope — addresses MSVC emitted **inside** that function, running on its frame with no prologue of their own. `src/disasm/seh32.ts` reads it, `seh32FuncletRelation` turns it into `funclet -> the starts whose prologue names it`, and the admission is `parents.has(prev)`. **Verified layout** (t32.exe, w32.exe, read byte for byte and cross-checked against the 31 and 29 `call __SEH_prolog4` sites CLAUDE.md already records): a 16-byte header `{GSCookieOffset, GSCookieXOROffset, EHCookieOffset, EHCookieXOROffset}` then 12-byte `{EnclosingLevel, FilterFunc, HandlerFunc}` records with **no count field**, `EnclosingLevel` −2 meaning TRYLEVEL_NONE and a NULL filter meaning `__finally`. Measured at `f3b89ec`, both sides pinned: **8 starts withdrawn per 32-bit binary** — t32 0x403334, 0x4037b2, 0x405c2a, 0x405d64, 0x406a83, 0x406c0d, 0x406d90, 0x40beba and w32 0x403588, 0x403a06, 0x404883, 0x404c91, 0x4050f4, 0x405f8b, 0x406440, 0x4065ca — `functions` **268 → 260** and **266 → 258**, instructions unmoved at 18025/16601, **t64/w64 byte-identical**, exactly the 8 parents' emitted C changed per binary, and `polarity guards audited` **exactly flat** at 517/574/497/441 with `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0` on all four. Every gate flat: gcc 260/260 and 258/258 clean, `offsetof` **unmoved** at 342/342 and 355/355 fields, 0 inverted, 0 mismatch, 0 throws, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, 0 loops short, arity exact/under/over unmoved, unrecovered values unmoved at 36/12/11/33, staleReads/staleGuards/popReads/lostDefs/armExits/wildBranches/unencodableNames all 0, and loop shape identical (`for` 6/6/6/6, `while` 111/106/99/98, `do/while` 78/77/76/77, `if` 1987/1938/1719/1759). Seven things, and the first is what the function count cannot tell you (`peek-a-bin-d827`):
  - **THE ORACLE IS THE PARENT'S EMITTED C AT THE JOIN, and all 16 were read against `objdump -d -M intel`.** Fourteen are the textbook outcome: the funclet body appears at the end of its parent as `loc_<addr>:` holding exactly its own two lines, and the parent's `call` at the join spells the same address — `sub_4069F0` now ends `sub_406A83(); eax = sub_4041B5(); return eax;` then `loc_406A83: eax = sub_406CC0(1); return eax;`. One per binary is a real improvement the bead predicted: t32 0x405c2a / w32 0x405f8b is the 12-byte variant `push 0xc; call _unlock; pop ecx; mov esi, [ebp - 0x1c]; ret`, and the base emitted it as a standalone function with a **phantom `int32_t var_1C;` nothing read**; withdrawn, the declaration is gone and the body reads `esi = var_1C` off the parent's own slot, which is what the machine does.
  - **The sixteenth is the `noreturn` case CLAUDE.md warned about, and the warning does not survive contact with the two texts.** t32 0x4037b2 / w32 0x403a06 sits after `call 0x402c35` (`_invoke_watson`), so its body merges into that block rather than becoming its own region, and the arm gains `eax = sub_406CC0(7); return eax;` — exactly what the fourth admission's relaxed-frame-test control produced. But **the base's claim about the same path was also false and less literal**: it closed the arm at `sub_402C35(...)` and let control join `loc_403793`, which the machine's block does not branch to. The change is the byte-for-byte fall-through (0x4037ad `call`, 0x4037b2 `push 7`, 0x4037b4 `call`, 0x4037b9 `pop ecx`, 0x4037ba `ret`), and 0x402c35 **has a `ret`** at 0x402c59 — nothing in this tree models `noreturn`, so neither reading is checkable and the change's is the one the instruction stream states. It is also why `internalLabelled` rises by 7 where `internal` rises by 8: this is the one funclet with no label to name.
  - **`corpus/undefinedCallees.ts` is the instrument, and it moved by exactly one row per withdrawn start**: `internal` **25 → 33** on t32 and **23 → 31** on w32, `internalDistinct` likewise, `internalLabelled` 8 → 15 and 6 → 13, external unmoved at 7. That is the documented, accepted consequence of folding a funclet (`peek-a-bin-pf5g`) and not a new class.
  - **`parents.has(prev)` is what makes it a RELATION, and protecting the same addresses instead is now measured rather than argued.** Feeding the tables' named addresses into `strong` re-introduces **9 withdrawn starts on t32 and 7 on w32** — 0x401e67, 0x401f61, 0x402928, 0x402a51, 0x402f64, 0x40363f, 0x4058a6, 0x4063b8, 0x40d990 and the w32 equivalents — each of which cuts its parent in half again. 0x4058A6 and 0x4063B8 are the two the sysf note names, so the control lands on exactly the addresses the warning was about. (What the control could **not** exercise is the other half of that warning, *promoting* a handler into the start set: 0x403270 is not in `addrSet` at all — nothing detects it — so there is no measurement behind "0x403276 six bytes inside" and it remains a reading of the bytes.)
  - **The pre-existing "no caller outside the previous function" refusal still runs first and the table does not relax it.** The three shared funclet bodies per binary stay detected (t32 0x40618d, 0x406196, 0x406c16, called from 0x40be90, 0x40beba and 0x40233a; w32 0x402020, 0x402029, 0x4065d3), and **no scope table in either binary names any of the six** — the table names the funclet that *calls* the shared body — so the two rules agree here and the refusal costs nothing measured. `t32!sub_40660A` / `w32!sub_4054A1`, the four-caller helper, is likewise named by nothing.
  - **All three bounds inside `seh32.ts` fire 0 times on this corpus, and that is stated rather than hidden.** Dropping the `call` after the pushes, dropping the `EnclosingLevel` chain test, or letting the table address be a code address each leaves the named set at **37 and 35** and the withdrawn set at **8** — identical. They are bounds that make the claim sound by construction, not measured savings, and each is pinned by a unit test in `src/disasm/__tests__/seh32.test.ts` instead. `parents.has(prev)` is in the same position: all 16 named-and-detected addresses have their parent as the immediately preceding start, so the relation half is unfalsifiable here and is pinned by a test too.
  - **The range form of the relation was NOT written, and the reason is a measurement.** `peek-a-bin-sysf` prescribed "every start inside `[handler, its ret]` is interior to P", because the handler can be the *unwinder* entry 0–6 bytes before the detected body. At `f3b89ec` **no detected start lies strictly inside any funclet's `[handler, ret]`** on either binary — the fourth admission had already taken every start that sat past an unwinder entry — so the address-exact form and the range form are indistinguishable on this corpus and the extra decode would be untestable. If the fourth admission ever loosens, this is the first thing to add back.

- **…and a folded funclet leaves its parent calling an identifier the output never defines — MEASURED, ADJUDICATED AND DELIBERATELY NOT REPAIRED.** The four admissions fold the funclet body into its parent; the parent still contains the `call <funclet>`, so `t32!sub_40388B` emits `sub_4038F7();` at line 16 — declared nowhere — while the funclet's body sits at line 27 under `loc_4038F4:` in the same function. Both halves are faithful in isolation and they are not connected. **25 sites on t32 and 23 on w32 at `d8d2d02`, 0 on both x64 binaries** (**33 and 31 at `f3b89ec` + `peek-a-bin-d827`**, the fifth admission adding one row per withdrawn start) (`corpus/undefinedCallees.ts`; 12 per binary are the fourth admission's, the rest the earlier ones'). All 48 read against `objdump -d -M intel`: every one is a funclet with exactly one direct caller, inside its own parent. **No code change was made**, and the reasons are measurements rather than caution (`peek-a-bin-pf5g`):
  - **`goto` is wrong and re-emitting the funclet is worse.** The machine CALLs and the funclet RETURNs, so control comes back; and a funclet cannot be both a member of its parent and its own function — that is the contradiction the withdrawal exists to resolve, and re-emitting it restores `peek-a-bin-qe8z`'s defect (a body reading `[ebp+8]` off a frame nothing in it establishes).
  - **A COMMENT NAMING THE LABEL IS AVAILABLE AT ONLY 15 OF 33 AND 13 OF 31 SITES, and at the rest it would state something false.** MSVC emits the register reloads only the *unwinder* needs and the parent's `call` names the body **past** them, so at those sites the block leader is the unwinder's own entry three to six bytes earlier: at t32 0x405f1a the label is `loc_405F17` and the line under it is `esi = arg_0`, a reload the call does **not** execute. At one site per binary (t32 0x4063b8, w32 0x40224b) the funclet body is a **fallthrough continuation** of the parent's own code and carries no label at all — verified against `objdump`: 0x4063b3 `call 0x406175` falls straight into it. A comment saying only "the body is inside this function" is what the audit already reports, does not tell the reader *where*, and would put editorial into 64 functions of output for no recovered fact — the objection `peek-a-bin-qbk3` records against naming a fence in prose. (8 of 25 and 6 of 23 at `d8d2d02`; the fraction rose to 45% and the refusal was RE-TAKEN at that ratio rather than inherited — the next two bullets are that re-take.)
  - **THE COMMENT'S COVERAGE IS PERFECTLY ANTI-CORRELATED WITH NEED, and that is measured over all 64 rows rather than argued.** `resolveNamedTarget` mints `sub_<HEX>` and `labelNameFor` mints `loc_<HEX>` from the same `addr.toString(16).toUpperCase()`, so a labelled site's identifier and its label **carry the same hex string** and a reader who searches it lands on the body with no comment at all. `internalThreaded` (`corpus/undefinedCallees.ts`) asks the wider question — does the target's hex occur ANYWHERE else in this function's emitted text, label or constant or anything — and at `84eed6e` it is **15/0/0/13, equal to `internalLabelled` on every binary and in both directions**: every one of the 28 labelled sites has a thread, and at every one of the 36 unlabelled sites the hex occurs **nowhere in the function but in the call itself**. So the repair is available precisely where the call can already be followed and unavailable precisely where nothing leads anywhere. The two rows are derived independently — one scrapes `loc_<hex>:` lines, the other the bare hex with hex-digit boundaries and the `sub_` spellings excluded — so their agreement is a differential; only `threaded >= labelled` holds by construction. The corpus cannot separate them (they agree on every row), so both directions of the difference are pinned in `build/undefinedCalleeAudit.test.ts` over shapes the corpus does not contain, and three negative controls each fail their own tests: answering `threaded` from `labelled` (1 test), dropping the `sub_` exclusion — which makes **every** internal row read as threaded and the harm figure structurally 0 (3 tests) — and dropping the hex-digit boundaries (1 test).
  - **THE REOPENING'S PREMISE IS TRUE OF THE FRACTION AND FALSE OF THE HARM: read `internalUnlabelled`, not `internal`.** Between `d8d2d02` and `84eed6e` the class grew 25/23 → **33/31** (+8 per binary) and `internalLabelled` grew 8/6 → **15/13** (+7 per binary), so the unfollowable residue grew **17/17 → 18/18**, by one. `peek-a-bin-d827`'s scope-table rule folds the funclets MSVC emits with **no unwinder-only reload**, whose body is therefore its own block leader — read against `objdump -d -M intel` at t32 0x403334 (`ret` at 0x403333 before it), 0x4058a6 (`jmp` at 0x4058a4), 0x406a83 (`ret` at 0x406a82), 0x405d64 (`ret` at 0x405d63), 0x40beba (`ret` at 0x40beb9) and w32 0x4065ca (`ret` at 0x4065c9) — so 7 of the 8 starts it withdrew per binary arrive already followable, and the eighth (t32 0x4037b2 / w32 0x403a06) is the `noreturn` fall-through that merges into `call _invoke_watson`'s block. The contrast pairs are decisive and sit inside single functions: `t32!sub_4031A4` emits `eax = sub_403276();` whose nearest label is `loc_403270` — two unwinder reloads (`mov edi,[ebp-0x28]` / `mov esi,[ebp-0x20]`) the call skips — and `sub_403334();` whose `loc_403334:` is the target exactly; `t32!sub_405CB9` pairs 0x405d5b (reloads `xor edi,edi` / `inc edi` / `mov esi,[ebp+8]` at 0x405d55) against 0x405d64 (`ret` before it). **So a rise in `internal` is not evidence of reader-visible damage and must be read beside `internalUnlabelled`**, which is what `compare.mjs` now flags separately.
  - **Forcing a label at the target is a CFG change, not a spelling one.** A `call` is not a CFG edge here, so the funclet's entry is mid-block by construction; making it a block leader splits a block at a merge point, which is where loop shape is decided and which no gate models. `emit.ts` also has no line to hang a label on, because the reload folded into the body's argument and **no emitted statement carries the target's address** — which is the same fact that denies the label. That is now measured rather than asserted, and it is **stronger than the "17 of 25" this used to claim**: at `84eed6e` the emitted `lineMap` carries the target address at **2 of the 64 internal sites** (t32 0x40beba, w32 0x4050f4 — the six-byte thunks, whose whole body is one call), and at the other 62 no line does, labelled or not. So the labels that exist come from `structure.ts`'s `pushLabel` at block leaders and not from `emit.ts`'s `placeGotoLabels`, and the textual variant of this idea — hanging `loc_403276` on the line that carries 0x403278, whose expression the folded `push 0xa` is part of — is refused by `placeGotoLabels`' own docstring rule ("inventing a label somewhere else would turn a visibly broken jump into a silently wrong one") and is in any case not implementable: `stmtAddr` records one address per line and nothing in the IR says which instructions an expression absorbed.
  - **The honest outcome was the instrument, both times.** Report-only, split internal from external, with `internalLabelled`, `internalThreaded` and `internalUnlabelled` reported beside them so the refusal keeps its evidence and so the premise it rests on is re-measured on every run rather than being a paragraph that can go stale. Emitted C is **byte-identical on all four binaries** for each landing (md5 of every `funcs_*.jsonl`, `diff -r` over the `cc/` and `offsetof/` trees, `compare.mjs` `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0`, verdict "no regression") — necessarily, since only `corpus/` changed, but measured rather than assumed. **The standing upgrade condition is restated against the right quantity**: if a change ever drives `internalUnlabelled` to 0 by connecting the bodies the reader cannot reach, gating that half becomes worth re-arguing; `internal` reaching 0 is the stronger and more distant condition. **NOT verified:** nothing here read the funclet bodies of the x64 pair, because there are none; the 28 labelled sites' `threaded` flag is trivially true and only the 36 unlabelled ones' falsity is a real measurement; and no judgement was formed about whether a GNU nested function is the right long-term spelling, which would need the inline region suppressed and is therefore a lost-code risk of its own.
- **"A call target immediately after a `ret`/`jmp` is a function start" is SUBSUMED, and reviving it as *strong* evidence is measurably WORSE than inert — the heuristic is deleted and this is why.** `detectFunctions`' sweep kept `prevWasUnconditional` (set on `ret`/`retn`/`jmp`, reset across a decode gap by `prevEnd`) for exactly one reader: `if (prevWasUnconditional && callTargets.has(insn.address)) addrSet.add(insn.address)`. That add can never be new — the call branch a few lines above adds every in-section target to `addrSet` on the line **before** adding it to `callTargets`, `addrSet` is never deleted from, and `callTargets` has no other writer — so `callTargets ⊆ addrSet` holds by construction. Re-censused at **`11408ac`**: the guard fires **156/34/35/146** times on t32/t64/w64/w32 and adds a new address **0** times on all four (the same figures `peek-a-bin-x40u` took at `6f2ce28`, so the count is stable across the intervening detection movement). Five things (`peek-a-bin-7lue`):
  - **THE INTERESTING OPTION WAS THE WRONG ONE, AND IT IS REFUTED BY THE SAME CENSUS.** The tempting reading — that a call target whose predecessor is a terminator is a start nothing may withdraw, i.e. a `strongStarts` member — is exactly backwards. An MSVC x86 `__finally` funclet is a call target emitted at the **tail** of its own parent, past the parent's last `ret` (CLAUDE.md's third admission exists for that layout), so "call target after a `ret`" is a *description of the withdrawn population* rather than evidence against withdrawing it. The retired guard names **15 of t32's 33 and 13 of w32's 32** starts `interiorBranchedOverStarts` withdraws, **every one with exactly one direct caller**, and the list includes **0x4058A6** and **0x40BEBA** (w32: **0x4050F4**) — the very addresses the `strongStarts` docstring already names as its measured counterexample. Promoting them would re-cut those parents in half: **worse than the 9 and 7** that docstring measures and refuses for the scope table's handlers, because this shape names a superset.
  - **The residue accounts for itself exactly, which is what makes the 15 a measurement rather than a coincidence.** t32's 15 = 8 of the `strongStarts`-control's 9 + all 7 of `peek-a-bin-d827`'s 8, disjointly. The two missing are the two whose predecessor is a **`call`** rather than a terminator: 0x4063B8, which CLAUDE.md records as a *fallthrough continuation* after `0x4063b3 call 0x406175`, and 0x4037B2, the `noreturn` fall-through after `call _invoke_watson`. The heuristic and the record agree about which addresses it can and cannot see.
  - **The `jmp` half is ambiguous evidence on a population where its one disambiguator is false by construction.** 3 per 32-bit binary are named on a preceding `jmp`, and `interiorBranchedOverStarts` admits an unconditional straddle **only where the boundary is unreached** — a tail call and a shared epilogue are both spelled `jmp`. Every address this guard fires on is a call target, hence *reached*, so the condition that would make a `jmp` conclusive can never hold here.
  - **On x64 it is doubly vacuous**: 26 of 34 and 26 of 35 fires are already in `strongStarts` (they are `.pdata` begins), and `interior` is empty on both because `.pdata` arbitrates the boundaries outright.
  - **The heuristic's own phrasing was wrong about the machine, and the bead inherited it.** Control does **not** fall through a `ret` or a `jmp`, so what the guard read was *layout* adjacency plus a terminator — "nothing falls into this address" — which is a funclet at its parent's tail precisely. **NO GATE CAN SEE EITHER CHOICE**: `npm run corpus` is byte-identical whichever way it goes (verified — `diff -r` over the whole artifact tree finds only the label line and the artifact path, `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0` on all four binaries), so the census above is the only instrument, and it is recorded in the `callTargets` and `strongStarts` docstrings rather than left to be re-derived.

- **A TLS callback is a FILE-DECLARED entry point and belongs in `strongStarts`; and the same call site carried the mirror-image unit defect on the EXPORT seeds.** (`peek-a-bin-j4uk.2`)
  - **The evidence.** The TLS data directory's `AddressOfCallBacks` is a null-terminated array of function pointers the loader calls **before** the image's entry point, and it is exactly the class `strongStarts` already accepts: the entry point, the exports, the `.pdata` begins, the `UNWIND_INFO` handlers — a table the linker wrote, not an inference from bytes. Before this it was parsed (`parseTLSDirectory`, VAs, a 256-entry cap) and had **one** consumer, `analysis/anomalies.ts`, which told the analyst "2 TLS callback(s) found … anti-debug or pre-entry execution" while the function list held nothing whatever at that address.
  - **It needs `strongStarts` and not just `addrSet`, and the reason is structural rather than incidental.** *Nothing inside the image calls a TLS callback* — the caller is the loader — so "no caller outside the previous function" is true of every one of them, for every image, by construction. That plus a conditional jump the previous function can execute straddling the address is `interiorBranchedOverStarts`' **first admission** verbatim, so a callback landing mid-parent is withdrawn from `addrSet` and folded back into the function in front of it. `functionDetect.test.ts` pins that with the `g7yp` fixture aimed at the callback: `je` at the parent's head crossing the callback at +0x10 and landing back at +0x14. With the seed in `strongStarts` the walk skips it on its first line; with `strongStarts.add` removed and `addrSet.add` kept, that row and **only** that row reddens.
  - **Both detectors, one commit.** The two option bags in `functionDetect.ts` and `arm64.ts` are hand-synced, and the TLS directory is machine-independent — an ARM64 image registers callbacks exactly as an x64 one does — so an A64-only omission would be a silent behaviour difference rather than a symmetry gap. The A64 half seeds `addrSet` and `nameMap` only: that detector has no `strongStarts` and no withdrawal pass, so there is nothing to protect a start *from*. `dispatch.ts` needed **no edit** — it forwards `args.options` wholesale, and unlike `dataWindows` these are plain numbers, so nothing in `prepareBinaryArgs` is involved and nothing has to be packed.
  - **Seeded ahead of the entry point and the exports, deliberately.** `__tls_callback_<hex>` is a synthetic name like `__handler_<hex>`, and `nameMap.set` is last-writer-wins, so seeding TLS third (after `.pdata` and the handlers, before the entry point and the exports) is what lets a real name win at a shared address. Pinned in both suites.
  - **THE UNIT IS THE WHOLE RISK, AND NOTHING IN THE TYPE SYSTEM HOLDS IT.** Every address in the options bag is a **VA**, because `baseAddress` is; `entryPoint`, `pdataFunctions` and `handlerAddresses` all add `imageBase` on the way in. `parseTLSDirectory` reads the callback array's pointers image-based and keeps them verbatim, so the callback list is *already* in the right unit — but the two agree by two separate readings of the format, not by construction. Both sides are `number`. A caller that subtracts `imageBase` "to get an RVA like everything else" **compiles**, every callback then falls outside `[baseAddress, endAddress)`, and each is dropped with `DetectResult.omitted` byte-for-byte identical to the run that kept them — a narrower answer wearing a complete one's shape, and unreachable by `omitted` because no *pass* was skipped. That is `peek-a-bin-yrh`'s class one field over, which is why the unit is written at the option's own declaration in both detectors and why one of the negative controls is "spell the callbacks as RVAs" (it reddens the three seed rows and the strong-start row, and correctly leaves the out-of-section row and the name-precedence row alone).
  - **THE MIRROR DEFECT, VERIFIED, AT THE SAME CALL SITE.** `ExportEntry.address` is documented as an **RVA** (`pe/types.ts`) and `mcp/session.ts` passed it **raw** into that VA-keyed bag, on the line directly above an `entryPoint` that converts. So on the MCP path — **and on `npm run corpus`, which loads through this same `FileSession`** — every export seed fell outside `[textBase, textBase + len)` and the detector dropped it: no seed, no name. `App.tsx` converts (`imageBase + e.address`) and additionally filters to `.text`. **The harness and the browser had therefore been detecting functions from different seed sets**, which is why it belongs with this stage rather than in a bead of its own: one line, in an options object this change was already editing, in the same unit class and with the same failure mode. It is **structurally invisible to every instrument here** — all six corpus binaries are EXEs with **zero exports** (measured, not quoted: `npm run corpus:parserdiff` prints `0 exports` for each and marks both export gates VACUOUS) and no DLL exists on this machine. `src/mcp/__tests__/detectSeedUnits.test.ts` is the standing guard, in two halves for two reasons: a **runtime** half that parses a fixture PE32 with one named export inside `.text` and drives the real `detectFunctions` over the real parser's output, so "an RVA seed is dropped and `omitted` does not move" is measured rather than argued (no Capstone needed — detection answers from the seed tables with a null decoder); and a **static** half over `session.ts`' source text, because importing `../session` for value pulls `../disasm` and `capstone-wasm` into the MCP suite, which `importGraph.test.ts` exists to keep out. Both spellings of both fields are asserted, since the hazard is that they look identical.
  - **A note on the scrape, measured the hard way.** `argumentsOf` counts parentheses from the call's own `(`, and the first version of the new comment in `session.ts` contained the prose `[textBase, textBase + len)` — one unmatched `)`, which cut the argument list short and made all four static rows fail. Fixed by stripping comments before scanning rather than by rewording the comment, on `importGraph.test.ts`' model. This is the trap `seeds.test.ts`' own docstring warns about ("a bead id in a comment is enough parentheses to cut the list short and pass a guard that should fail"), arriving from the other side: here it made a passing guard fail, which is the benign direction, but the same scrape could as easily have stopped *before* the field it judges.
  - **The 256-callback cap now admits itself, at the drop site, decided exactly.** `MAX_TLS_CALLBACKS` is exported and the loop is `for (;;)` with three exits: the end of the buffer (a fact about the *image* being short, deliberately not spelled as this budget's admission — the same split `parseResourceDirectory` makes between an unread entry and a malformed shape), the array's own NUL terminator (the answer is whole), and the cap — reached only having *already read a further non-zero pointer*, so `TLSDirectory.callbacksTruncated` is set there and an array of exactly `MAX_TLS_CALLBACKS` entries plus its terminator is **not** marked. That is `dhcx`'s rule rather than `remaining > 0`; the control that proves it is the inferred form (`callbacksTruncated = callbacks.length >= MAX_TLS_CALLBACKS` after a counted loop), which reddens the boundary row and **only** the boundary row. It **renders nothing** — the census of which parser admissions reach a surface is `peek-a-bin-ul9m`, and `parseAdmissions`' sixth subject stays deferred as `peek-a-bin-8pod` left it. The reason it was worth landing now: before this change a clipped list only understated an anomaly sentence, and now it **under-seeds function detection**, which is worse in kind — a dropped entry costs a function start rather than a line of prose.
  - **A new `DetectPhase`, not a fold into `handler-seeds`.** `"tls-seeds"` is added to the union *and* to `corpus/detectPhaseCost.ts`' `PHASES`, whose `Exclude<…> extends [never]` check fails the build otherwise — the contract working. A phase silently attributed to another is exactly the instrument drift that file exists to prevent. Two pre-existing suites list the phases by hand and both were updated.
  - **FIXTURE-VERIFIED ONLY, AND NO BINARY ON THIS MACHINE CAN FALSIFY EITHER HALF — stated positively, because it is a reason to record the measurement rather than to skip it.** Measured at `0870e14`: all six corpus binaries have **no TLS data directory at all** (`tlsDirectory` is `undefined` for every one) and **zero exports**. So `npm run corpus` is byte-identical over the four x86 binaries with `functions` unmoved (260 / 279 / 258 / 275), 27/27 gates, `VERDICT: no regression`; `npm run corpus:arm64` is 51/51; `npm run corpus:parserdiff` is 98/118 with the same 20 vacuous rows. That is the *expected* result, and it means the entire case for both halves rests on synthetic fixtures plus reading. Named holes: **nothing exercises `App.tsx`'s threading** — the corpus path goes through `FileSession` and no DOM suite asserts the option — no real image has ever had a TLS callback seeded, the `callbacksTruncated` flag has never been produced by a real file, and no DLL has ever been loaded by this tool on this machine, so the export fix has never been *observed* to recover a single start.

- **A jump-table case target is not a function start.** `detectFunctions` used to add every case target to the function-entry set, and function sizes are the gap to the next entry in that same set — so the function holding the `jmp [table]` ended at its first case and each case body became a bogus function. Downstream, `buildCFG`'s range guard rejected every target as a block leader, the indirect-jmp block got **zero** successors, and `structureSwitch` was dead code on real input: **no `switch` had ever been emitted for a real binary in the project's history**. Case targets now go to a separate set, outrank a byte-pattern guess at the same address (case bodies routinely follow alignment padding), and are fed to `hybridDisassemble` as seeds via `seeds.ts` — without them, phase-2 gap fill starts *on the table*, decodes its entries as instructions and eats the head of case 0.
- **A recovered jump table's bytes are data, and only `DetectResult.jumpTableSpans` says so.** Seeding the case bodies is not enough: nothing walks *into* a table, so the gap fill still reaches it as an uncovered range and decodes the case addresses as instructions — 32 bytes at t32.exe's 0x4086a4 came out as six conditional jumps aiming past the end of the function they were filed under, and w32.exe produced three more, which were the corpus's last 9 lost CFG edges (peek-a-bin-y1di, the residue of peek-a-bin-g7yp). `detectFunctions` reports the extent it actually read, `hybridDisassemble` takes it as a last optional argument and marks it covered before computing gaps, and the client and `mcp/session.ts` carry it beside the seeds. Omitting the argument keeps the old behaviour, deliberately: "nobody said where the tables are" is not "there are none". A bound the dispatch compares against a *register* — MSVC's `push 7 / pop ecx / cmp eax, ecx`, two bytes cheaper than `cmp eax, 7` — is followed back to the constant, because a table that cannot be sized is also a table whose bytes nothing knows to be data (peek-a-bin-mk42; +3 tables on t32, +1 on w32, and three more `switch`es emitted).
- **…and a table with NO bounds check has bytes but no cases, so those are answered separately.** A bound is the only statement of a switch's *length*, and mk42's sentence — "a table that cannot be sized is also a table whose bytes nothing knows to be data" — left a residue: where nothing bounds the index there is no size, so nothing marked the bytes and the gap fill decoded them. `unboundedTableExtent` (`functionDetect.ts`) answers the second question alone and reports `TableRead.dataOnly` — an extent with **no targets**, because reporting entries whose case order is unknown is what peek-a-bin-div refused. So `jumpTableSpans` is no longer a subset of `jumpTables`' extents; a span with no table is normal and the reverse is not. Four things are load-bearing:
  - **The extent is the maximal run of pointer-width words on the base's own grid that each hold an address inside the code section, scanned in BOTH directions.** Backwards is not symmetry for its own sake: MSVC's reverse `memmove` does `neg ecx` and then `jmp dword ptr [ecx*4 + <base>]`, so the base names the table's **last** slot (t32 0x40b968, w32 0x409602) and a forward-only read finds one entry.
  - **The fallback is on "no cases recovered", not on "no bound found".** The descending shape *does* have a `cmp ecx, 8` in front of it, and reading eight entries forward from the last slot yields exactly one — which is not a switch, and which the caller has always discarded. Keying on the absence of a bound leaves that table decoded as code.
  - **It errs SHORT, deliberately.** Over-reporting marks real code as data, which deletes instructions with nothing said; under-reporting leaves them as they are today. The run must contain the named base — a base that is not an address at all is not answered here but **deferred** to `overlappedTableExtent`, see the next gotcha; it stops at one non-resolving word rather than tolerating a gap; `MIN_UNBOUNDED_TABLE_ENTRIES` is **4**, not the bounded path's 2, because with no `cmp` the run *is* the whole evidence; and it is confined to the code section, since a table in `.rdata` is not gap-filled and there is nothing there to fix. It is also restricted to the *indexed* operand form — `jmp dword ptr [0x40f0a8]` is an import thunk, not a one-entry switch.
  - **The corroboration comes from outside the rule**: at all four sites it fires on, the run length is **8**, exactly the bound the machine imposes (`cmp ecx, 8` / `jb`) — a bound `readAbsoluteTable` cannot see because the compare sits in a control-flow predecessor that is *later* in address order, so the linear `recent` window never holds it.

  **The harm was bigger than fiction in the view, and the two instruments that found y1di were both blind to it.** Measured at `d514274`: 2 tables per 32-bit binary, 64 bytes each, decoded as **25 phantom instructions on t32 and 33 on w32** — and the misaligned walk ran off each table's end and ate the head of the case body after it (3 real bytes on t32, 10 on w32), so real instructions were replaced by fiction *outside* the table too. In the emitted C that fiction was **feeding a real store**: `t32!sub_40B780` printed `*(int32_t*)(edi + (ecx << 2) - 0x1C) = eax` where `eax` came from nine invented instructions including `/* unlifted: out 0xb8, eax */`, and it now reads `= *(int32_t*)(esi + (ecx << 2) - 0x1C)`, the CRT's actual unrolled copy. The y1di instrument — conditional jumps aiming outside their filed function — reads **0 before and after**, because none of this fiction happened to be a `jcc`; and no gate moved either, since gcc compiles fiction, polarity judges guards that exist and the statement-drop audit counts drops rather than inventions. The instrument that does see it is the **spurious-run census**: maximal runs of ≥4 code-pointer words anywhere in the code section, split by whether they intersect anything the disassembler files as code — 6/5/0/0 runs (t32/w32/t64/w64) with **2/2/0/0 intersecting, and 0/0/0/0 after** (peek-a-bin-7lb9).
- **…and a base that is not an address at all can still be a table base: MSVC overlaps an unbounded table's entry 0 with the instruction in front of it.** `unboundedTableExtent` requires the run to *contain* the named base, and at four sites it does not, because the base's four bytes are opcode. t32.exe, and the other three are the same shape (t32 0x40b985 base 0x40b990, w32 0x409491 base 0x4094a4, w32 0x409625 base 0x409630):

  ```text
    40b7ec  and eax, 3                          ; index is 1..3, never 0
    40b7ef  add ecx, eax
    40b7f1  jmp dword ptr [eax*4 + 0x40b804]    ; base 0x40b804
    40b7f8  jmp dword ptr [ecx*4 + 0x40b900]    ; Shape 2 — negative index, see below
    40b7ff  nop
    40b800  jmp dword ptr [ecx*4 + 0x40b884]    ; 7 bytes: 40b800..40b806
    40b807  nop                                 ; so [0x40b804] = b8 40 00 90
    40b808  dd 0x40b814, 0x40b840, 0x40b864     ; entries 1..3, real
    40b814  and edx, ecx                        ; case body 1
  ```

  Entry 0 is provably unreachable — the block is entered only when `test edi, 3` was non-zero, so `and eax, 3` cannot produce 0 — so the assembler spent its slot on an instruction rather than four bytes of padding. **A byte that belongs to a decoded instruction cannot be a table entry**, which is real evidence rather than statistical, so `overlappedTableExtent` (`functionDetect.ts`) starts the table at the first grid slot past that instruction and reports `[base + ptrSize, hi)` as the span — the overlapped slot is code and marking it data would delete the `jmp` in front of the dispatch. Five things (peek-a-bin-xqxy):
  - **The evidence arrives by deferral, not a second decode, and there was a measured choice here.** The overlapping instruction is at a *higher* address than the dispatch (0x40b800 against 0x40b7f1), so the linear `recent` window structurally cannot hold it. `TableRead.deferredBase` hands the refusal back to the sweep, which settles it when it walks onto an instruction containing the base and **drops it the moment `insn.address >= base`** — the sweep is address-monotone, so that is proof no later instruction can contain it. Cost is a handful of live entries and no extra work: no instruction-extents array (2.4 MB on a 657 KiB `.text`) and no forward Capstone probe, which would re-decode exactly what the sweep is about to decode and could then disagree with it. `MAX_PENDING_OVERLAP_BASES` is hostile-input hygiene, and dropping a pending base is the safe direction — it is today's behaviour.
  - **The instruction must end INSIDE the base slot** (`base < end <= base + ptrSize`). That is the idiom — one instruction's tail shared with entry 0 — and it is the whole claim; anything deeper has swallowed a slot whole and a "base" that far inside code is not a table base.
  - **The run is counted from the BASE, not from the span**, so `MIN_UNBOUNDED_TABLE_ENTRIES` still applies to the table's own length: the overlapped slot is a table slot (the dispatch names it) and is exactly one slot by the rule above, so four entries means three readable words. Lowering the threshold instead would weaken every *unanchored* run, which is the population that 4 was calibrated against.
  - **SHAPE 2 IS NOT A DEFECT AND MUST NOT BE "FIXED", and it is now refused three ways over.** t32 0x40b7f8 and w32 0x409498 dispatch through a **negative** index (`sub ecx, 4 / jb` leaves ecx at 0xFFFFFFFC..0xFFFFFFFF), so the words read are *below* the base and are a table already recovered and already spanned; the "base" 0x40b900 / 0x4095a0 is a case body. The caller refuses any `deferredBase` a recovered table already dispatches to — an address a `jmp [table]` reaches is code, so it is not any table's slot — which is why `overlappedTableExtent` is called exactly 4 times per 32-bit binary and never sees these. Measured with that check disabled, they are *also* refused: t32 by run-too-short (entries=1) and w32 by the overlap-extent test (its base sits inside a 6-byte `add` running to 0x4095a5). The case-target refusal is what makes it a property rather than two counts happening to disagree.
  - **The `and <index>, 3` at all four sites is corroboration from OUTSIDE the rule and is deliberately not used *here*.** It bounds the index to `[0, 3]` — four slots, exactly what the overlap plus the run finds — but it says nothing about which bytes are opcode, so on its own it would report a span covering four bytes of a real `jmp`. **The other two reasons this bullet gave — "a mask is not a `cmp`" and "using it would widen the bounded path's vocabulary at every dispatch" — have since been answered and the mask IS admitted as a bound, on `boundedCaseCount`'s side of the question only** (`peek-a-bin-padl`, see the gotcha below). What is refused here is unchanged: the span question is not the index question, and `overlappedTableExtent` still reads no mask.

  **The harm and the measurement.** 12 bytes per site were gap-filled, and the misaligned walk then ate real code past the table: t32 0x40b813 `add byte ptr [ebx], ah` swallowed the `23` of `and edx, ecx` and the next five decodes were fiction over 21 bytes of the CRT's real 3-byte unrolled copy. In the emitted C, `t32!sub_40B780` and `w32!sub_409420` each carried a region ending `eax = sub_288402B(); return eax;` — **a call to a function outside the image, followed by a return** — where the machine copies three bytes and loops. Measured at `6d5ae92` + the new audit, both sides pinned: instructions **18032 → 18025** (t32) and **16611 → 16601** (w32), x64 **byte-identical**; emitted C moved in exactly one function per 32-bit binary, read line by line against `objdump`, with three invented prelude declarations dropped per binary; `functions` 288/279/275/285 and `jumpTables` 13/0/0/11 unmoved, gcc all clean, `offsetof` 331/301/303/418 fields and 54/54/53/58 definitions **ratio 1.00 and unmoved**, polarity 0 inverted with `CHANGED 0` / `only-base 0` / `only-change 0` on all four, unrecovered values 91/21/17/72 unmoved, and **loop shapes identical** (`for` 3/5/5/3, `while` 114/107/100/101, `do/while` 78/77/76/77). One `nop` per site leaves the view and that is the pre-existing all-padding gap rule working: each is a single 0x90 that was only ever decoded because the gap ran on into the table.

  **7lb9's spurious-run census cannot see this residue** — Shape 1's run is 3 words and does not contain its base — so the gate is `corpus/wildBranches.ts`, **a filed direct branch whose target the image does not contain**, 1/0/0/1 → 0/0/0/0. That is deliberately phrased without mentioning jump tables: this fix works by marking table bytes as data, and an audit in those terms would measure its own input. It is a **lower bound**, and a loose one — it saw 1 of the 2 sites per binary, the other site's fiction happening not to decode as a branch out of the image.
- **…and what bounds a table is one rule about one register, which it took two disagreeing declarations to notice.** `readAbsoluteTable` walked back to the **first `cmp` it met**, read its immediate as the case count and stopped, never asking which register was compared; `boundedCaseCount`, forty lines away in the same file, tracked the index register but could not read a bound carried in a register. So the two paths answered the same question differently, and **each was wrong where the other was right** — the shape `sections.ts`, `ripRelative.ts`, `funcInsns.ts` and `stackIdiom.ts` each exist to end. There is one declaration now, `boundedCaseCount`, and it is at least as strong as either copy was. Measured at `51264fe`, both sides pinned (`peek-a-bin-padl`):
  - **The census, which corrects the bead in both directions.** Of 20/18 absolute-table dispatches on t32/w32, **9 per binary took a bound from a register the `jmp` does not index** — the bead's list, re-derived — every one an MSVC SSE memcpy/memset tail whose index is bounded by `and edx, 3` while `cmp ecx, 8` bounds the *dword count*. But **4 more per binary were bounded correctly by `readAbsoluteTable` in a way `boundedCaseCount` cannot reach** (t32 0x4046cd, 0x407c36, 0x40cd50; w32 0x40b0dc — MSVC's `push 7 / pop ecx / cmp eax, ecx`, `peek-a-bin-mk42`'s route), so *sharing `boundedCaseCount` as it stood* would have deleted four 8-case switches. Three pre-existing tests fail under a register check alone, which is how that half shows up.
  - **THE WRONG BOUND COST NOTHING OBSERVABLE, and the bead is right that this is luck.** At all 16 recovering sites the bound claimed 9 (or 5) cases and the read stopped at **4**, because the words after the table are the case bodies and `t32!0x40b900` holds `0x5E08458B`. Verified site by site: identical target lists, and the span is computed from `targets.length` so it was 16 bytes and correct too. What the bound was actually supplying is a **cap**, and the range check on each entry was doing the sizing. So this is not a measured repair of wrong output; it is the removal of an unsound reading, and the number that moves is the **exposure**: words the bound would have let the read accept past the end of the real case list, i.e. draws taken on the ~1-in-65536 chance that a case body's first word looks like a code address. **54 over 11 dispatches → 8 over 2, on each 32-bit binary.** The residual 2 are `peek-a-bin-xqxy`'s overlapped bases (t32 0x40b7f1, 0x40b985), where entry 0 is provably opcode so the read stops at `c = 0` and the slack is nominal.
  - **`and <index>, imm` is admitted as a bound of `imm + 1`, and that answers the refusal recorded above rather than ignoring it.** A mask states the index's range *exactly*, where a `cmp` states it only together with the sense of the branch below it — which the code does not read, hence `imm + 1` over-claiming by one after a `jb`. So the mask is the **stronger** of the two forms, not a weaker substitute. `overlappedTableExtent`'s refusal stands untouched: there the question is which *bytes* are opcode, which a mask cannot answer, and the scope here is narrower than either that bead or `peek-a-bin-ayhj` considered — the mask counts only for the register the dispatch actually subscripts with, and only if the register-tracking walk has not already been broken.
  - **A write of the index between the bound and the dispatch ends the search**, which `boundedCaseCount` did not do (it walked straight past `add ecx, eax`, `sub ecx, 4` and a `rep movsd` to reach a `cmp ecx, 8` five blocks back). A bound on a value the register no longer holds is not a bound — `conditionSpoiled`'s rule for a guard. `cmp`, `test` and `push` are the read-only forms; the x86 string primitives are refused explicitly because they write RCX/RSI/RDI while naming none of them, which `destReg` cannot see. Without this the change would additionally have recovered t32 0x40b800's real 8-entry table **by unsound reasoning** — right answer, walk across three writes — and that is declined.
  - **What keeps the output is that a table already read is a table: the base is the unit of identity.** The register check alone, mask included, is **refuted by measurement and reproduced here — `jumpTables` 13 → 6 (t32) and 11 → 4 (w32)** — because these tails are reached by `jmp`, so the eight addresses in front of one are not the eight instructions that ran, and the `and edx, 3` is out of linear reach at all but the first. No lookback length fixes that. But a base another dispatch has already resolved holds the same entries however it is reached, so `readAbsoluteTable` consults the sweep's `tablesByBase` before giving up — reusing the *entries*, **never a longer run than the first reading took**, which would be a length claim the second dispatch has no evidence for. First reading wins, and it is the one whose own evidence bounded the table.
