  - **That reuse is also the only route by which two dispatches per 32-bit binary are read at all, and their absence was a FALSE control-flow claim.** t32 `0x40b8e7` and `0x40ba83` (w32 `0x409587`, `0x409723`) are the tail dispatch after the unrolled dword copy; with no bound in reach they got no successors, so the emitted C ran off the end of the unrolled block straight into `loc_40B925: ecx = ecx; goto loc_40B928;` — **always the 3-byte tail**, where the machine dispatches on `edx` into one of four. `armExits`' character: a statement the output makes that is not true. `jumpTables` **13/0/0/11 → 15/0/0/13**, every pre-existing target list byte-identical, x64 untouched (0 tables, and `readRvaTable` is the x64 path).
  - **Every moved row accounted for; t64 and w64 byte-identical throughout.** `compare.mjs` verdict no regression, `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0` on all four, `guards audited` exactly flat at 517/574/441/497. Emitted C moved in **1 of 268 t32 functions and 5 of 266 w32**: the two memcpy/memset routines gain the four `switch (edx)` arms, and the other four w32 functions are `struct_N` renumbering (three are byte-identical after normalising the numbers) plus `w32!sub_40A420`'s 4-field partial view **completing into the 11-field shape** — the documented cross-function refinement, order-dependent through `fingerprintIndex`. `offsetof` t32 346 → **342** fields at 55/55 definitions and w32 354 → **355** at 59 → **56**: on t32 `struct_41`'s four accesses re-base onto `struct_13` (the case bodies are now reached from a merged `edi`/`esi`, so `splitStaleReads` repairs them, which is *more* correct — the base's `edi_2` was already wrong on the paths through the unrolled block), and on w32 three definitions merge for one net field gained. **Every ratio 1.00.** Also moved and all consequences of four more switch arms: `arms examined` and `truthful closures` 72 → 80 and 54 → 62 with **0 false breaks**, `case labels` 72 → 80 and 54 → 62 with **0 empty case bodies** and `lone goto` 29 → 37, `statements lifted` +12, `emitted lines scanned` +24. Flat: `functions`, `instructions`, gcc, polarity (0 inverted, 0 mismatch), `wildBranches` 0 over the same 4240/3791 denominators, loop exits, callees lost, statements dropped, unrecovered values, arity, staleReads, staleGuards, popReads, lostDefs, unencodableNames, and every loop-shape count.
  - **NOT verified.** The unified rule is also what `recoverX64RvaChain` and `recoverDenseByteTable` call, and **this corpus recovers 0 tables on t64 and w64**, so the three arms those two gained (the constant-register route, the mask, the write break) and the vocabulary they lost are **unmeasured on real x64 output** — synthetic fixtures only. `peek-a-bin-ayhj`'s Go image has **20** dense dispatches and recovers 0 of them — and it does not exercise `recoverDenseByteTable` either, which takes **0 calls** there as on all four corpus binaries (re-measured at `baa7f61`); it is deliberately not in the corpus directory and is driven separately by `npm run corpus:jumptables`. Nothing here rendered anything, and the exposure figure is a property of these four binaries, not a bound.
- **There is exactly one notion of "loop": dominance.** `cfg.ts`'s `detectLoops` delegates to `decompile/ssa.ts`'s `detectNaturalLoops` (an edge `u → v` is a back edge only when v dominates u) and repackages the result as `Loop[]`. It used to approximate back edges with BFS layers from the entry, which called the merge block of every `if`-without-`else` a loop header — ~86% of the loops reported on real binaries did not exist, and the mis-structuring **deleted guards**, turning conditional stores into unconditional ones. A diamond is immune to that mistake and a triangle is not, so hand-written fixtures never caught it; `__tests__/cfg.test.ts` and `decompile/__tests__/pipeline.test.ts` now pin both shapes. `detectLoops` is also what draws loop markers in the disassembly view (`useDisassemblyRows.ts`), so its semantics are shared with the UI.
- **`structureCFG` closes an `if` at the immediate post-dominator**, computed by `computePostDominators` (`structure.ts`) — `computeDominators` over the reversed CFG rooted at a virtual exit. The two "one arm ends in `ret`" shortcuts must not fire when that arm *is* the convergence point: in a triangle the branch target is the shared tail, so structuring it as the `then` body yields an empty body and drops the guard. A nearest-common-successor heuristic is not a substitute — for a switch it picks the default block, which is not on every path, and the code after the switch is then lost.
- **`extractCondition` returns the condition under which the jump is TAKEN.** Every entry in `regstate.ts`'s `condMap` is taken-polarity (`jg`→`>`, `jbe`→`u<=`, `js`→`< 0`), and `identifyBranches` returns the block the jcc jumps to. So the branch target is the `then` body under the *un-negated* condition, and `RegState.negate` is for the fallthrough — its own docstring says "for structuring: if-not-taken path". Getting this backwards inverted **every `if` and `while` the decompiler ever emitted** while leaving the bodies in place: valid C stating the opposite of the machine code, invisible to every stage-level test. `__tests__/pipeline.test.ts` guards it end to end now.
- **A guard is answered from the flags the Jcc *actually* reads, and only while the compare still describes them.** Two refusals in `extractCondition`, one defect class — the emitted guard naming the **right operator over the wrong operands**, which no gate could see because polarity checks the operator, gcc compiles it, and it is not `__unrecovered_N`. (a) The forward walk clears the flags on any instruction that is not `isFlagTransparent`, because it used to clear them on *nothing* — not a call, not arithmetic, not a shift — so `cmp eax, 5 / … / sub ecx, edx / jne` emitted `eax != 5` where the machine branches on `ecx - edx != 0` (`peek-a-bin-jitf`; 2 corpus occurrences, both MSVC's branchless `cmp / sbb eax,eax / inc eax / jne` size check, where the **`inc`** sets the ZF the `jne` reads). (b) A compare whose operand something overwrote before the Jcc is refused, because the block's statements are emitted **above** the `if`: `cmp eax, 5 / mov eax, edx / je` printed `eax = edx;` and then `if (eax != 5)`, reading the new EAX (`peek-a-bin-xe01`). **Both use `flagModel.ts`, not a copy of it** — `isFlagTransparent` is that module's own table exported, and the overwrite scan is `clobberedAfter`, asked by `extractCondition` of the `cmp`'s *operands*. It had a second caller, `flagResultSetter`'s condition 6, asking the identical question of an arithmetic *result* register; keeping them in one predicate is why they could not disagree, and they had disagreed since `peek-a-bin-b531` with condition 6's docstring stating the `cmp` path's bug verbatim. That caller is gone with the backward walk — a result-derived guard is an `IRBranch`, so SSA answers it by construction and `flagModel`'s `spoils` answers it forwards for the lifter. **Refusal is the whole repair**: the guard becomes `__unrecovered_N`, which is "clean is not recovered" working as intended, and it is why the cost shows up as unrecovered values rising (108/85/67/85 → 159/94/73/122 *at the time*; `peek-a-bin-92yy` has since taken the total down again, so do not read either pair as current) rather than as guard text changing — `compare.mjs` reports `CHANGED 0` and `only-change 0` on all four binaries. Gated by `corpus/staleGuards.ts`; do not weaken either refusal without reading it.
- **`push` and `pop` write no flags, they have been in `NO_FLAG_WRITE` since `b35a786`, and a guard behind an epilogue restore is refused for a DIFFERENT and CORRECT reason.** `peek-a-bin-thsj` was filed as "add `push` and `pop` to `NO_FLAG_WRITE`"; they were already there, and the census says the flag model displaces a compare owner on a `push`/`pop` **0 times on all four binaries** (the clearers are `sbb`, `bt` and `movnti`). Adjudicated and pinned at `41113c9` rather than fixed. What is actually going on is that **membership in `NO_FLAG_WRITE` and `spoils` are two questions about the same instruction, and a `pop` answers them oppositely**: it writes no flag, so the owner stands, *and* it writes its operand register, so the name the guard would be spelled with is gone. `test edi, edi / pop edi / pop esi / pop ebx / jne` (t32 `sub_40E1D8` 0x40e275, verified against `objdump -d -M intel`) keeps the `test` as owner and is then refused by `spoils`, because EDI at the Jcc holds the **restored callee-saved value** and `edi != 0` would be a test the machine does not make. Both scans already get this right — `spoils` forwards for the lifter, `clobberedAfter` for `structure.ts`'s `conditionSpoiled` — so the bead's hazard 2 was not a hazard, it was the mechanism. Five things worth carrying:
  - **The bead's symptom sentence is refuted, and by the majority of the population.** "Every Jcc whose flag owner is separated from it by a pop emits `__unrecovered_N`" is false: of the compare owners with a `push` or `pop` between the compare and the Jcc — **34/0/0/27** (t32/t64/w64/w32) — **26/0/0/20 are recovered today**, and they are recovered *because* `push`/`pop` are members. The x64 pair has 0 of the shape at all, so anything measured there is measured on nothing.
  - **The residue is 2/0/0/2 guards, and in all four the popped register IS a compare operand.** Of 54/9/6/38 spoiled compare owners the ones every one of whose spoilers is a `pop` are t32 `sub_40B780` 0x40b7bd (`cmp edi, esi / pop esi / pop edi / jne`) and `sub_40E1D8` 0x40e27a, and w32 `sub_409420` 0x40945d and `sub_40C7F8` 0x40c89a. So **0 of them are recoverable by any change to the flag tables** — the split the bead asked for has everything on the unrecoverable side.
  - **Recovering them needs the tested value materialised into a temporary before the clobber**, which `conditionSpoiled`'s docstring already named, and that mechanism is **general rather than `pop`-shaped: it was worth 104 guards corpus-wide, of which these 4 are 4%.** The spoiler histogram over all 107 spoiled compares is `mov=157 pop=15 lea=7 movsx=6 movzx=6 push=6` (re-derived at `97249dc`; an earlier count of `mov=133 movzx=6 pop=6 lea=2` was taken over a narrower stretch). Doing it `pop`-only would have spent the whole design cost on the smallest slice and left a second copy of a rule the general fix must own. **`peek-a-bin-xskz` LANDED that fix** — see the `spoiledCompareCapture` gotcha above — and all four of these sites are now recovered, `t32!sub_40E1D8` 0x40e27a as `flg_40e275_0 = edi;` above the three pops.
  - **`corpus/staleGuards.ts`'s gate FORBIDS the bead's own DONE criterion, and that is checkable.** All four target jccs are `clobbered` rows in `staleguards_<bin>.jsonl` with `emitted: null`; `named` counts *any* emitted guard at such a jcc, so emitting one takes the gate 0 → 4 red **even though the guard would be correct**. "The guard at 0x40e27a is emitted" and "`staleGuards` stays at 0 named" cannot both hold. The general fix therefore has to refine that audit — a decision to be taken *with* the audit, exactly as `branchFor`'s refusal 3 says of its own shape.
  - **DO NOT try to fix it by moving the `IRBranch` earlier in the block's statement list.** It is tempting, because `pipeline.ts` step 4b extracts branches into a `Map` before `structureCFG` so their position never reaches the page, and an earlier position *does* make `buildSSA` bind the condition to the pre-`pop` version. It still emits the wrong C: `structureCFG` emits a block's statements **above** the `if` it closes with, so the restore runs before the guard regardless of where the branch statement sat. The value has to exist as a statement, not merely be read early.
  - **Hazard 1 was already correct, and it is live rather than hypothetical.** `popf`/`popfd`/`popfq` load every flag and `pushf`/`pushfd`/`pushfq`, `pusha`/`popa` are different instructions again; none is a member, so all clobber — `baseMnemonic`'s exact match is what makes that true and a `startsWith("pop")` test would silently admit `popf`. **`pushfd` occurs twice in each 32-bit corpus binary** (t32 0x402b8b/0x403c4b, w32 0x402ddf/0x403eab — the CRT capturing an exception context). One latent asymmetry is documented and costs nothing today: `clobberedAfter` reports `push`/`pop` as writes of RSP and `spoils` does not, so the two disagree about a `cmp [esp + 4], 0 / pop edi / jne` — 4/39/34/4 compare owners name ESP/RSP at all and the scans disagree about **0** of them, with `conditionSpoiled` as the backstop for the block-local case either way.
- **…and refusing a spoiled compare was never the only sound answer: MATERIALISE the compared values at the compare, and the guard can be recovered.** `conditionSpoiled` refuses because the block's statements are emitted *above* the `if`, so `cmp eax, 5 / mov eax, edx / je` would print `eax = edx; if (eax != 5)` — the right operator over the wrong operands. Its own docstring named the better answer, and `spoiledCompareCapture` (`lifter.ts`) is it: one statement per non-constant operand at the compare's own program point, `flg_<compare address>_<operand index> = <parsed operand>`, and the `IRBranch`'s condition built over those. The clobber then runs between the capture and the guard and cannot reach either. **104 guards recovered corpus-wide** and unrecovered values **91/21/17/72 → 39/12/11/35** (`peek-a-bin-xskz`, measured at `97249dc`). Six things, and the first three are judgements that cost something:
  - **The destination is an `IRVar`, and a deletable pseudo-*register* was tried first, measured, and REJECTED.** `flg_x = eax` is a copy by `isCopyStmt`'s reckoning, so `copyPropagation` deletes it, rebinds the guard to the pre-clobber SSA version, and `splitStaleReads` repairs that as it repairs every other stale read — free where it recovers nothing, and a natural register name on the page where it does (`matchedStackSlots`' `stk_<addr>` argument, `peek-a-bin-6f3v`). It works. It was refused for two reasons that are one reason: **it leaves the class ungateable**, because after copy propagation a recovered `eax == 5` and a defective `edx == 5` are both bare register names and no text rule separates them; and **its correctness becomes a chain of passes** rather than a property — in the shape where DCE deletes the clobber outright the guard is a bare `eax` that is right *for that reason alone*. An `IRVar` is written once, at the compare, by a statement `copyPropagation`, `foldBlock`'s single-use inlining and `deadCodeElimination` all leave alone by construction (each requires a register destination). The price is 114 statements and 114 temporaries over the four binaries, including the 27 sites where a register would have folded away. That is what the gate costs, and it is worth it.
  - **The signal to `structure.ts` is the compare's ADDRESS, not a boolean, and the two walks must agree.** `IRBranch.capturedAt` carries it; `extractCondition` bypasses `conditionSpoiled` only when `capturedAt === flagSetterAddr`, i.e. when the instruction *its own* forward walk found to have set the flags is the one the lifter captured at. A disagreement leaves the refusal standing. And the bypass additionally requires a usable IR condition to fall through to, or it would hand back `cond` — the raw reading the refusal exists to suppress. Asking `conditionSpoiled` of the IR condition instead is what `peek-a-bin-xe01` records as defeating it outright, so the signal has to come from the lifter.
  - **`foldBlock`'s refusal to inline into a branch stops applying, and that is fine but not free.** A definition whose only reader was the guard was refused; its only reader is now a *capture*, an ordinary assign, so it gets inlined — `and edi, 0xf / and esi, 0xf / cmp edi, esi` becomes `flg_40b7b9_0 = edi & 0xF; flg_40b7b9_1 = esi & 0xF`, one statement fewer than the capture count on t32. The two reasons that refusal exists are both unaffected: `blockLiveOut` still stops a value that escapes the block, and `structureCFG` matches loop shapes on a block's *final* statement, which a capture never is. Empirically confirmed — **loop shapes identical on all four binaries**.
  - **The scope is a BLOCK-LOCAL compare owner — and `peek-a-bin-zylv` has since taken the cross-block population without extending it.** `blockFlagOwner` will answer from a sole predecessor, and a capture *placed* for that owner would have to go in the predecessor's statement list, which `liftBlock` does not have. It does not have to be placed: measured at `97249dc` and again at `16f1633`, all 2/0/0/1 cross-block spoiled owners (against 52/9/6/37 block-local, 3 of 107) are spoiled on the **predecessor's** side of the edge, and in every one the predecessor's own trailing jcc reads the *same* compare — so the predecessor is itself a block-local site and the capture is already on the page. `reusablePredecessorCapture` looks it up; see the gotcha. A `result` or `bittest` owner is still untouched — its value is the instruction's own destination, and capturing it would mean reading a destination the instruction has not written yet.
  - **The dominant shape is a compare over MEMORY refused for want of alias analysis** — 74 `mem/imm` plus 3 `mem/reg` of 104 at `97249dc`, against 25 `reg/reg` and 2 `reg/imm`. `test byte ptr [ebp - 0x408], 0x20 / mov dword ptr [ebp - 0x10], edi / je` is refused because `clobberedAfter` reports a memory write and the condition holds a deref, with no attempt to prove the two slots differ. Materialising the load needs no alias reasoning at all, which is why this is the right recovery rather than a weakening of `spoils`.
  - **Five sites were read by hand against `objdump`, and two of them settle a constant.** t64 `0x140005752` is `mov ecx,0x30 / cmp eax,ecx / mov ecx,0x58 / jne`: the fix emits `flg_140005752_1 = 0x30` and the negative control emits `eax_6 == 0x58`, the value the *spoiler* put there — a provably wrong test in compiling C, and the clearest evidence the capture is taken at the right point. t32 `0x40e27a` is `peek-a-bin-thsj`'s own site (`test edi, edi / pop edi / pop esi / pop ebx / jne`), recovered here as `flg_40e275_0 = edi;` **above** the pops; thsj's adjudication said this needed the general fix, and it did.
  - **…and the emitter must DECLARE the capture, which it did not until `peek-a-bin-x54q`.** A capture is an `IRVar` the emitter itself invents and writes, so the emitted C named an identifier nothing in it declares — **114 of them at `f169c00`** (53/14/9/38 on t32/t64/w64/w32), each in a function where a promoted frame slot beside it *was* declared. **This is not the documented decision to leave a *register* undeclared** (`structPointer`: nothing the reader supplies for a register may make C scale arithmetic on it, and an entry value really is conceptually a parameter): a capture is neither a register nor an incoming value, and the other locals the pipeline invents (`var_N` from `promoteVars`) are declared. Six things, and the first is the reason nobody had noticed:
    - **No gate could see it, and one of them was actively hiding it.** `corpus/emitAudits.ts`' `preludeFor` answers gcc's `'flg_…' undeclared` by manufacturing `long flg_…;`, so **gcc read 1127/1127 clean over C the harness had completed rather than over the C the emitter wrote** — the same structural blindness the file already records for a wrong register *name*. So the instrument is the **count of invented prelude declarations** (the `long <name>;` lines in `artifacts/<label>/cc/<bin>/*.c`), the one `peek-a-bin-k8i` used: **1579/2520/2337/1520 → 1526/2506/2328/1482**, down by exactly the 53/14/9/38 captures, with **0 `flg_` prelude declarations left** — which is also the proof that no capture is read without its definition being emitted.
    - **The `long` was the wrong WIDTH for every one of the 114, not merely absent.** 64 are 4 bytes, 42 are 1 and 8 are 2, and **not one is 8**, so the program gcc compiled was not the one the emitter wrote. Worse where the guard is unsigned: `cOp` spells `u<` as a bare `<`, so nothing in the expression says which comparison the machine makes and the declaration is the *only* thing that decides it — under `long` two `jb` guards per run compiled as signed 64-bit comparisons.
    - **The type is `IRVar.size` via `sizeToType`, preferring type inference only where it names the SAME width.** `IRVar.size` is what `capturedWidth` took from the operand `parseOperand` read out of the machine text, and it is already what `operandWidth` reads for that same variable when it decides a cast — so a declaration derived from anything else can disagree with the casts the emitter has already emitted around it. Inference is preferred where it speaks, exactly as `promoteVars` prefers it for a local, and that is what makes the 2 unsigned guards compile unsigned. The width test is a **bound, not a saving**: `typeInfer.ts` types a comparison operand `{ int, size: 4 }` with the width hard-coded, so for a 1- or 2-byte capture its spelling would claim a width the operand does not have; and it refuses every non-integer spelling, which is what keeps a declaration from turning `emitsAsPointer` true and rescaling arithmetic the body already emits. Measured: of the 114, inference has an opinion about **10** and all 10 agree in width, so it refuses nothing today.
    - **`isCapturedOperandName` is the scope, and "every undeclared `IRVar` destination" is NOT this set.** `ssadestroy.ts`'s `splitStaleReads` parks a pre-clobber value in an `IRVar` too — **2114 undeclared (function, name) pairs against these 114** — and those are spelled as registers deliberately, are compared as names by `corpus/staleReads.ts`, and are covered by the register decision. Declaring them is a much larger change with an emitted-C effect of its own (a pointer-typed one flips `emitsAsPointer`). So the predicate lives beside `capturedOperandName` in `lifter.ts`, one declaration read in both directions; `corpus/staleGuards.ts` still rebuilds the spelling itself, because an audit that imported it would be measuring its own input.
    - **Destinations only, deduped BY NAME — both bounds rather than savings.** A capture is built per *block* while a declaration is per *function*, and `structureCFG` can emit one block more than once (the switch-arm and leftover passes both emit), so two declarations of one name would not be valid C; measured, 0 of the 114 is emitted twice. And a capture *read* where its definition was not emitted is a lost definition, so declaring it would state that the function computes a value it never assigns — it stays undeclared and keeps showing up in the prelude, which is the honest signal; measured, 0 of 114. Both are pinned by tests in `emit.test.ts` for exactly that reason, and all five rules are negative-controlled.
    - **`collectCapturedOperands` is a NINTH compiler-caught `IRStmt` dispatch, `emit.ts`'s fourth**, measured with the throwaway-kind probe this file describes; the stamped table above says 8 and was taken at `f685b6d`, so it needs re-measuring rather than trusting. Measured at `f169c00`: emitted text **+57/+14/+9/+42** lines (114 declarations plus 8 new separator blank lines), 21/6/5/19 functions changed and **every diff exactly the added declarations**, `compare.mjs` verdict "no regression" with `CHANGED 0` / `only-base 0` / `only-change 0`, gcc 1127/1127, `offsetof` ratio 1.00 unmoved, polarity 0 inverted and 0 mismatch with the audited set unmoved, unrecovered values unmoved at 39/12/11/35, arity/staleReads/staleGuards/popReads/lostDefs/armExits/wildBranches/unencodableNames all flat, and loop shapes identical.

- **…and the cross-block half of that needed no new placement at all: the capture was already on the page.** A spoiled compare owned by a *predecessor* stayed refused because `liftBlock` returns one block's statements, and the standing plan was a `pipeline.ts` pass of its own appending into the predecessor through `pushBeforeTerminator`, `peek-a-bin-6ilz`-style. **The population refutes the premise.** Measured at `16f1633` (identical to `97249dc`): all **2/0/0/1** cross-block spoiled compare owners — against 52/9/6/37 block-local, which is exactly `staleGuards`' independently-counted `clobbered` shape count — are spoiled on the **predecessor's** side of the edge, have **exactly one** predecessor, and in every case the predecessor's *own* trailing jcc reads the same compare. So the predecessor is itself a block-local spoiled site, `spoiledCompareCapture` already ran there, and the emitted C read `flg_404957_0 = var_40C; … if (flg_404957_0 >= 0) { if (__unrecovered_3 /* jne */) {` — the admitted guard **one line below the value it needed**. `reusablePredecessorCapture` (`lifter.ts`) is a *lookup*, not a placement: no new pass, no `pushBeforeTerminator`, no statement emitted, and none of `6ilz`'s four refusals transfers, because nothing is defined early. Five things (`peek-a-bin-zylv`):
  - **Re-asking `spoiledCompareCapture` of the predecessor with no `flagPred` is provably its own answer, not an approximation.** A non-null answer means `flagOwnerBefore` over the predecessor's own instructions found a compare — and `flagPredecessor`'s first test is that a block's own scan reports `{none, no-owner}`, so it returns undefined for exactly that block. The predecessor's real lift therefore saw the same stream and emitted the same statements. A null answer agrees the same way: a predecessor with no local owner takes the `fromPredecessor` path and builds nothing.
  - **The names are the whole channel and no statement is emitted.** `IRVar` is a leaf for `renameVariables`, so a read binds across the edge with no versioning and no phi; a *second* assignment in the reading block would sit **below** the clobber and be the defect this recovers from. That the definition is *emitted* above the read is dominance plus `structureCFG`'s walk rather than something the lifter can assert — the instrument is `peek-a-bin-x54q`'s count of invented `long flg_…;` prelude declarations, **0 before and 0 after**, with the totals unmoved at 1526/2506/2328/1482.
  - **THE CORPUS CANNOT SEPARATE THIS FROM A WRONG FIX, AND THE TESTS CAN.** All three real sites are spoiled by stores to *other* frame slots — `spoils` refuses on any store, with no alias analysis — so simply bypassing the refusal and reading the raw operands recovers **the same 3 guards** with the same unrecovered counts, and every gate stays flat; measured, its only diff from the fix is `var_40C != 0` for `flg_404957_0 != 0`, which is also correct text here. The discriminator is `pipeline.test.ts`, whose fixture stores over the compared slot itself: the raw variant fails **three** tests, emitting `var_18 == 0` below `var_18 = edx;`, `edx == 0x53` for `cmp eax, 0x53 / mov eax, edx / je` (the register the spoiler *read*, `peek-a-bin-xe01`'s signature) and a guard where no capture exists. The test that pinned the old refusal states the new rule instead, as method requires.
  - **Two refusals, and one of them is provably unreachable today.** The capture must be at the compare this jump reads (both walks are forward and last-writer-wins over the same stream, so asking makes it a property). And the reading block must have **exactly one** predecessor: since `peek-a-bin-xdxt` a unanimous *several* may answer for the flags, and each holds its own compare at its own address, so a capture would exist on one edge only. That arm cannot currently fire — `unanimousCompare` refuses a spoiled compare outright (its refusal 3), so a multi-predecessor block never reaches `branchFor` with a spoiled owner — and it is kept as a bound on the rule rather than a saving, exactly as `pop esp` and the `rip`/`rsp` refusals are. The class it names, a lone jcc whose several unanimous predecessors are all *spoiled*, has 0 occurrences: `peek-a-bin-xdxt`'s census found a spellable, i.e. unspoiled, compare on both edges of all 14 such blocks.
  - **What stays refused is the honest half.** A cross-block owner spoiled on the *reading* side leaves the predecessor's compare intact, so the predecessor had no reason to take a capture and there is nothing to read; likewise a predecessor ending in `jmp` or `ret`, which reads no flags and answers no guard of its own. Both are pinned by tests and both have **0** corpus occurrences — that is the case the new placement would be for, and it has no population to justify it. Measured at base `16f1633`, both sides pinned: unrecovered values **38/12/11/34 → 36/12/11/33**, token occurrences 76/24/22/68 → 72/24/22/66, 2 of 288 and 1 of 285 functions changed, **t64/w64 byte-identical**, `compare.mjs` `CHANGED 0` / `only-base 0` / **`only-change 0`** on all four with the verdict "no regression". Every gate flat — gcc 1127/1127, `offsetof` ratio 1.00 with fields and definitions unmoved, polarity 0 inverted and 0 mismatch with the audited set **unmoved** at 517/574/497/441 and `guards w/o single compare` unmoved, 0 throws, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, 0 loops short, arity exact/under/over unmoved, staleReads 0/0, staleGuards **0 named** with `shapes` unmoved at 53/9/6/38, popReads 0, lostDefs 0, armExits 0, wildBranches 0, unencodableNames 0 — and **loop shapes identical**: `for` 6/6/6/6, `while` 111/106/99/98, `do/while` 78/77/76/77, `if` 1987/1938/1719/1759. The three recovered guards do **not** enter the polarity audit, and that is expected rather than a shortfall: each guards a block whose first statement is itself a nested `if`, which carries no line-map entry, so there is no `bodyAddr` to anchor. The oracle is hand-reading — all three read against `objdump -d -M intel`, all three MSVC's `_output` `cmp [ebp-X], 0 / <stores> / jge L` with `L: jne M`, and in each the `!= 0` body is the taken target's `cmp [ebp-X], eax / jle` clamp.

- **…and the same materialisation is placed for EVERY in-block flag reader, because `setcc` and `cmovcc` had the Jcc's defect one reader over.** `spoiledCompareCapture` asked its question of ONE reader — the block's trailing Jcc — while `setcc`/`cmovcc` built their conditions from `regState.getCondition` at their own program point (`lifter.ts`), and `RegState` has no spoil tracking: `cmp eax, 0x53 / mov eax, edx / sete al` lifted `al = (eax == 0x53)` after `eax = edx`, SSA bound the read to the `mov`, and the page said `return edx == 0x53` — the register the *spoiler* read, `peek-a-bin-xe01`'s signature, in C that compiles, at a site no gate examined because `staleGuards` anchors on jcc addresses. `operandCaptures` replaces it (`peek-a-bin-n9cl.6`): one map per block, keyed by setter address, built by asking every reader for its owner — the trailing Jcc through `blockFlagOwner` exactly as before, each `setcc`/`cmovcc` through `flagOwnerBefore` at its own index — and merging the needs of readers that share a setter. The `cmp`/`test` handler then records the CAPTURES into `regState`'s flag state where a capture exists, so `getCondition` spells the value the compare read, and `branchFor` looks its owner up in the same map. Five things:
  - **The shape is real and small: 3/5/5/3 spoiled `setcc`/`cmovcc` readers (t32/t64/w64/w32) over 51/108/97/48, measured at `6299113`.** Two per binary are `setge` after `cmp [ebp-0x1c], ebx` spoiled by a store (MSVC's `_output` width loop); the x64 pair add `cmp eax, ebx / mov ebx, [rbp-0x20] / setne dil` and `test al, 0x80 / lea eax, [rcx-0x7f] / cmove ecx, eax`; all four have `cmp [reg+0x14], reg / lea|mov reg, … / sete`. Recovered on the page: 1/2/2/1 (`cl = flg_40c3dd_0 == flg_40c3dd_1;`, `ecx = (flg_14000b243_0 & 0x80) == 0 ? 1 : 0x80;`); the rest fold into a later statement and have no line at the reader's address, which is the same lower-bound the Jcc rows carry.
  - **Measured at base `6299113`, both sides pinned:** `compare.mjs` verdict "no regression", `CHANGED 0` / `only-base 0` / `only-change 0` on all four for guards, +6 lifted statements per binary (the captures), polarity audited 577 → 578 and 500 → 501 on the x64 pair, and one report-only `CHANGED` row per x64 binary whose text is **byte-identical** (`eax != 0`) with the anchor moving A2 → A. gcc clean, `offsetof` ratio 1.00, staleGuards `named` 0 with `shapes` unmoved at 53/9/6/38, every other gate flat.
  - **The audit gained the reader population rather than a second audit** — `readers`/`readerShapes`/`readerNamed`/`readerEmitted` in `corpus/staleGuards.ts`, the same `spoiledReading` scan asked of the instructions strictly before each reader, judged from the emitted LINE the line map places at the reader's own address (`sweep.ts` builds `emittedLineAt`; the Jcc rows keep the polarity pass's anchored condition). A reader the emitter admitted — `__unrecovered_N`, or the instruction left `unlifted` — is a refusal and is not judged, or a `superseded` `setcc` the lifter correctly answers `unknown` would count as a wrong-operand row. `readerNamed` is gated at 0 with liveness on `readerShapes > 0` and `readerEmitted > 0`.
  - **The negative control is the setcc arm of `operandCaptures` disabled, and it discriminates:** `readerNamed` goes 0 → **1/2/2/1**, the recovered count exactly, `npm run corpus` exits 1, and the lines it names are provably wrong tests in compiling C — `cl = *(int32_t*)(ebp - 0x1AE0 + 0x14) == 0;` at t32 `sub_40C2E1` 0x40c3e6, where the machine compared `[eax + 0x14]` against ECX and the `lea eax, [ebp - 0x1ae0]` between them has been copy-propagated into the load (peek-a-bin-xe01's finding one reader over), and `ecx = (al & 0x80) == 0 ? eax : 0x80;` at t64 `sub_14000B094`, where AL was rewritten by the `lea eax, [rcx - 0x7f]`. `pipeline.test.ts` prints `edx == 0x53`.
  - **Two readers of one compare merging is currently unreachable**, and that is the flag model's narrowness working as documented: `setcc`/`cmovcc` are not in `NO_FLAG_WRITE`, so a `setcc` clears the owner for anything after it and a `cmp / sete / je` answers the `je` `__unrecovered_N`. The merge rule exists for `peek-a-bin-n9cl.6`'s CF readers, which read the compare through a different owner walk.
- **CF is a VALUE, and it is spelled as an EXPRESSION substituted into the consuming instruction's right-hand side — never a statement, never a named pseudo-register.** `sbb` and `adc` fell to `raw`, and a `raw` is a dataflow hole rather than a display gap: `fold.ts` reads it as reading nothing and nothing models its write, so every register a `sbb` defined was invisible to SSA, liveness, DCE and struct grouping. Two w64 witnesses, both wrong values: `sub_140001444` (`neg edi / sbb rax, rax / and rax, rbp`) returned `rax & 0x1400156B0` over the RAX from before the `sbb`, with the `edi = 1` arms deleted as dead because their only reader was the hole; `sub_14000BB30` (MSVC's strncmp, tail `sbb rax, rax / sbb rax, -1 / ret` entered from `cmp al, dl / jne`) returned the last loaded qword. The `eflags = …` proxy that once carried a compare was actively harmful — a statement GVN merged with a real `sub` and DCE deleted (`peek-a-bin-c33` stage 2b) — and a CF pseudo-register would be the same object one flag narrower, so what a `sbb`/`adc` gets is exactly what a `setcc` gets from `getCondition`: an expression over the setter's operands, built at lift time, bound by SSA like any read (`peek-a-bin-n9cl.6`). Nine things:
  - **The CF grammar is `flagModel.ts`'s, beside the whole-flags one, so there is still one copy of what x86 does to the flags.** `CARRY_PRESERVERS` (`inc`/`dec`: "The CF flag is not affected"), `CARRY_BORROW_SETTERS` (`cmp`/`sub` → `a u< b`), `CARRY_NONZERO_SETTERS` (`neg` → `a != 0`), `CARRY_ZERO_SETTERS` (`test`/`and`/`or`/`xor` → 0), `CARRY_REFUSED_SETTERS` (`add`/`adc`), `bt` through `parseBitTest`, and `carryEffect` as the transfer function with the same clear-on-unknown default. `carryOwnerBefore` is `flagOwnerBefore` run over it, with the compare arm of `spoils` (`spoilsOperands`, now one declaration) applied to EVERY setter. It is a **per-flag** owner, which the whole-flags model deliberately is not: after `cmp eax, ecx / inc edx / sbb ebx, ebx` the Jcc owner is the `inc` and CF is still the borrow. Nothing about `flagEffect`, `blockFlagOwner` or any guard's ownership moved; `flagModel.test.ts`'s drift guard gained the `{test, and, or, xor}` signature.
  - **`sbb d, d` is a SETTER spelled `d != 0` over the destination read after it, not a preserver.** `d - d - CF` borrows exactly when CF was set and leaves `d = -(CF)`, so its carry-out is its carry-in and is exactly `d != 0` afterwards — the `neg` spelling. The first cut made it a pass-through, and the strncmp tail then refused its second `sbb`: `sbb rax, rax` writes RAX, of which the compared AL is a part, so the compare's operand was spoiled — correctly — with no capture possible across the edge. Spelling it over the destination needs nothing held. `carryFor` **recurses** through a `sbb` owner to its own carry-in, so a chain whose first link was refused (`add eax, ecx / sbb eax, eax / sbb eax, -1`) is refused throughout: `eax` read after a `raw` `sbb` names the value from before it.
  - **A `sub`'s destination is CAPTURED before the `sub` runs.** `sub esi, eax / sbb edi, edx` — MSVC's 64-bit subtract, 5 per x86 binary — has its borrow spelled over the value the `sub` destroyed, so `operandCaptures` (the (a) map) holds `flg_<sub>_0 = esi` ABOVE `esi = esi - eax`, which is why the capture statements are pushed at the top of the lift loop rather than inside the compare handler. A spoiled `cmp`/`sub`/`neg` read by a `sbb`/`adc` is captured the way a spoiled compare read by a Jcc is; a spoiled `bt` is refused (0 occurrences).
  - **Cross-block CF comes from the block's SOLE predecessor, and `carryScanStream` prepends it UNCONDITIONALLY.** `carryPredecessor` is one non-self predecessor and nothing else — no unanimity arm (a CF value is an expression; the two-predecessor site has no equality rule to justify) and no "own instructions write nothing" precondition, because whether the predecessor matters depends on where the reader sits. Unlike `flagScanStream` the tail is prepended whenever known: the walk is last-writer-wins so extra history changes no block-local answer, and the recursion through `sbb d, d` must see the compare across the edge — a stream cut at the first in-block setter hid it and refused the whole strncmp tail. `pipeline.ts` and the three corpus replicas (`staleReads`, `popReads`, `lostDefs`) pass it as `liftBlock`'s last argument; omitting it is the pre-existing behaviour.
  - **`sbb`/`adc` moved from `CARRY_IN_WRITERS` to `RESULT_OWNERS`, as that set's docstring promised, and the promise came with a condition the lifter now honours: `branchFor` refuses a result owner the lifter left `raw`** (`resultOwnerLifted`). A `raw` `sbb eax, eax / jne` would otherwise emit `eax != 0` over the EAX from before the `sbb` — `peek-a-bin-3qrl`'s "lift first" rule, made checkable because liftability is decidable from the instruction stream: `carryFor` is re-asked over the same stream (for a predecessor's owner with none of its cross-block context, the conservative side), and the `lock or [mem], 0` fence `isValueNeutralLockedRmw` sends to `raw` is refused by the same predicate. `xadd` is the remaining carry-in clobber. The move recovers the 64-bit subtract's `js` (`edi >= 0` on the fallthrough, 2 per x86 binary — the "4 have a predecessor whose owner is cleared by `sbb`'s carry-in" of `peek-a-bin-suql`'s ledger) and leaves its `jg` refused: the result arm has no expression for a signed comparison over a register pair.
  - **Two exact folds, so the idioms read as what they are:** `-x == 0`/`-x != 0` → `x == 0`/`x != 0` (a two's-complement negation is zero exactly when its operand is, at every width; `neg edi / sbb rax, rax` folds to `-(edi != 0)`), and `x - (-c)` → `x + c` under the exact arithmetic `+`/`-` already claim (`sbb rax, -1` after `sbb rax, rax` reads `rax + 1 - (rax != 0)`).
  - **Measured at base `6299113`, both sides pinned (t32/t64/w32/w64).** Unlifted `sbb` **96 → 14** (24/25/24/23 → 3/4/3/4), `adc` **6 → 0**, total **621 → 533** with (a). `compare.mjs` verdict "no regression": guards `CHANGED 0/3/0/2`, `only-base 0` everywhere, `only-change 0/1/0/1`; unrecovered values 36 → 34 and 33 → 31 on the x86 pair (the `js`), 14 and 13 unmoved on x64; polarity audited 521, 577 → 579, 445, 500 → 502, all correct; staleGuards `named` 0 with `shapes` unmoved, `readerNamed` 0; arity over 0, callees lost 0, popReads/lostDefs/selfAssigns/armExits flat; gcc clean; `offsetof` ratio 1.00. Every `CHANGED` row was read: two are the (a) anchor move with byte-identical text, three are `splitStaleReads` repair variables renumbered (`cx_84` → `cx_78`) because the block's definition count changed. The two `only-change` guards are the `test eax, eax / je` whose only arm is `mov edi, 1` — in `sub_140001444` and its t64 twin — previously deleted as dead because EDI's only reader was the hole. `statements lifted (denom)` FALLS (7281 → 7272, 7764 → 7717, 6724 → 6717, 6893 → 6867): the new single-use definitions are inlined by `foldBlock`, so the `neg`, the `sbb` and the `and` become one statement.
  - **Both witnesses read against `objdump -d -M intel`:** `w64!sub_140001444` now ends `return -(edi != 0) & 0x1400156B0;` with the `edi = 1` arms present — RBP is the `lea rbp, [rip+0x14247]` at 0x140001462 and EDI the found flag, so it returns that pointer when a `SearchPathW` succeeded and 0 otherwise, which is what `neg edi / sbb rax, rax / and rax, rbp` computes; `w64!sub_14000BB30`'s `loc_14000BBAE` reads `rax = -(al < dl); return rax + 1 - (rax != 0);` — −1 when `al u< dl`, else +1 — beside its `return 0` arms, i.e. strncmp's −1/0/1. The `u<` is emitted as `<` by `cOp` (a known spelling limit) and the two `movabs` masks are still `raw`, taken in step (d).
  - **The negative control is `carryFor` returning null unconditionally:** `sbb` 14 → **96** and `adc` 0 → **6**, exactly the baseline, with **`npm run corpus` still exiting 0** — no gate sees a `raw` `sbb`, which is the class. **The 14 residual sites are each a refusal, not a gap**: 4 are `and ecx, imm / add eax, ecx / sbb ecx, ecx` (t32/w32 `sub_407000`/`sub_406810`, `add`'s carry-out); 2 are t32/w32 `0x40b734`/`0x40a464`, a `sbb eax, eax` block with SEVEN `jne` predecessors from `cmp al, [ecx+k]` unrolled compares (a unanimity arm over *memory* operands at different displacements would be a merge, refused); 4 are `0x14000ce53`'s pair on each x64 binary, with two predecessors; and 4 are `0x14000cdf8`'s pair on each, a block NO jump reaches — dead code after `xor eax, eax / ret`, decoded by gap fill — whose refusal is the right answer.
- **`bts`/`btr`/`btc` are statements over the bit base, and the CF they leave is deliberately not recorded.** All 94 `bts` and 18 `btr` corpus sites fell to `raw` — MSVC's `_bittestandset`/`_bittestandreset` on flag words (`bts r12d, 0xf`, `btr dword ptr [rcx + 0x18], 0xf`, `lock bts dword ptr [rdi + 0x18], 0xd`) — and a `raw` is a dataflow hole, so the flag word read after one named the value from before it, in every function that tests a bit it just set (`peek-a-bin-n9cl.6`). `bitWrite` (`lifter.ts`) lifts them to `d | (1 << i)`, `d & ~(1 << i)`, `d ^ (1 << i)`, a store when the base is memory. Five things:
  - **CF stays unrecorded, for the reason `peek-a-bin-frt8` recorded: it is the bit's value BEFORE the write**, so a reader after the instruction would see the post-write register. They remain `PARTIAL_FLAG_WRITERS` clobbers in the whole-flags model and clobbers in the CF value grammar, `parseBitTest` stays `bt`-only, and a `sbb` after one is `raw`. What `frt8` said about *ownership* stands unchanged; what changed is that the instruction's *value* effect is on the page.
  - **The index rule is the SDM's, applied so the spelling cannot contradict the width.** A REGISTER base reduces the bit offset modulo the operand size — an immediate is reduced at lift time (`bts eax, 0x21` writes bit 1) and a register index is spelled `(idx & (W-1))`. A MEMORY base addresses a bit STRING, so an offset may select a bit outside the operand the text names: a register index over memory is refused outright (`bts dword ptr [esp], eax`, 2 per PE32 binary, exactly `parseBitTest`'s refusal and the whole residue), and an immediate is admitted only below the operand width, where the string and the operand are the same bits. An 8-bit base and an unprefixed memory operand (no width) are refused.
  - **Atomicity is lost from the page** for the `lock`-prefixed forms, the same trade `peek-a-bin-3qrl` recorded for every locked read-modify-write.
  - **Measured at `f079f03` (step b), both sides pinned (t32/t64/w32/w64):** unlifted `bts` **94 → 4** (2/54/2/36 → 2/0/2/0), `btr` **18 → 0**, total **533 → 425**. `compare.mjs` "no regression"; unrecovered values unmoved at 34/14/31/13; `offsetof` 393/393, 282/282, 309/309, 287/287 with struct members and definitions scanned unmoved; guards `CHANGED 0/3/0/1` — every one a `splitStaleReads` repair variable renumbered (`r12d_14 → r12d_19`, `r13b_13 → r13b_24`) because the block gained definitions — and **`only-base 1` per x64 binary, read and benign**: `t64!sub_14000270C` 0x140002718's `if (ecx < 0x14)` is on the page with identical text, but its body now opens with `rdx_0 = rdx;`, a stale-read repair copy carrying no address, so the polarity auditor can no longer anchor it (`compare.mjs`' own note names this case). The `btr`s of `sub_140004D0C` are the `(0x200 & r12d) != 0` chains the `CHANGED` rows sit in: bit 15 written, bit 9 tested, both now visible.
  - **The negative control is the handler disabled** (`bitWrite` never consulted): `bts` 4 → 94 and `btr` 0 → 18 exactly, corpus exit 0 — the raw-is-a-hole class no gate sees.
- **`movabs` is a `mov`, and a 64-bit immediate that cannot be held exactly is refused rather than rounded.** Capstone spells the 64-bit-immediate form `movabs`; the `mov` handler tested `mn === "mov"`, so all 42 corpus sites were `raw` (`peek-a-bin-n9cl.6`). Accepting the spelling is one token; the refusal is the part that matters. `IRConst.value` is a JS number, exact only to 2^53, and `parseImm` rounds past it silently — the constant folder would then compute with a number the program never contains, and nothing downstream can tell. `exactImmediate` (`lifter.ts`) admits an immediate whose magnitude, or whose 64-bit signed reinterpretation, is a safe integer, and sends the rest to `raw`, which the unlifted census counts. Four things:
  - **`0xffffffffffffff0` is FIFTEEN hex digits.** It reads as −16 and is 0x0FFFFFFFFFFFFFF0 = 2^60 − 16, positive, past 2^53, and refused — the first draft of the test asserted −16 and was wrong about the machine. Sixteen digits with the top bit set (`0xfffffffffffffff0`) IS −16 and is exact. The row pins both.
  - **Measured at `c54fff4` (step c), both sides pinned:** unlifted `movabs` **42 → 36** (21/21 → 18/18 on t64/w64), total **425 → 419**. Lifted per binary: `0x2b992ddfa233`, `0x2b992ddfa232` (the /GS default cookie and its complement) and `0xffffffffffff`; refused per binary: the strncmp masks `0x8101010101010100` (3) and `0x7efefefefefefeff` (3), `0x101010101010101`, `0x1fffffffffffffff` (3), `0x2aaaaaaaaaaaaaab` (2), `0x2e8ba2e8ba2e8ba3`, `0x7ffffffffffffff0`, `0xffffffffffffff0` (4). `compare.mjs` "no regression", unrecovered and `offsetof` unmoved, guards `CHANGED 0/1/0/1`, both the same site: `__security_init_cookie`'s `if (rax == rdi)` — over an RDI nothing on the page assigned — is now `if (rax == 0x2B992DDFA232)`, with `r11 = (r11 ^ rbx) & 0xFFFFFFFFFFFF; r11 = r11 == 0x2B992DDFA232 ? 0x2B992DDFA233 : r11;` below it, which is the cookie's own logic.
  - **The refusal keeps a `raw` hole, and that is a stated cost, not an oversight.** In `w64!sub_14000BB30` the `r10`/`r11` zero-byte masks remain unassigned — `rax = rax ^ -1 ^ r10` reads an R10 nothing on the page defines — because the honest alternatives are worse here: a rounded constant is a wrong value in compiling C, and an `r11 = <unrecovered>` assignment (a definition SSA sees) is epic 2's (f) shape for `setcc` and belongs with it. 36 sites carry the cost.
  - **Negative control: `movabs` not accepted by the handler** — 36 → **42**, exactly the baseline, corpus exit 0.
- **`rep movs*`/`stos*` are lifted to the MSVC intrinsics with their side effects modelled, and the dispatch is on the raw mnemonic's TOKENS.** The handler tested `mn === "rep"` or `opStr.startsWith("rep ")`, and Capstone produces neither: it spells the prefix into the mnemonic — `rep movsd` with `dword ptr es:[edi], dword ptr [esi]` in `opStr` on the real binaries, an empty `opStr` in the fixtures — so the path was DEAD against every real instruction, pinned as a KNOWN BUG in `lifter.test.ts` since it was found, and all 32 `rep` + 12 unprefixed `stosd` corpus sites were `raw`, i.e. dataflow holes that write EDI/ESI/ECX with nothing modelling it (`peek-a-bin-n9cl.6`). `parseStringOp` (`lifter.ts`) reads the tokens and the operands are implicit, as the machine's are. Six things:
  - **The spelling is the intrinsic, never `memcpy`/`memset`.** `__movsd(edi, esi, ecx)` and `__stosd(edi, eax, ecx)` have the machine's exact semantics; `memset(edi, al, ecx)` — what the dead path emitted — misstates a dword fill as a byte fill and needs a size multiplication nobody wrote. The accumulator follows the width (`al`/`ax`/`eax`/`rax`), `repe`/`repz` collapse to `rep`.
  - **The side effects are MODELLED, or the registers the primitive leaves behind are read as their old values**: `edi = edi + ecx*S; esi = esi + ecx*S; ecx = 0`, increments BEFORE the zeroing. Then RCX is marked SPENT (`regState.noteRead`) so `collectArgs64` does not hand the zeroed counter to the following call as its first argument — an arity OVER-count, which is a gate at 0; pinned by a row whose `call` after `rep stosd` takes no arguments. An unprefixed `stosd`/`movsd` is one store plus the pointer advance (`*(int32_t*)(edi) = eax; edi += 4`).
  - **Refused: a primitive inside a `std` region, and every `repne`/`repnz` form.** Under DF=1 the primitive runs BACKWARDS and the intrinsic spelling assumes forwards; `directionDown` is block-local (`std` sets it, `cld` clears it, DF is assumed clear on entry as the ABI guarantees at every call boundary), and all 8 corpus `std` sites are `std / rep movs / cld` in one block — MSVC's overlapping-`memmove` tail. `std`/`cld` themselves stay `raw`. `repne scasw` (4 per x64 binary, `wcslen`) is a search position and a flag with no single destination to name.
  - **The SSE `movsd` is left alone by the one fact that separates it**: it names an XMM register, which no string primitive can, and `SSE_SCALAR` still takes it. The string `movsd`'s memory operands are `es:`-prefixed, but the test is on the XMM name because the x64 spelling (`rep stosw word ptr [rdi], ax`) carries no segment.
  - **Measured at `f40e2e3` (step d), both sides pinned (t32/t64/w32/w64):** unlifted `rep` **32 → 8** (14/2/14/2 → 4/0/4/0), `stosd` **12 → 0**, total **419 → 383** — the census's final figure against 621 at `6299113`; the 8 remaining `rep` are exactly the 4 `std`-region `rep movsd` per x86 binary (`t32!sub_40B780`, `w32!sub_409420`), beside their 8 `std`/8 `cld`, and `repne` 8 is unmoved. Emitted: 7 `__movsd` + 3 `__stosd` per x86 binary, 2 `__stosw` per x64. `compare.mjs` "no regression": guards `CHANGED 0` / `only-base 0` / `only-change 0` on all four; unrecovered unmoved; arity over 0; callees lost 0; undefined callees unmoved (the intrinsics are neither `sub_` nor imports and are not counted); `offsetof` **393/393 → 400/400 and 309/309 → 316/316** on the x86 pair, ratio 1.00, +2 members and +1 definition scanned each — the unprefixed `stosd` stores and the advanced EDI now give `structs.ts` a base to group in one function per binary, and every one of the seven new fields is verified by the compiled-and-run program; +19 lifted statements per x86 binary, 0 on x64.
  - **The negative control is the handler disabled (`parseStringOp` never consulted):** `rep` 8 → **32**, `stosd` 0 → **12**, total 383 → 419 exactly, corpus exit 0. The `lifter.test.ts` row that pinned the dead path as a KNOWN BUG is now its first positive row, and `pipeline.test.ts`'s "operands verbatim" row moved to `repne scasw`, the form still refused.
- **Every `setcc` form reads the Jcc table, and a form `getCondition` cannot answer becomes an assignment of `__unrecovered_N` rather than `raw`.** The lifter carried `COND_SET`, a fourteen-entry hand-written map (`sete` … `setns`), and every other form — `seto`, `setno`, `setp`, `setnp`, and every alias spelling (`setc`, `setnbe`, `setnl`, …) — fell through to the `raw` arm as `__asm { seto al }` (`peek-a-bin-5b6q.3`). A `raw` is a dataflow hole: `fold.ts`'s `blockLiveOut` reads it as reading nothing, nothing models its write, so the byte read after it named the value from BEFORE the instruction. `CONDITION_CODES` (`lifter.ts`) now holds the thirty `cc` suffixes once, `SETCC_PATTERN`/`CMOV_PATTERN` match the family, and `conditionJcc` rewrites the suffix onto `j` — `setb` → `jb` — so `getCondition` is asked the Jcc's question for every form, exactly as `cmovcc` already was. Four things:
  - **An unanswerable form is an ASSIGNMENT, and that is the point.** `getCondition` returns `unknown` for `jo`/`jno` (OF is not modelled), `jp`/`jnp` (PF has no cheap spelling) and `jb`/`jae` after `test` (constants); the arm emits `al = __unrecovered_N`, a definition SSA sees, so the `movzx eax, al` after it binds to the admission and the return reads `__unrecovered_1` rather than an `al` nothing defined. **The constant forms are not spelled `al = 0`** — the reason `getCondition` will not emit `if (1)` (a constant is a control-flow claim no gate models, `peek-a-bin-x72e`) applies to a value the next instruction tests just the same.
  - **The set is the guard on the pattern.** `set(\w+)` alone reads `setssbsy` (CET) as a `setcc` and hands `getCondition` a `jssbsy`; with the set it stays `raw`. `cmovcc` was already dispatched by pattern with no such guard — inert today, since no other `cmov*` mnemonic exists — and now takes the same one.
  - **The refusal text was `jo([object Object], [object Object])`.** `getCondition`'s last line interpolated two `IRExpr`s into a template string; a `jo` guard would have printed it, and the corpus has 0 such lines, so a `setcc` is the first thing to carry it to the page. It reads `jo after cmp` now, named after the owner kind.
  - **Measured at `2c2ceeb` → step (f): every corpus `setcc` is `sete`/`setne`/`setge`/`setns`/`setbe` (t32 51, t64 51, w64 48, w32 48 by objdump), all forms the old map carried, so all four `funcs_*.jsonl` are BYTE-IDENTICAL and every row of `compare.mjs` reads `N -> N`.** The corpus negative control is therefore INERT and is reported as such, not tuned: the discriminating control is the `pipeline.test.ts` row asserting that `seto` reaches the page as an unrecovered return rather than an `unlifted` comment, which the old map fails.
- **`rol`/`ror`, `bswap`, the one-operand `imul` and `movnti` are lifted; each was a `raw` hole with a measured population.** At `2c2ceeb` the x64 census read `movnti` 24, `imul` 3, `bswap` 2, `rol` 1, `ror` 1 per binary, 0 on the PE32 pair (`peek-a-bin-5b6q.3`). The spellings and their refusals:
  - **`rotate` (`lifter.ts`) spells `rol d, n` as `(d << n) | (d >>> (W - n))` and `ror` as `(d >>> n) | (d << (W - n))` over the destination's width.** An immediate is reduced the way the machine reduces it — masked to 5 bits (6 at 64-bit operand size), then modulo the width (`rol al, 9` is a rotate by 1) — and a count reducing to 0 is refused rather than spelled as a self-assignment, a shape `selfAssigns.ts` would then have to be taught about. A `cl` count is `cl & (W-1)`, and the complement is `(W - cl) & (W-1)`: `W - cl` alone is `W` at `cl == 0`, a shift by the full width, undefined in C. Memory destinations are stores. **`rcl`/`rcr` stay `raw`**: they rotate through CF once per iteration and this file spells CF for one setter at a time; both PE32 binaries carry 2 `rcr` (`div ecx / shr edx, 1 / rcr eax, 1`, a 64-bit shift idiom) and they are the fixture `pipeline.test.ts` now uses where `rol` used to stand in for "an unlifted flag writer".
  - **`bswap` is the MSVC intrinsic of its width — `_byteswap_ulong` or `_byteswap_uint64` — as an `IRCall` expression.** The 16-bit encoding exists and the SDM says its result is undefined, so it is refused rather than spelled `_byteswap_ushort`. Like `__movsd`, the intrinsic is deliberately NOT in `apitypes.ts` (the arity oracle must not measure its input) and compiles under the harness's gnu89 as an implicitly declared function, so `gcc clean` stays 279/279.
  - **One-operand `imul` is `mul`'s shape with signed casts on both operands, and `wideningMultiply` is now the one declaration of both.** The high half is the product shifted by the width, written FIRST so the low half's write cannot be read by it — `mul`'s standing rule — but that rule is not enough when the source IS the high register (`imul rdx`, two of the six corpus sites) or a memory source addressed through it (`mul dword ptr [edx]`): writing RDX first destroys the operand the low half then reads. Those go through a `tmp_mul` temporary, as `xchg` does, and copy propagation folds it (0 `tmp_mul` on the page). A byte multiply widens into AX alone; the old `mul` handler mapped a byte source onto EDX:EAX, fixed in the shared helper (0 corpus occurrences). **The 64-bit high half is spelled `>> 64`, which is not C**: it is `mul`'s standing spelling limit, `__mulh`/`__umulh` would be exact, and changing it belongs to a change that takes `mul` with it. **The casts reach the page only where the inferred type disagrees**: the emitter's redundant-cast suppression absorbs `(int64_t)rax` once `inferTypes` has typed RAX signed, so the corpus text shows none — the IR carries them and the type inference reads them.
  - **Every corpus one-operand `imul` follows a REFUSED `movabs`** (`0x2aaaaaaaaaaaaaab`, `0x2e8ba2e8ba2e8ba3` — MSVC's divide-by-constant magic, past 2^53). RAX is therefore the raw hole the movabs entry records, and the lift now NAMES it: `rdx = 0x140014390 * (rcx - 0x140014000) >> 0x40 >> 3` reads an RAX left over from an earlier `lea`. Before, the `raw imul` left `rdx = rcx - 0x140014000 >> 3` — RDX read stale across the multiply — which was equally wrong and less visible. No new wrongness class; the repair is the unknown-assignment for a refused `movabs`, which is epic 2's and not this bead's.
  - **`movnti` is a plain store** — the SDM gives it `mov` semantics and no flags; the non-temporal hint is about the cache. Memory destination, register source is the only encoding; anything else is refused. All 48 corpus sites are the CRT's `memset`/`memcpy` large-block tails (`movnti [rcx+N], rdx` / `movnti [rcx-N], r9|r10`).
  - **A lifted statement is subject to DCE where a `raw` was not, and one site shows it.** `__security_check_cookie`'s failure path is `ror rcx, 0x10 / jmp __report_gsfailure`; the tail jump is spelled `sub_140004290()` with no arguments (an arity under-count already on the report), so the rotate-back's result is dead and the statement is deleted, where the `raw` stood because nothing could delete it. The emitted call already omitted RCX; the deletion is a consequence of that, not a new fact.
  - **Measured at `8060f99` → step (g), both sides pinned (t32/t64/w64/w32):** unlifted total **126/66/66/125 → 126/35/35/125**, `movnti` 24 → 0, `imul` 3 → 0, `bswap` 2 → 0, `rol` 1 → 0, `ror` 1 → 0 on each x64 binary; PE32 pair byte-identical. Every gate flat: polarity inverted/mismatch 0, arity OVER 0, callees lost 0, stale version-0 0, pop-restored 0, self-assign 1/0/1/0 unchanged, unrecovered 34/14/13/31 unchanged, gcc clean and `offsetof` ratios unmoved. Emitted C changed in **7 functions per x64 binary**: the cookie check, the three `imul` sites, two `movnti` block-copy/fill routines and the `bswap` `memcmp` tail; hand-read against `objdump -d -M intel`: the cookie's `rol rcx, 0x10 / test cx, 0xffff` is `rcx = rcx << 0x10 | (uint64_t)rcx >> 0x30; if (((uint16_t)rcx & 0xFFFF) == 0)`; `t64!sub_14000CE10`'s `mov rcx,[rcx+rdx] / bswap rax / bswap rcx / cmp rax,rcx / sbb eax,eax / sbb eax,-1` is `rax = _byteswap_uint64(rax); rcx = _byteswap_uint64(*(int64_t*)(rcx + rdx_1)); eax = -(rax < rcx); return eax + 1 - (eax != 0);`; `t64!sub_140007EA0`'s `movnti [rcx] / [rcx+8] / [rcx+0x10] / add rcx,0x40 / [rcx-0x28] …` is the store sequence in that order; `t64!sub_1400036B0`'s `sub rbx,[r8+r14*8] / imul rbx / mov r12,rdx / sar r12,4` is `r12 = rax * (rbx - *(int64_t*)(0x140016EE0 + (r14_1 << 3))) >> 0x40 >> 4`. **One CHANGED guard per x64 binary, read and benign**: 0x140002012's `(cx & 0xFFFF) == 0` became `((uint16_t)rcx & 0xFFFF) == 0` because RCX now has a definition to read the alias from.
  - **The negative control is the four arms disabled** (`rotate`, `bswap`, `movnti` unreachable; the one-operand `imul` handed `null`): unlifted **35 → 66** on both x64 binaries, `movnti` 0 → 24, `imul` 0 → 3, `bswap` 0 → 2, `rol` 0 → 1, `ror` 0 → 1 — each bucket to its baseline exactly — and all four `funcs_*.jsonl` byte-identical to the pre-change artifact; corpus exit 0 throughout, the raw-is-a-hole class no gate sees.
- **A `div`/`idiv` is admitted only behind the high-half setup matching its signedness; otherwise it is refused, and the refusals are the finding.** The machine divides the DOUBLE-width `EDX:EAX` (`RDX:RAX`, `DX:AX`) by the operand; the IR spells `eax / ecx`, the dividend as its low half alone. That is exact only when the high half was that half's extension — zero for `div`, sign for `idiv` — and the handler used to assume it for every site, lifting `div` and `idiv` identically with no casts (`peek-a-bin-5b6q.3`). Six things:
  - **`dividendSetup` (`lifter.ts`) walks BACKWARDS from the divide and the first writer of the high register decides.** `xor edx,edx` / `sub edx,edx` / `mov edx,0` / `and edx,0` → zeroed (a 32-bit write zero-extends, so `xor edx,edx` sets up a 64-bit `div rcx`; `xor dx,dx` does NOT zero EDX and is refused for one); `cdq`/`cqo`/`cwd` at the dividend's own width → sign-extended; any other writer → refused; the start of the stream → refused. **Instructions that do not write the high half are stepped over**, because the corpus's commonest shape is `xor edx,edx / lea rax,[rdx-0x20] / div rcx` (5 of 14 t64 sites; the `lea` reads the zeroed RDX to spell −0x20) and `xor edx,edx / or rax,-1 / div r12`, `xor edx,edx / mov rax,rbx / div r12` — "the immediately preceding statement" would have refused nearly every well-set-up site. `writesRegister` is the one declaration of the write test, conservative in the refusing direction: the implicit DX writers (`call`, `syscall`, `cdq`/`cqo`/`cwd` at the wrong width, one-operand `mul`/`imul`/`div`/`idiv`, `rdtsc`, `cpuid`, `cmpxchg8b`, …), both operands of `xchg`/`mulx`, the second of `xadd`, else the first operand.
  - **The stream carries a unique predecessor's tail ahead of the block's own instructions** — `carryScanStream`, the step the CF and flag walks already take, since a block with one way in is entered with that predecessor's registers. It is what admits `t32!sub_4026E8`'s `xor edx,edx / cmp ebx,ecx / jbe L / mov eax,ecx / div [ebp-0xc]` (the divide's block is entered only through the `jbe`) and t64's `xor edx,edx / cmp rbp,r10 / jbe L / mov eax,r10d / … / div r9d` on BOTH edges. A block with two predecessors is refused whatever they hold: `mov eax,ecx / jmp L / mov eax,ebx / L: div [ebp-0xc]` — 0 corpus sites, since that `div` shares its block with the `xor` in every occurrence.
  - **The mismatch is refused, not the missing setup alone.** A `div` after `cdq` divides a huge dividend whenever EAX is negative; an `idiv` after `xor edx,edx` divides a large positive one whenever EAX is past 2^31. Neither shape appears in the corpus (there is no `idiv` at all — all 52 divisions are `div`), so the rule costs nothing measurable and is stated rather than tuned. The byte form (`AX / r/m8` into AL, AH) is refused outright: its "high half" is AH.
  - **Both operands carry the cast of their signedness and width — `(uint32_t)`/`(int32_t)` … `(uint64_t)`/`(int64_t)` — which is now the whole difference between the two mnemonics in the IR.** On the page they show where `fold.ts` leaves them: `fold.ts` strips a cast at a register's own width and folds one on a constant away (both pre-existing, deliberate rules), so `(uint32_t)eax / (uint32_t)ecx` prints `eax / ecx` and the corpus's `-0x20 / rcx` keeps its (wrong, pre-existing, baseline-identical) signed spelling — while a memory or parameter divisor keeps it (`(edi - ebx) / (uint32_t)arg_2`, `ebx % (uint32_t)var_C`, `rbp % (uint64_t)r9d` where R8 came from `mov r8d, r9d`, a zero-extension the old text lost) and the promoted variables' declared types carry it (`uint32_t var_8` vs `int32_t var_8`, the `pipeline.test.ts` rows). Changing either fold rule moves the whole corpus and is not this bead's.
  - **Measured at `3257f3f` → step (h), both sides pinned (t32/t64/w64/w32): 4 refusals, `unlifted div` 0 → 2/0/0/2, every one hand-read against `objdump -d -M intel` and CORRECT.** Both are in the CRT's 64-bit unsigned divide helper (`t32!sub_40AD70`, `w32!sub_40C0E0` — `_aulldvrm`): `mov eax,[esp+0xc] / xor edx,edx / div ecx / mov ebx,eax / mov eax,[esp+0x8] / div ecx` — the second `div` divides `remainder:low`, a genuine 64-bit dividend the old lift printed as `eax_6 = eax / ecx`, a WRONG VALUE; and `shr ecx,1 / rcr ebx,1 / shr edx,1 / rcr eax,1 / or ecx,ecx / jne / div ebx`, the shifted 64-bit dividend, printed as `eax_12 = eax_11 / ebx_2`. No well-set-up site was refused: the other 48 divisions lift as before with the casts added. Emitted C changed in 2/1/1/2 functions; `compare.mjs` marks the four `unlifted div` rows `<<<` because it judges ANY rise in a bucket a regression — here the rise is the refusal of a wrong value, adjudicated as such, and every other gate row is flat (polarity, arity OVER 0, callees lost 0, stale version-0 0, pop-restored 0, fold-lost 0, cross-edge 0, self-assign 1/0/1/0, unrecovered 34/14/13/31, gcc clean and `offsetof` unmoved, guards CHANGED 0 on all four).
  - **The negative control is the refusal disabled** (`dividendSetup` not consulted): `unlifted div` 2 → 0 on both PE32 binaries and the two `_aulldvrm` functions return to their old text, casts aside; corpus exit 0 either way — the wrong-value class no gate sees.
- **`test` clears OF and CF; it does NOT clear SF. Which is why `getCondition`'s `test` arm answers strictly more Jcc forms than its `result` arm.** These two arms look parallel and are not, and conflating them costs recovery in one direction or correctness in the other. For a **result** owner (`dec ecx`, `sub eax, ecx`) only ZF and SF are functions of the result — CF and OF depend on the operands and on which arithmetic ran — so only `je`/`jne`/`js`/`jns` are answerable and everything else must stay unknown. For a **`test`** owner every flag a Jcc can read is either a function of `a & b` or a known constant, because `test` pins OF and CF to 0 (Intel SDM: "The OF and CF flags are set to 0. The SF, ZF, and PF flags are set according to the result"). So `jle` — `ZF=1 or SF≠OF` — collapses to `(a & b) <= 0`, and `jg`, `jl`, `jge`, `ja` and `jbe` follow the same way. The arm answered only the ZF/SF forms until `peek-a-bin-92yy`, which recovered **122 unrecovered values corpus-wide** (70 `jle`, 50 `jg`, 2 `jl`) with 0 pre-existing guards changed. **The suite had asserted the defect as the rule** — `it("returns unknown for a jcc with no meaning after test")` with the comment "jle depends on SF/OF, which `test` clears" — and its own neighbour refuted it, since `js` reads SF alone and was already answered. Two things not to re-try: `jb`/`jae` and `jo`/`jno` read *only* a flag `test` clears, so they are constants rather than conditions, and emitting `if (1)` is a control-flow claim that `structureCFG` may act on and no gate models (`peek-a-bin-x72e`, **6 real sites — 4 on t32 and 2 on w32, and 0 on BOTH x64 binaries** — re-measured at `075c031` two ways, adjacent and over a flag-transparent window, both giving the same 4/0/0/2; the bead and this file both said 12, which is wrong under either reading. MSVC's 64-bit negate idiom, which on a PE32 image is synthesised from 32-bit registers, hence the x64 zero. The flag owner there is correctly identified and the shape is **not** evidence of misattribution); and `jp`/`jnp` read PF, which is a real function of the result with no cheap spelling.
- **A Jcc alone in its block is answered from its single predecessor, and the terminator skip is the whole mechanism.** `blockFlagOwner` read only `block.insns`, so for a block holding nothing but the Jcc the walk ran zero iterations, reported `{kind: "none", reason: "no-owner"}`, and the guard emitted as `__unrecovered_N` — 88 blocks across the corpus, of which **69 are recoverable** (`peek-a-bin-suql`). `flagScanStream` (`flagModel.ts`) prepends the predecessor's instructions when, and only when, the block's own scan finds no flag writer at all; `flagPredecessor` supplies it (it was `solePredecessor` until `peek-a-bin-xdxt` generalised it — see the next gotcha), and both consumers — `lifter.ts`'s `branchFor` and `structure.ts`'s `extractCondition` — read the one stream, because two would drift about which edge and which terminator. Four things are load-bearing:
  - **The predecessor's terminator must be skipped explicitly.** `jmp`, `ret` and every Jcc are absent from `NO_FLAG_WRITE` — correctly, that set is narrow on purpose — so `flagEffect` classes them `{kind: "clobber", why: "unrecognised"}` and a walk that reads one clears the very owner it came for. Measured both ways: without the skip the change recovers **0** guards and the emitted C is byte-identical to base on all four binaries. It is not an optimisation, it is the difference between 0 and 69.
  - **Either edge counts.** A Jcc writes no flags, so a predecessor's exit flags reach its successor on the taken edge exactly as on the fallthrough; the predecessor's condition being true rather than false on that path is a fact about *values*. Restricting to the fallthrough leaves 17 of the 69 unclaimed, and on the x64 pair the taken edge is the *majority* (6 of 13 on t64, 4 of 9 on w64).
  - **The walk continues *through* the block rather than stopping at the boundary**, so `spoils` sets `spoiled` for a clobber on either side of the edge with no second grammar. Across the edge a **compare** owner is therefore filtered on `canSpellCondition`, where block-locally it deliberately is not (`structure.ts` re-asks that of the machine text); and refusal 3 (`blockHasCompare`) is asked of the *owner's* block, since keeping the compare and result paths disjoint is a property of the block the flags were set in.
  - **The condition cannot come from the reading block's `RegState`** — that state is what walking *this* block left behind and this block never executed the compare. It is re-read from the owning instruction through `setFlagsFromCompare`, the same call `extractCondition` makes, so the two readings cannot disagree.

  The 19 refusals are each an already-correct mechanism: 12 have more than one predecessor (`peek-a-bin-xdxt`, which has since taken *2* more of that class by unanimity and left those 12 refused — see the next gotcha), 4 have a predecessor whose owner is cleared by `sbb`'s carry-in, 3 have a spoiled compare. **Recovery reaches the page for only 51 of the 69**, and the gap is a separate defect rather than a shortfall here: 18 of the blocks are in regions `structureCFG` never emits at all (`peek-a-bin-3zji`).
- **…and "one predecessor" was the special case, not the rule: what makes a block's entry flags knowable is that every way in leaves them saying the same thing.** `solePredecessor` refused any block with two predecessors, on the correct reasoning that the flags — and so the condition — differ per incoming edge. Correct for most of that population and *false* for the part of it where both edges make the identical test: MSVC's `_stricmp` tail joins `cmp ah, al / jne` and `xor ecx, ecx / cmp ah, al / je` into one `mov ecx, -1 / jb` block, so the `jb` reads `ah u< al` however it was reached and one block-local `if` states the machine exactly. `flagPredecessor` (the renamed `solePredecessor`) now answers with any predecessor when **all** of them are unanimous, via `unanimousCompare`, and the single-predecessor arm is the degenerate case of the same sentence. Five things (`peek-a-bin-xdxt`):
  - **It is a UNANIMITY test and never a merge**, which is what keeps it out of the per-edge-condition business the surrounding machinery cannot do. Census re-derived at `f169c00`: of 35/18/13/24 lone-Jcc blocks (t32/t64/w64/w32) over 2285/2219/1966/2019 jcc blocks, 3/5/4/2 = **14** have more than one predecessor — every one of them exactly two, and every one with a spellable compare on both edges — of which **2 agree** (one per 32-bit binary, the `_stricmp` tail) and **12 do not**. The bead's figure of 12 was that differ count, measured before `peek-a-bin-xskz`; the 2 agreeing blocks were never in it.
  - **The 12 are refused on evidence and all three schemes for them were costed.** They are four shapes: `test rbx,rbx` against `test rbp,rbp` (2 sites, genuinely unrelated); `test dword ptr [rbx+0x18], 0x2000` against `test byte ptr [r13+rax+8], 4` (4 sites, unrelated); `cmp byte ptr [rbp-0x68], r11b` against `cmp …, sil` (3 sites, same place, different right-hand side); and `cmp eax, -1` against `cmp rax, -1` (2 sites, same test at different widths, so merging needs a width decision). Each needs a boolean or a value **materialised in each predecessor** and a phi over it — `liftBlock` returns one block's statements and cannot place one, so it would be a new `pipeline.ts` step, and the boolean-phi form states strictly *less* at the jcc than the machine text does (`if (cond_1)` is not a test the reader can check). Duplicating the block per predecessor instead was refused as changing the CFG the structurer sees at a *merge* point, which is where loop shape is decided and no gate models it. 12 guards is not worth either; refusing them is what the code now does explicitly.
  - **The agreement test is compared over Capstone's own operand TEXT, with two refusals that make text equality exact rather than approximate.** No `rip` — `parseOperand` resolves a rip-relative operand against the instruction's own address, so identical text at two addresses is two different expressions — and no stack pointer, because `spoils` and `clobberedAfter` read `push`/`pop` differently about RSP and `structure.ts`'s `conditionSpoiled` scan is asked of the *returned* predecessor only. Both have 0 corpus occurrences and are there so the claim is sound by construction. A `result` or `bittest` owner is refused for the same reason: its condition is a function of the destination too, so operand text would not settle it (also 0 occurrences).
  - **Every edge gets the forward `spoils` scan, which is why one `conditionSpoiled` call still suffices.** The owner is read over `[predecessor's tail, ...the block's own instructions]` per edge — `flagScanStream`'s rule, from the same declaration — so a predecessor that overwrote what names its compare never reaches unanimity, and the returned predecessor is representative of the others in that respect as well as in the condition.
  - **THE DECISIVE MEASUREMENT IS THE WRONG VERSION, NOT THE ABSENT ONE.** Drop only the agreement test and answer from the first predecessor: **12 more guards are recovered — exactly the 12 differ sites — and `npm run corpus` exits 0 with VERDICT "no regression"**, polarity 0 inverted and 0 mismatch over 519/577/499/442 audited all correct, gcc 1127/1127, offsetof ratio 1.00, staleGuards named 0, every other gate flat. 8 of those 12 are *anchored and passed* by the polarity auditor, because the operator is right and only the operands are wrong on one path — and `staleGuards`' population is block-local, so a cross-edge error is not in its denominator (`peek-a-bin-0xe2`). Hand-read: `t64!sub_140002A2C` emits `if (rbx_2 == 0) goto loc_140002C5B;` at 0x140002afd while the edge from 0x140002c19 tests RBP. **So nothing in the corpus suite protects these 12 refusals; only the code and `pipeline.test.ts`/`flagModel.test.ts` do**, and three of those tests fail under that variant.

    Measured at base `f169c00`, both sides pinned: **2 guards recovered**, t32 0x40E696 and w32 0x40CDB6, both `jb` after `cmp ah, al` emitted as `ah >= al` on the fallthrough arm, both anchor **A** and `OK`. Unrecovered values **39/12/11/35 → 38/12/11/34**, token occurrences 78/24/22/70 → 76/24/22/68; polarity audited 516 → 517 and 440 → 441, correct 517/517 and 441/441, `CHANGED 0` / `only-base 0` / `only-change 1` on each 32-bit binary; **t64 and w64 byte-identical** (an x86-only idiom), and exactly one function of emitted C changed per 32-bit binary. Every gate flat — gcc 288/279/275/285 clean, offsetof ratio 1.00 with fields and definitions unmoved, 0 inverted, 0 mismatch, 0 throws, 0 statements dropped, 0 callees lost, 0 loops short, arity exact/under/over unmoved, staleReads 0/0, staleGuards 0 named with `shapes` unmoved, popReads 0, lostDefs 0, armExits 0, wildBranches 0, unencodableNames 0 — and **loop shapes identical**: `for` 6/6/6/6, `while` 111/106/99/98, `do/while` 78/77/76/77, `if` 1987/1938/1719/1759. With `unanimousCompare` disabled the emitted C is **byte-identical to base on all 1127 functions**, which is what proves the rename and the early return for a block that already owns its flags changed nothing.
    **Stated limitation**: the two recovered guards read `ah` and `al`, which `canonReg` maps to one register, and in these two functions the emitted C never assigns `ah` — a pre-existing sub-register aliasing defect shared with the `if (ah != al)` guard two lines above, not something this introduces.
- **A memory destination is spellable, and refusing it was a spelling limit that read as a dataflow fact.** `dec dword ptr [ebp + 0x10] / je` owns its Jcc's flags like any other `RESULT_OWNERS` form, and the value tested is the *destination read after the instruction ran* — which for memory is a deref, exactly as it is a register read for `dec ecx`. It emitted `__unrecovered_N` anyway, for two mechanical reasons in different files: `flagModel.ts` published only `destReg`, which is null for memory, so `canSpellCondition` answered `destReg !== null` and refused; and `branchFor` then built the condition with `irReg(destText, regSize(destText))`, which cannot express `dword ptr [ebp + 0x10]` — and would have been *worse than a refusal*, since `regSize` falls back to 4 for any unrecognised name. The bead on file had recorded the cause as dataflow ("the result is in memory; naming it would need a load that is not in the IR") and that was **wrong**: `liftBlock` lifts the form to a `store`, `promoteVars` names the slot, and t32 0x4020EE printed `arg_2--;` on the line directly above `if (!!__unrecovered_1 …)`. The value was already on the page. Four things about the fix (`peek-a-bin-ie0j`):
  - **`destForm: "reg" | "mem" | "none"` is what spellability is asked of, and `destReg` stays for `spoils`.** They answer different questions — "which register do I watch for an overwrite" has the answer *none* for memory, which is not the same claim as "nothing can name this". Conflating them is what refused every row. The `"none"` arm keeps the relaxation from being "anything that is not a register" and is pinned by a test, since no well-formed x86 result owner reaches it.
