These are per-function analyses feeding a view: the disassembly, the xrefs and the strings are all correct on the same image, and the caller is a `useMemo` that must still render, so a throw would remove a side panel over an image whose analysis is fine. `unsupportedOnArch` is deliberately *not* called — there is no channel to carry a message through `StackFrame | null`, and adding one would be the parallel machinery the file warns against.
  - **The two refusals have completely different standing and must not be quoted as one result.** `analyzeStackFrame` already returned null for **all 1033** detected A64 functions of t64-arm.exe and w64-arm.exe at `cc70fe6`, so its refusal changes no output that exists: it is **unfalsifiable on this corpus**, pinned by `stack.test.ts` and nowhere else, the status `pop esp` and the `rip`/`rsp` refusals have. `inferSignature` answered `{ convention: "fastcall", paramCount: 0 }` for the same 1033, and that reached the page.
  - **`FunctionSignature | null` is a widening that had to happen, because the type could not say "unknown".** Its two fields are a convention name and a count, both required, so silence was unrepresentable and the function had to assert something. Both render sites already had the null path wired (`DisassemblyRows` through `getSigForFunc`, `InstructionDetail` through `signature ? … : ""`) and `decompileFunction` already declares `signature: FunctionSignature | null`, so the null propagates into the one non-view consumer with no change at all. **Answering `{ convention: "aapcs64", paramCount: 0 }` was considered and refused**: the count is the false half, and spelling the convention correctly would dress an unanalysed function as an analysed one.
  - **The refusal must precede `inferSignature`'s empty-instruction early return**, which is the one path that answers from `is64` alone without looking at an instruction, and would go on inventing `fastcall` for an A64 image. That ordering is pinned by a test and negative-controlled — moving the refusal below it fails exactly one test. The same assertion for `analyzeStackFrame` is **inert** and was removed rather than kept: that function answers null for a function with no instructions in range on every architecture, so both orderings agree at every input, and a test of it would pin nothing. It was written, run as a control, and deleted.
  - **`src/disasm/__tests__/archThreading.test.ts` guards the residue the type cannot see** — that a caller *derived* the architecture from the machine word rather than writing a literal `"x86"`, which compiles and reinstates the defect at that call site. It matches identifiers rather than layout, excludes the two declaring modules, and asserts the caller count before asserting anything about it, since a scrape that matches nothing reports a clean tree.

- **…AND THE SAME FILE DID, ON x86, EVERYTHING THE ESSAY ABOVE FORBIDS ON A64 — FOUR SITES, THREE LINES BELOW IT.** `inferSignature` is rendered by `InstructionDetail` and by `getSigForFunc` in `DisassemblyRows`, i.e. beside essentially every function in the list, and it could contradict the decompile panel one pane over. Everything below is measured at `97ef927` (`peek-a-bin-j4uk.6`).

  **(a) THE x64 STACK-ARGUMENT CLAIM IS DELETED, AND THAT IS A MEASURED NARROWING RATHER THAN A REPAIR.** `inferSignature64` scanned the first 20 instructions for `[rsp\s*\+\s*0x…]` and read an offset `>= 0x28` as argument `floor((N - 0x28) / 8) + 5`, **with no tracking of `sub rsp, N` and none of a single `push`**. Incoming argument 5 sits at `[rsp + allocation + pushes + 0x28]`, so with the allocation unread the pattern did not describe the incoming argument area at all — it described the function's OWN OUTGOING one, which is where MSVC puts `mov [rsp+0x20], rax` on essentially every non-leaf x64 function. **`t64!sub_140001000` does `sub rsp, 0x848` and the rule answered 262 parameters** where the decompiler emitted 4; over the two x64 binaries **54 functions each over-claimed, worst +258**. Three reasons the arithmetic was not simply repaired, and they are the substance of this entry:
  - **`stack.ts` cannot answer the question, by construction rather than by omission.** `analyzeStackFrame` records *every* `[rsp + N]` access as a NON-parameter — the final `false` argument to its `record` — and `isArgumentSlot` is asked only of `bp`-based offsets. That is the same refusal `inUnfilledHomeSpace` and `peek-a-bin-g186` are made of: being inside an argument area does not make a slot an argument, and on the stack pointer there is not even a fixed area to be inside of.
  - **An entry-SP tracker must not be written in `signatures.ts`.** It would have to model the allocation *and* every push — a second stack grammar beside `stack.ts`'s, which is the tenth-hand-rolled-copy shape `peek-a-bin-w6f` ended for operand parsing and `pe/sections.ts`, `ripRelative.ts` and `stackIdiom.ts` each exist to prevent. If it is ever wanted it belongs in `stack.ts`, once, published on `frameDelta`'s model.
  - **There is no oracle for the answer it would give.** `corpus/arity.ts` measures CALL-SITE arity against `apitypes.ts` and can never see a declared parameter list; gcc accepts any list; and the one differential that now exists (`signatureAgreement`, below) compares this count against the decompiler's, which declares a fifth argument only from the *same* frame recovery that refuses `sp`-based slots — so it could not adjudicate a fifth parameter either.

  So a genuine fifth argument is now missing from the count, admitted rather than hidden: the `peek-a-bin-f51x` direction, and the direction the decompiler already errs in for the same slots. **REOPENING CONDITION**: an entry-SP displacement published by `stack.ts` (allocation and pushes), plus an instrument that can see a declared parameter count from outside the tool. Neither exists.

  **(b) The x86 count is read off the RECOVERED FRAME, not off `[ebp + N]` operand text.** The old scan counted every `[ebp + off]` with `off >= 8` **with no frame-pointer check at all** — `peek-a-bin-ikd`'s defect verbatim one module over, on exactly the population `decompile/structs.ts` keys `^arg_(\d+)$` to exclude: under FPO `mov ebp, ecx` makes EBP an object pointer and `[ebp + 0x10]` a struct field access, `mov ebp, edx` is how an MSVC funclet receives its parent's frame, and `xor ebp, ebp` makes it a constant. So the panel was asserting what the decompiler was carefully refusing. `framedParamCount` reads `stackFrame.vars` for `^arg_(\d+)$` and answers `max + 1`, which inherits `addressesOwnFrame`, `inlineFrameGeometry`, the helper-framed `__SEH_prolog4` prologue, the sub-slot refusal and the FPO refusal **in one declaration**. `max + 1` rather than the count of names, because an untouched argument leaves a gap in the numbering — `stack.ts`'s own rule — so a *trailing* untouched argument is invisible and the answer is a lower bound.

  **(c) `thiscall` was asserted from ECX WITHOUT EVER CONSULTING EDX.** The two conventions differ in exactly one fact — `__thiscall` passes `this` in ECX and everything else on the stack, `__fastcall` passes the first two integer arguments in ECX and EDX — so every 32-bit `__fastcall` helper, and MSVC's CRT is full of them, was labelled `thiscall` on the panel. `registerConvention32` records read-before-write per register over the same ten-instruction window and answers `fastcall` when both are read; **it no longer stops at the first ECX read**, which is what made EDX unaskable and which changes nothing about the ECX answer. **ECX is required for both**: `__fastcall` fills ECX first, so EDX alone is a garbage read and is refused rather than reported as a one-argument shape.

  **(d) A function with no instructions in range answered `{ convention: is64 ? "fastcall" : "cdecl", paramCount: 0 }`** — a complete-shaped answer invented from the optional header's magic over a function not one byte of which was read, which is the exact falsehood the essay above exists to prevent, one branch below it. It returns `null`. **On x86 `null` is now also the answer where none of the three kinds of evidence exists** (`ret N`, a register convention, a recovered argument slot): x86 has four conventions the tool can name and they are not interchangeable, so printing one is a claim about the interface, where x64's `fastcall` is a property of the architecture and states nothing. For zero recovered arguments `cdecl` and `stdcall` are ABI-identical, so the old answer was not falsifiable *about behaviour* — but it was still a claim on a screen, over a function whose frame was never recovered and whose arguments may all be in `[esp + N]` slots `stack.ts` deliberately declines to read.

  **Two consequences worth keeping in view rather than rediscovering.** `paramCount` on x86 counts STACK slots only, so a `this` in ECX is not added — a deliberate, stated under-count, because adding it would be a new arity claim with no oracle anywhere here to check it. And the ARCHITECTURE REFUSAL IS NOW UNOBSERVABLE AT AN EMPTY INSTRUCTION LIST, every architecture answering `null` there, so the two assertions that used to pin its ordering are **inert** in exactly the way `stack.test.ts` records for `analyzeStackFrame`; both files say so at the tests, and the differential that still discriminates is over a non-empty x86 body.

  **THE ORACLE LANDED FIRST (`peek-a-bin-02fa`'s standing rule) AND IS AS MUCH THE DELIVERABLE AS THE FIX.** `signatureAgreement` (`corpus/emitAudits.ts`) compares `A = inferSignature(...).paramCount`, recorded on `FuncRec.sigParams`, against `B =` the parameters on the emitted function's own signature line — read through `declaredParams`, which is now the ONE declaration of which emitted line that is, `sweep.ts`'s `emittedCallees` having carried a second, positional reading (`i < 6` plus a return-type shape) that a function with enough struct typedefs above its header walks past, at which point the function counts as a caller of itself. **`over` GATES at 0 on x64**; `under` and the whole x86 comparison are report-only; `withSignature` is the liveness half. Base (at `97ef927` plus the oracle, emitted C byte-identical to `base-0870e14`) → after, t32/t64/w64/w32:

  | row | base | after |
  |---|---|---|
  | `over` | 7 / 54 / 54 / 6 | **7 / 0 / 0 / 6** |
  | worst `over` | +4 / +258 / +258 / +4 | +4 / 0 / 0 / +4 |
  | `agree` | 170 / 217 / 213 / 168 | 192 / 271 / 267 / 188 |
  | `under` | 83 / 8 / 8 / 84 | 0 / 8 / 8 / 0 |
  | `withSignature` | 260 / 279 / 275 / 258 | 199 / 279 / 275 / 194 |

  The x86 `over` of 7/6 is unchanged and is the `ret N` count exceeding the touched argument slots, which is legitimate; the x86 `under` reaching 0 is **structural, not a finding** — since the fix both sides of the x86 comparison read `stack.ts`. The `withSignature` fall on the PE32 pair is the cost of (b) and (d): 61 t32 and 64 w32 functions that used to read `cdecl, 0 params` on the panel now show nothing. **`compare.mjs` flags that fall as a REGRESSION and is right to** — a liveness half falling is how this gate would go green by no longer looking, so the row must be adjudicated rather than absorbed; here it is the intended withdrawal, and the x64 pair, where the gate lives, does not move at 279/275.

  **CORPUS.** t32 and w32 **byte-identical** — the clean control, and it holds by construction: `signature` is read at exactly one place, `promote.ts:806`, gated on `is64`, so every x86 change here is panel-only. t64 and w64 differ in **36 and 38 functions and on the signature line only**, entirely withdrawn `int64_t argN` parameters. Every other gate unmoved (`arity over/under/exact`, `offsetNamedArgs` 0 everywhere, gcc clean, `offsetof`, and `paramClobberedAtEntry` still 0 with its liveness denominator falling 578 → 475 and 565 → 454 — the same withdrawal counted from the other side).

  **ADJUDICATED AGAINST `objdump -d -M intel`**, which is the only instrument that separates a right withdrawal from a wrong one (`peek-a-bin-emlv`, `peek-a-bin-g186`, `peek-a-bin-sx57` all say so):
  - `t64!sub_140002248`, 4 → **0**. `rex push rbx / sub rsp,0x20 / and dword [rsp+0x40],0 / lea r8,[rsp+0x40] / call …` — RCX, RDX, R8 and R9 are never read before being written, so it takes no register argument. And `[rsp+0x40]` is `E + 0x18` after the push and the allocation, i.e. **RDX's home slot used as scratch** — `peek-a-bin-g186`'s exact population. The old rule read it as argument 8.
  - `t64!sub_140001394`, 4 → **1**. `mov rsi,rcx` is the only argument-register read before a write (`lea edx,[rdi+0x2e]`, `xor r8d,r8d`, `mov r9d,0x400` all write). Its `[rsp+0x60]` is again `E + 0x18`.
  - `t64!sub_1400027C8`, 4 → **3**. `mov r13,r8 / mov rsi,rdx / mov rbx,rcx`, and R9 never appears. The operand that claimed "argument 6" was **`lea rbp,[rsp+0x30]` — the frame-pointer setup itself**.

  **FIVE NEGATIVE CONTROLS, FOUR DISCRIMINATING AND ONE INERT AND REPORTED.** Restoring `extraStackParams` turns the new gate **red** at 54/54 with worst +258 and returns the emitted C to base byte-for-byte. Returning the old shape for zero instructions reddens 2 rows. Dropping the EDX discrimination reddens 2 rows. Making `inferSignature` refuse everything drives `over` to 0 — *the gate itself is satisfied* — and is caught by `expected 0 to be greater than 100` on `withSignature` plus a second liveness row, which is the whole point of that floor. **THE INERT ONE IS THE FRAME-POINTER REQUIREMENT**: reinstating the operand-text scan reddens 2 unit rows and moves **not one number in the entire corpus report**, the x86 `sigAgree` rows included — so on these four binaries there is no function where the two readings of `[ebp + positive]` differ. That is `peek-a-bin-5zpo`/`peek-a-bin-cvri`'s "the FPO refusal is unfalsifiable on this corpus" measured from a new direction; it is reported rather than tuned away, and the unit fixture (`mov ebp, edx` and `xor ebp, ebp`, both named in `StackFrame.frameDelta`'s own docstring) is the whole instrument for the class. Note the third shape there, `mov ebp, ecx`, is deliberately **not** asserted null: reading ECX before writing it is genuine register-convention evidence, so the answer is `thiscall` with the parameter claim withdrawn, which is the property that matters.

  **NOT VERIFIED.** Nothing has looked at the panel in a browser (`peek-a-bin-v2u`), and the corpus is structurally silent on all three x86 changes.

- **`arg_N` in a stack frame means argument *position*, and only when the frame was verified.** `stack.ts` numbers a slot `arg_<index>` from its offset, but only after confirming a real `push rbp` / `mov rbp, rsp` prologue; otherwise the name is offset-based (`arg_0x10`). That distinction is load-bearing — under frame-pointer omission RBP is usually a callee-saved object pointer, so `[rbp+0x10]` is often a struct field access, and `structs.ts` keys struct provenance off `^arg_(\d+)$` precisely to exclude those. The name is the only channel between the two files (`IRParam` carries name and type and nothing else), so do not loosen it. On x64 the first slot is RCX's ABI home slot, which puts the fifth argument at `[rbp+0x30]`, past the shadow space. **The frame register's displacement from the entry stack pointer is published outright, as `StackFrame.frameDelta`** — the name channel is silent for a function that takes no arguments, and `promote.ts` needs the fact for those too. It uses it for one thing: `splitStaleReads` parks a register version in a variable named after it, so a frame slot can arrive at `promoteVars` addressed off `ebp_1` rather than off `ebp`, and `frameRegisterAliases` follows that copy **only when `frameDelta !== null`**. A frame pointer is invariant for the whole body, so every version of it denotes the same frame and the split changed nothing about the address — leaving the aliased sites unmatched did not preserve a distinction, it printed one slot under two spellings (`arg_0` at one site, `*(int32_t*)(ebp_1 + 8)` at another, 501 such slots across 114 x86 functions at `f685b6d`). With `frameDelta === null` the copy is *not* followed, and that is the whole safeguard: under frame-pointer omission two versions of RBP are two different objects, and `[rbp+0x10]` is a struct field access `structs.ts` must still get to see. The stack pointer gets none of this: it moves, so no version of it stands in for another (peek-a-bin-5zpo).

  **The gate was `StackFrame.framed`, i.e. `D === slotSize`, until `peek-a-bin-cvri`, and that boolean was answering two questions with one bit** — "is the frame register a frame pointer", which is what invariance needs, and "is the geometry canonical", which is what a positional *name* needs and which `peek-a-bin-sx57` had already replaced with `D`. A shifted frame (`lea rbp, [rsp + k]`, or `mov rbp, rsp` after N pushes — both ordinary MSVC output) satisfies the first and not the second, so it was refused for having its frame pointer in the wrong *place*. `framed` is gone; the quantity is the field, and each consumer asks its own question of it.

  **The evidence recorded here for gating on `framed` was itself the conflation.** This paragraph said "every one of the 185 `rbp_N`-based sites on t64/w64 is in an FPO function" (measured at `f685b6d`), and that reading of "FPO" was *not framed* — which lumped every shifted frame in with genuine frame-pointer omission. Re-measured at `99203fb` with the two separated: of **197** alias-spelled `[<fp>_N ± off]` occurrences on the x64 pair (114 t64, 83 w64), **195 are in functions whose displacement IS recovered** and 2 are not — and both of those are `rbp = rbp_5 - 1`, RBP used as a **loop counter** in a function that never addresses memory through it. So the FPO population contributes **0** memory accesses through a frame-register copy in this corpus, and the sentence that justified the gate was counting functions that have frame pointers.

  **Two consequences of that, and the second is the uncomfortable one.** Widening the gate to `frameDelta !== null` resolved **88 of t64's 114 and 59 of w64's 83** alias-spelled slot occurrences (13 arguments and 75/46 locals per binary) across 7 of 279 and 5 of 275 functions, with **t32 and w32 byte-identical** and emitted line counts unchanged on all four — it is a respelling, not a recovery. `t64!sub_1400080E0` is the shape: `arg_0` used at one site with the same slot spelled `*(int32_t*)(rbp_1 + 0x48)` at three others, and `arg_1` a **declared parameter with zero uses** while both reads of its slot went through the copy. And **the FPO refusal is unfalsifiable on this corpus**: removing it entirely leaves the emitted C **byte-identical on all four binaries** (md5 over every function). It is kept on the structural argument alone — it is the only thing between `promoteVars` and the struct-field reading of `[rbp + N]` in 259 t64 and 257 w64 FPO functions — so do not read a green corpus as evidence that it works, and do not remove it because nothing moved. `promote.test.ts` is the only place that exercises it (peek-a-bin-cvri). **The refusal is unfalsifiable here; the MECHANISM is not, and the difference is worth 1147 sites.** Disabling `frameRegisterAliases` outright at `d8d2d02` takes alias-spelled constant-offset frame derefs 0 → **1147/106/798/75** (t32/t64/w32/w64) over 58/11/56/9 functions and `var_NNN` occurrences 2225 → 1240 and 1774 → 1138 on the PE32 pair — including `t32!sub_4045B1` 220 → 48 `var_NNN` with 172 alias derefs, which is `peek-a-bin-0fik` reproduced at its filed magnitude (that bead recorded −144 on the same function and t32 1858 → 1418 corpus-wide). 0fik prescribed exactly this pass and is closed by `d024ed5` + `b6a44ce`; the control is how the attribution was established, rather than archaeology across the twenty commits of detection movement since.

  **The invariance is a claim about the whole body, and `stack.ts` only reads the prologue**, so it was checked rather than assumed, and **re-derived at `84eed6e` figure for figure** against the `99203fb` measurement although detection moved 288/285 → 260/258 underneath it: over every function with a recovered displacement on all four binaries there are **exactly two mid-body repurposings**, `t32!sub_40A810` 0x40a851 and `w32!sub_4092B0` 0x4092f1 (`mov ebp, dword ptr [eax + 0x10]`, `longjmp` reloading EBP out of a `jmp_buf`), both **canonical** — i.e. already inside this pass's reach before cvri widened anything — with the framed populations 204/20/18/201 (t32/t64/w64/w32), 203/2/2/200 of them canonical, and the shifted-frame population contributing **0**. **The latency condition is stronger than "no `ebp_N` site", and the alias is not what would make it live**: `matchStackAccess` resolves a **bare** `ebp` deref with no program-point awareness, so what matters is *any* `[ebp ± N]` operand after the reload — measured **0** in both, against 1 each before it (`push dword ptr [ebp + 8]`, which is correct).

  **THE REPAIR THE BEAD PROPOSES IS REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT AND MUST NOT BE RE-ATTEMPTED AT THAT LAYER** (adjudicated at `84eed6e`, both wrong versions executed rather than reasoned about). Asking `inlineFrameGeometry` whether the frame register survives to the returns cannot work, because **`stack.ts` has no CFG and can only ask in ADDRESS order, while MSVC lays a mid-function epilogue before code that executes earlier**. `fpSurvivesToReturn`'s own semantics — any post-establish write invalidates — refuse the frame of every function with an epilogue: `declared params scanned` **430 → 0 on t32 and 415 → 0 on w32** (578 → 557, 565 → 544 on x64), only 56/260 and 58/258 functions of emitted C unchanged, and `offsetof` t32 **299/52 → 319/57 fields/definitions** as EBP stops being excluded from struct bases and shapes are *fabricated*. The bead's weaker form — "no load of `<fp>` before the last read of `[<fp> + N]`" — refuses 59 functions and **1123** `[<fp>]` operands (379/200/165/379) and **does not refuse either target function**, whose one `[ebp + 8]` operand precedes every load. **And `frameDelta` is the wrong lever even where it fires**: nulling it for the two functions alone costs exactly one correct `arg_0` each (`arg_0` → `*(int32_t*)(ebp + 8)`, the parameter withdrawn from the signature — verified against `objdump`, EBP is still this frame's at 0x40a820) and still leaves the hazard open, because `analyzeStackFrame` records a `[<fp> − N]` local with **no `addressesOwnFrame` gate** and `matchStackAccess` resolves a bare frame-register deref — with `frameDelta` nulled on `t32!sub_4026E8` the bare-register sites still print `var_8 = ecx;` and only the `ebp_1`-spelled ones fall back to derefs. Closing the class needs a program-point rule inside `promote.ts`, which has no per-expression address, for **0** measured benefit. **Every gate in `npm run corpus` stays green through both wrong versions**; only `declared params scanned` and `offsetof`'s denominators move, and `compare.mjs` flags them — read those rows, not the exit code. For `stackDerivedBases` the stale premise costs *recovery* rather than correctness (a field access reading as byte arithmetic), which is the benign side of that pass's own trade (peek-a-bin-633s).

  **AND THE HAND AUDIT IS NOW A GATED ROW, so do not re-do it by hand.** `633s` left the hole open as *latent* and the latency was checked by somebody remembering to look — three times (`99203fb`, `fe244dc`, `84eed6e`). `corpus/frameRepurpose.ts` is that check: **`frame ops after repurpose`, 0 of 2792/427/355/2300 frame-relative operands, GATED at 0**, with `framed` **204/20/18/201** and `repurposings` **1/0/0/1** beside it as liveness. The oracle is the instruction stream and the operand text, never `stack.ts`'s answer about survival — which does not exist, and whose absence is the defect. **The write is CLASSIFIED, not merely found**, which is the address-order trap above in the audit's own terms: `pop <fp>`, `leave` and `popa`/`popad` are epilogue restores and open no window, and a control classifying them as repurposings exits 1 at **264/81/79/264 operands over 168/18/16/167 functions**. **The x64 pair's zero is STRUCTURAL** — no repurposing there at all — so only the PE32 pair demonstrates anything, and the report says so. Positive-controlled by reversing the address comparison, which names exactly `t32!sub_40A810` 0x40a820 and `w32!sub_4092B0` 0x4092c0, the "1 each before it" `633s` recorded. The gate has never fired and three of the classifier's four arms have no occurrence here, so the discrimination is controlled in `build/frameRepurposeAudit.test.ts` instead (peek-a-bin-nhw0).
- **…and the copy is only recognisable while the *tie-back* survives, which is not always: DCE deletes it, and the frame register's own definition is then indistinguishable from a copy of the stack pointer.** `swapDefWithCopy` writes the prologue as `ebp_1 = esp; ebp = ebp_1;`, so `frameRegisterAliases`' two shapes — a copy forward, and `<fp> = v` — need the second statement, because the first one's source is the **stack** pointer and says nothing on its own. Where every read of the frame register was rewritten to the copy nothing reads `ebp` any more, DCE takes `ebp = ebp_1`, and the body is left with `ebp_1 = esp;` alone — which must be refused (CLAUDE.md's standing rule: no read of RSP may be reinterpreted at another program point). **27 frame-slot derefs per PE32 binary over 7 functions each**, at `d8d2d02`, every one of them a slot the function *declares*: `t32!sub_40667A` declared `var_4`, `var_8`, `var_18`, `var_1A`, `var_4C` and used **none** of them while spelling all five as `*(int32_t*)(ebp_1 - N)`, and `t32!sub_401884` declared `arg_0` and `arg_1` with zero uses while its three reads of `[ebp + 8]` and one of `[ebp + 0xC]` went through the copy. **Shape 3** is `stack.ts` publishing **`StackFrame.frameEstablishedAt`** — the address of the instruction that set `D` — and `frameRegisterAliases` seeding its fixpoint with any `assign` at that address whose destination is a variable. Five things (`peek-a-bin-xb2f`):
  - **It is not a relaxation of shape 2, it is the same fact from a STRONGER witness.** A `mov`/`lea` into a register lifts to exactly one statement, and `swapDefWithCopy` is the only pass that swaps such a statement's destination for a variable while keeping its address — every other var-destination copy this tree inserts (`splitStaleReads`' own, at a block top or the entry) carries **no** `addr` at all, and the two passes that do carry one (`spoiledCompareCapture` at a compare, `liftCrossBlockPops` at a pop) write a different address and, for the pop, a register destination. So the variable holds the value the frame register was established *with*, at the point it was established. Shape 2's `<fp> = v` is weaker: it would also admit a var holding **version 0** of the frame register, which under a frame-pointer prologue is the *caller's* frame pointer and a different object; that has 0 occurrences here only because `pop`/`leave` are unlifted.
  - **It keys on the ADDRESS, never on the variable's name, and never on the source.** `splitStaleReads` names its variable after the register, so `ebp_1` looks like the answer and is a *spelling*; keying on it would make the alias set a function of a naming convention rather than of dataflow, which the bead explicitly forbade. Not examining the source is what makes the shifted `lea rbp, [rax - 0x488]` form work — `t64!sub_140005980`'s own prologue, where RAX itself holds the entry stack pointer and no test of the source expression would recognise it. Measured: shape 3 fires on **57/13/56/12** functions (t32/t64/w32/w64), of which **9 on t64 and 8 on w64 have a `binary` source**, so the source-blind half is exercised on the real `lea` shape rather than argued.
  - **The FPO refusal is untouched and unreachable through it.** `frameEstablishedAt` is null in exactly the cases `frameDelta` is — one walk sets both — plus the helper-framed prologue, where the establishing `lea` is inside `__SEH_prolog4` and this function's stream holds no statement at that address to match. A caller-side address there would be an address that matches something else, so `frameGeometry`'s helper arm reports null deliberately. `promote.test.ts` pins the pairing anyway, as defence in depth.
  - **The x64 pair is the byte-identical control, and it is a control that was REACHED rather than skipped.** Shape 3 admits `rbp_1` in 13 and 12 functions there, including all four whose only unresolved sites were `[rbp_1 + 0x470]` and `[rbp_1 + 0x690]` — offsets *below* the return address in a shifted frame, which `stack.ts` records as neither a parameter nor a local, so the alias promoted nothing and the emitted C did not move. That is `peek-a-bin-ikd`'s refusal working, not this rule failing.
  - **It is a respelling, not a recovery, and the emitted line count says so.** Measured at base `d8d2d02`, both sides pinned: alias-spelled constant-offset frame derefs **27 → 0 on both PE32 binaries** (t64/w64 unmoved at 18/16, all of them the ikd population above), `var_NNN` occurrences 2213 → 2225 and 1762 → 1774, `arg_<N>` occurrences 1118 → 1133 and 1086 → 1101 — +12 locals and +15 arguments per binary, exactly the 27 sites — with **emitted line counts identical on all four** (16572/17140/15101/15472) and 7 of 268 and 7 of 266 functions changed. **`compare.mjs` reports NOT ONE MOVED ROW**: verdict "no regression", `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0` on all four, t64 and w64 **byte-identical throughout**, gcc 268/279/275/266 clean, polarity anchor-A 517/574/497/441 all correct with the audited set flat at 919/824/821/729, `offsetof` **346/301/303/354 fields over 55/54/53/59 definitions with the whole `offsetof/` artifact tree byte-identical** — so no struct shape was gained, lost, renumbered or fabricated — invented prelude declarations unmoved at 1495/2506/1451/2328, loop shapes `for` 6/6/6/6 and `while` 111/106/99/98 and `do/while` 78/77/76/77 and `if` 1987/1938/1719/1759, and 0 on staleReads, staleGuards, popReads, lostDefs, armExits, wildBranches, unencodableNames, offsetNamedArgs, paramClobberedAtEntry, arity over, callees lost, loops short, statements dropped and throws, with unrecovered values 36/12/11/33. Every diff was read: each is one unpromoted deref becoming the slot's declared name, plus three locals whose declaration goes `int32_t` → `uint32_t` because `inferVarTypes` re-derives a type from the access width and these slots are now *accessed* — the documented behaviour that any local actually read comes out unsigned.
  - **Every refusal is negative-controlled and each control fails only its own test.** Dropping the address test — "follow any copy of the stack pointer" — fails exactly the two tests that forbid it; dropping the `frameDelta` gate fails exactly the two FPO tests; and disabling shape 3 fails exactly the five tests that assert it. **No corpus gate can see any of this**: the base output was true, merely less readable, so gcc compiles it, polarity judges guards that exist, and `staleReads`/`lostDefs` each check that a definition reaches a read, which it did. The instrument is the one `peek-a-bin-xb2f` prescribed — *sites whose slot the function declares and which nevertheless did not resolve* — and it is 0 on all four now. It is a **target and not a gate**: the raw "alias slot sites remaining" row falls further for the *wrong* change, since removing the FPO refusal entirely also drives it down (`peek-a-bin-cvri`).

- **…and `isParam` is decided by the frame register's displacement from the stack pointer on ENTRY, not by whether the prologue was canonical — a `[<fp> + N]` in an unverified frame was recorded as a parameter regardless, and only the *name* was withheld.** `analyzeStackFrame` compared the offset against a fixed `is64 ? 0x10 : 0x8`, which is the canonical geometry's answer, so in a frame-pointer-omitted function every `[rbp + N]` at or above it became a parameter called `arg_0x<N>`. That put a phantom argument in the emitted signature — `int sub_1400058F4(int64_t arg_0x10, int64_t arg_0x11D, int64_t arg0)` for a function whose RBP is `mov rbp, rcx`, i.e. argument 0's *object* — and, where the body did match, wrote to one: `arg_0x1A20 = rax` is `t64!sub_14000D8C4` storing its GS cookie into a local, in C that says the cookie is an incoming argument. `inlineFrameDisplacement` (`stack.ts`) computes `D = E - V`, where `E` is the stack pointer on entry and `V` what the function puts in the frame register, and the parameter threshold is `D + slot` — written inline at its three readers in `stack.ts`, not as a named constant. Five things (peek-a-bin-ikd):
  - **`D` is the sound quantity and a yes/no is not.** `E` is where the return address is, so argument 0 is at `[E + slot]` and `[<fp> + off]` is argument `(off - D - slot) / slot`; an `off` below `D + slot` is *below the return address*, hence a local. Because `D` is measured from entry it does not matter **what** was pushed before the frame was established — `push rbx; mov rbp, rsp` is `D = slot` and `[rbp + 2*slot]` really is argument 0 there too, which is why the old strict `push <fp>` pattern-match was refusing correct frames. It is a small abstract interpretation of the prologue over `<sp> - E` plus the registers standing in for an earlier value of it, since MSVC's large-frame shape is `mov rax, rsp` … `lea rbp, [rax - N]`.
  - **A refusal is total: nothing is recorded and the deref is left alone**, exactly as `[<fp> + N]` below the threshold always was. Renaming it to a local would claim a stack slot as falsely as calling it an argument did, and leaving it is what lets struct synthesis see an object field for what it is.
  - **THE BEAD'S OWN SUGGESTED FIX IS REFUTED BY THE CORPUS AND MUST NOT BE RE-TRIED.** It proposed gating `isParam` on the existing `framed`. Measured as a control at `97249dc`: that takes `offsetNamedArgs` to 0, which *looks* better, while changing **6 more t64 functions than this fix** — `sub_140001150`, `sub_1400038B8`, `sub_14000794C`, `sub_1400080E0`, `sub_14000B094`, `sub_14000EE7C`, every one a `push*; mov rbp, rsp` frame whose `[rbp + N]` are genuine incoming arguments. `sub_1400080E0` spills ECX with `mov [rsp + 8], ecx` at entry and reads it back at `mov ecx, [rbp + 0x48]` with **no store to that slot anywhere**, and `D = 0x40` makes `[rbp + 0x48]` exactly `[E + 8]`; the control demotes it to `*(int32_t*)(rbp + 0x48)`. **94 of t64's 147 offset-named slots are that shape.**
  - **`framed` stayed `D === slot` deliberately, so ikd added no new `arg_<index>` name and left `structs.ts`'s cross-function parameter provenance untouched at the 83 remaining sites. `peek-a-bin-sx57` is where those were taken up — read its bullet below before touching the naming.** Exactly one function per x64 binary *enters* the framed set (`t64!sub_140006804`, `w64!sub_1400069EC`), because its displacement turned out to be `slot` all along; verified from outside the arithmetic — the machine does `mov WORD PTR [rsp + 8], cx` at entry and `movzx edx, WORD PTR [rbp + 0x10]` later, so `(uint16_t)arg_0` names the argument the ABI passed in CX.
  - **The predicted `offsetof` rise did NOT happen, and the reason is worth knowing**: fields and definitions are **unmoved on all four binaries** (346/301/303/354 and 55/54/53/59). Struct synthesis was never blocked — `matchStackAccess` only fires on a deref spelled literally `<fp> ± const`, and by `promoteVars` copy propagation has already rewritten `rbp` to the register it was copied from at every site in the object-pointer class, so `t64!sub_1400045DC` was **already** emitting `((struct_12 *)r9)->field_0x18` for all six offsets. The phantom lived in the parameter list alone. Where the base *did* promote a body site, the frame register was a real (shifted) frame pointer and `stackDerivedBases` refuses it a struct, so no bogus shape appeared either.

  **Three tests pinned the defect as the rule** and are replaced: `it("does not number slots of a function with no frame-pointer prologue")` asserted the two phantom names as expected output; `it("does not number slots when a register is pushed before the frame pointer")` asserted `arg_0x8` for what its own arithmetic makes the **return address**; and `it("does not mistake a push of another register for the frame-pointer push")` asserted `arg_0x8` where `push esi; mov ebp, esp` genuinely puts argument 0 at `[ebp + 8]` — a claim about a byte pattern, not about the machine. Negative-controlled: forcing `inlineFrameDisplacement` to null takes t64's 83 to 0, so the survivors are kept *because* a displacement was recovered.
- **…and `promote.ts` no longer re-derives that threshold, because it never had the `D` to derive it from.** `matchStackAccess` decided "is `[<fp> + off]` a parameter" from a hard-coded `is64 ? 0x10 : 0x8` — which is `D === slotSize`'s answer — while `stack.ts` decided the same thing from the recovered displacement. The constant is gone: a positive `bp:` key is resolved in `paramLookup` and a negative or `sp:` one in `varLookup`, with **no threshold anywhere in `promote.ts`**, so the argument-area judgement exists in one file (peek-a-bin-s7hl). Four things:
  - **Removing the threshold is not a widening, and the reason is a partition rather than a measurement.** `analyzeStackFrame` writes a positive `bp:` key *only* from its argument-slot branch, and every other shape it records is negative or `sp:`-based, so a positive key is in `paramLookup` or in neither map. Consulting one map is therefore the same answer as consulting both, and a slot resolves *only* because `stack.ts` recorded it — which is what keeps `peek-a-bin-ikd`'s and `peek-a-bin-g186`'s withdrawn phantom parameters withdrawn. `StackAccess.aboveFrame` is the renamed field and asks that structural question; do not make it consult both maps or route on the name, and `promote.test.ts` pins the direction with a local wrongly parked at a positive key.
  - **The disagreement was reachable in honest code, at any `D < slotSize`.** The two arithmetics agree for every `D >= slotSize`, which is every canonical and every push-shifted MSVC prologue, so nothing was ever observed — but `mov ebp, esp` with no push ahead of it gives `D = 0` and puts argument 0 at `[ebp + 4]`, which `stack.ts` records and declares while every read of it stayed an unpromoted `*(int32_t*)(ebp + 4)`. A mismatch is a *miss*, never a wrong name, which is why it was silent.
  - **`D < 0` is now refused outright, and that is the second half.** `addressesOwnFrame` (`stack.ts`) is the one predicate, with `isArgumentSlot` and `argSlotName` as its two callers. `D = E - V`, so `D < 0` means the frame register points **above** this function's entry stack pointer — at storage the *caller* owns — and there is one way for that: the function is establishing somebody else's frame. `__SEH_prolog4` is that function and this corpus detects it as one twice (`t32!sub_404170`, `w32!sub_4043D0`, `D = -8`), where the threshold `off >= D + slot` is `off >= -4` and would record **every** `[ebp + off]` as a parameter — `[ebp + 4]`, the caller's return address, as `arg_2`. It is the record `promote.ts` now follows unconditionally, so it had to be honest at the one geometry where the two files used to differ. `frameDelta` itself is deliberately *not* nulled: it also answers "is the frame register a frame pointer", and `__SEH_prolog4`'s EBP is stack-derived and invariant, so `frameRegisterAliases` must still follow a copy of it — conflating the two is the defect the boolean `framed` was.
  - **HYGIENE, NOT A MEASURED FIX, and say so.** Both halves are **0 occurrences** at `cc45263`: over all four binaries, **0** of the 430/21/21/415 recorded parameter slots sit below the canonical threshold (so `promote.ts`'s band is empty), and the two `D < 0` functions contain **no `[<fp> + N]` operand at all** (so the refusal is never consulted — their recorded vars are `var_4/var_8/var_10/var_10_sp/var_18`). Emitted C is **byte-identical on all four binaries** (md5 over `funcs_*.jsonl`), `compare.mjs` `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0` over 919/824/729/821 guards with verdict "no regression", the compiled-and-run `offsetof` artifacts byte-identical (`diff -r`), `paramClobberedAtEntry` and `offsetNamedArgs` 0 with `declared params scanned` unmoved at 430/578/565/415, and every other row identical — the two reports differ only in their label. Both halves are negative-controlled by their own test, and each control fails only its own test.

- **…and the prologue may be in another function: MSVC delegates it to `__SEH_prolog4`.** A
  function that opens `push <framesize> / push <scopetable> / call __SEH_prolog4` has a perfectly
  ordinary EBP frame — the helper does `mov [esp+0x10], ebp` / `lea ebp, [esp+0x10]`, which lands
  EBP on the slot holding the caller's saved EBP exactly as `push ebp / mov ebp, esp` does, so
  `[ebp+8]` is argument 0 and `[ebp-0x1c]` is a local. `hasFramePointerPrologue` only looked at
  the function's own first two instructions, so **296 genuine argument accesses corpus-wide were
  named `arg_0x8` instead of `arg_0`** (156 over 32 functions on t32, 140 over 29 on w32 at
  `41113c9`), and — because `structs.ts` keys parameter provenance off `^arg_(\d+)$`, deliberately
  — those slots were **invisible to `findOrCreateLinked`** as well as mis-named. Five things
  (`peek-a-bin-emlv`):
  - **The rule is stack ARITHMETIC, not a byte pattern, and that is the whole soundness argument.**
    `push imm / push imm32 / call` is what an ordinary two-argument call looks like. Let `E` be the
    caller's stack pointer on entry; the canonical prologue leaves `<fp> = E - slot`, which is what
    makes `[<fp> + 2 * slot]` argument 0. The offset of the stack pointer from `E`
    is known all the way into the callee — one slot per caller push, one for the return address,
    one per helper push — so `lea <fp>, [<sp> + N]` establishes the *caller's* frame precisely when
    `N + delta === -slot`. `__SEH_prolog4` satisfies it at `16 + (-5 * 4) === -4` and an ordinary
    callee does not. **Corroborated from outside the rule**: t32 has exactly 31 `call 0x404170`
    sites and the rule marks exactly 31 functions framed; w32, 29 and 29.
  - **Three refusals carry it and none is decoration.** The caller may only `push` immediates
    before the `call` — that is what keeps the search off ordinary calls rather than trusting the
    arithmetic to decline them. The helper may not `push <fp>`, because that is a callee-saved save
    with a `pop <fp>` to come and **the arithmetic alone does not refuse it**
    (`push ebp / lea ebp, [esp+4]` satisfies `N + delta === -slot`). And `<fp>` must survive to the
    helper's `ret`, refusing on a branch, a nested call, `leave`, or any write naming `<fp>` —
    including when the window ends before a `ret` was seen.
  - **It is NOT the shape `peek-a-bin-ikd` warns about, and x64 is measurably untouched.** ikd's
    x64 population is an in-function `lea rbp, [rsp - N]` after several pushes, where `[rbp + N]` is
    a *local* in a large frame and gating on `framed` is right by accident; requiring a `call`
    excludes it and the arithmetic would refuse it anyway. Measured: the rule fires **0 times** on
    both x64 binaries, whose emitted C is byte-identical.
  - **It also recovers a displacement for functions with no argument slot at all** — 7 per 32-bit
    binary, which is exactly the case `StackFrame.frameDelta`'s docstring exists for (it was
    `framed` when this was measured) — and their emitted C is **byte-identical**, so
    `frameRegisterAliases` found nothing to follow in them.
  - **Measured at base `41113c9`, both sides pinned.** Aligned `arg_0x*` occurrences **156 → 16 on
    t32 and 140 → 14 on w32**, over 32 → 8 and 29 → 7 functions; t64/w64 unmoved at 147/141.
    Exactly 24 of 288 and 22 of 285 functions are a **pure rename** (verified by normalising the
    two names and diffing), plus 3 per binary that are struct re-identifications — see the
    `offsetof` delta in the Verification section. `compare.mjs` verdict **no regression**, with
    `CHANGED 5 / only-base 0 / only-change 0` per 32-bit binary, all ten an `arg_0xN → arg_N`
    rename at the same jcc with the same operator, verdict and anchor, and `polarity guards
    audited` **exactly flat** at 497/428. Every other gate flat.
  - **The residue is out of reach here and is not a shortfall.** All 15 remaining functions
    (8 t32, 7 w32) have their prologue *outside* the detected range — each begins by *reading* an
    argument, which no real entry point does — so EBP is the ENCLOSING function's frame pointer and
    no index can be derived. That is PE32 detection over-production (`peek-a-bin-abv`), it should
    shrink if detection improves, and a prologue fix cannot reach it.
- **…and the ARGUMENT INDEX follows the recovered displacement, not the canonical geometry — but on x64 an index is not enough to name a slot, because the ABI hands the first four to the callee as scratch.** `analyzeStackFrame` spelled `arg_<index>` only when `D === slot`, so every other displacement MSVC emits left its genuine arguments spelled `arg_0x<offset>`: **83 occurrences over 9 of 279 t64 functions and 83 over 9 of 275 w64 at `f169c00`**, 0 on both PE32 binaries. The index is `(off - D - slot) / slot` and `D` was already computed, so the arithmetic was there; what was missing was the second question. Result at `f169c00`: **83 → 49 occurrences, 9 → 8 functions, 12 → 9 distinct names on both x64 binaries, t32/w32 byte-identical**, with `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0` on all four and every gate flat — `offsetof` fields *and* distinct definitions unmoved at 346/301/303/354 and 55/54/53/59, and every struct declaration in the corpus byte-identical. Six things (`peek-a-bin-sx57`):
  - **THE HOME SPACE IS THE WHOLE JUDGEMENT, AND NAMING ALL 83 IS THE WRONG ANSWER.** The Microsoft x64 ABI has the *caller* reserve four slots for the arguments arriving in RCX/RDX/R8/R9 and then gives them to the callee as scratch, so `[E + slot]` … `[E + 4*slot]` holds argument N only if the callee spilled that register there itself. All 35 slots were read against `objdump -d -M intel` and **15 are not arguments**: `sub_1400027C8` saves rbx/rsi/rdi/r12 into arguments 0-3's home slots and restores them at the epilogue, `sub_14000BEE8` and `sub_14000C24C` do the same with rbx/rsi/rdi, and 5 more are byte locals or out-param buffers (`sub_140001150` zeroes `[rbp+0x38]` and hands its address to a callee, then reads it 12 times). `ARG_AREA.homeRegs` is the geometry — `homeRegs[N]` is which register carries argument N, and its length is how many leading slots are scratch — and it is **empty on x86**, where every argument is pushed by the caller, which is why both PE32 binaries are the byte-identical control.
  - **The discriminator is a spill of the argument's OWN register, and `argsPristine` is why it needs no liveness analysis.** `inlineFrameGeometry` returns `homed` beside `delta`: an index whose home slot the prologue stores `homeRegs[N]` into, at any width (`mov word ptr [rsp+8], cx` counts — `sub_14000EE7C` spills R9's low half and reads it back with `movzx`). The store counts only while **no instruction has yet written any register but the stack pointer and the frame register**, so every argument register provably still holds its incoming value. Blunt on purpose: every spill in this corpus is in the entry store block before any register write, so it costs nothing measured, and the alternative — tracking argument-register liveness — needs a second register-write grammar that has to be right about `cdq`, `mul` and `xchg`. `mov [rsp+8], rbx` is the same instruction shape stating the opposite fact and is refused by the register test alone.
  - **Getting the home space wrong is not cosmetic, because `paramIndexByBase` lets a home slot's `arg_<N>` DISPLACE the argument register's provenance claim** (`byBase.delete(heldByRegister)`). So a saved register named `arg_0` would be linked to whatever the callers pass as argument 0, and `structs.ts`' own docstring argued the displacement was safe because "`[rbp+0x10]` … can only be argument 0's storage; that is the ABI" — which is the wrong way round, and is corrected in place. Index ≥ 4 links to nothing at all (`collectArgs64` stops at four registers), which is why 15 of the 20 names are provenance-free by construction.
  - **NO GATE CAN SEE THE DIFFERENCE, and that is measured rather than argued.** A variant with the home-space refusal removed names all 35, takes `offsetNamedArgs` to **0/0/0/0** — the flattering number — and moves **nothing else in the report**: `offsetof` 346/301/303/354 fields and 55/54/53/59 definitions unchanged, ratio 1.00, `CHANGED 0`, verdict "no regression". It prints `void sub_1400027C8(int64_t arg_0, int64_t arg_1, int64_t arg_2, int64_t arg_3, …) { arg_0 = rbx; arg_1 = rsi; arg_2 = rdi; arg_3 = r12; }` and `int sub_140001150(int64_t arg_1, …) { arg_1 = 0; sub_140003BF8(arg_1, 0, 2); … }`. This is CLAUDE.md's standing warning made concrete — **an `offsetof` ratio of 1.00 cannot see a wrong parameter identity, only a self-consistent declaration** — so the only instrument that separated the two variants was reading the emitted C against `objdump`.
  - **The one existing name it WITHDRAWS is a false one.** `t64!sub_140006804` / `w64!sub_1400069EC` was the only x64 function with positional stack slots (2 of them). `arg_0` is a genuine `mov word ptr [rsp+8], cx` spill and is kept; `arg_2` at `[rbp+0x20]` has no spill anywhere and its first access is `lea rax, [rbp+0x20]` handed to a callee, then `movzx eax, WORD PTR [rbp+0x20]` reading what the callee wrote — an out-param local, now `arg_0x20`. That is why the audit *rose* by 2 on those two functions while falling by 36 elsewhere.
  - **`framed` no longer takes part in naming, and `arg_<N>` now means something weaker than it did.** It means "`D` was recovered", i.e. the frame register is derived from the entry stack pointer — still sufficient for `structs.ts`' `stackDerivedBases`, which it *widens*: the name channel used to be silent for every non-canonical prologue, so a `lea`-established frame was excluded by the surviving assignment or not at all. `StackFrame.framed` stayed `D === slot` for its one remaining consumer, `promote.ts`'s `frameRegisterAliases`, which asks a different question (is the frame register invariant, so may a split copy of it be followed); `peek-a-bin-cvri` closed that gap by publishing `D` as `StackFrame.frameDelta` and deleting the boolean — see the stack-frame gotcha. The 16 non-arguments it left emitted as *parameters* were `isParam`'s question and not this one; `peek-a-bin-g186` closed them, see the next bullet. **Eight tests pinned the old rule and are replaced**, two of them stating the correct answer in prose while asserting the wrong one: `it("records no parameter for the return address of a shifted frame")` asserted `arg_0xC` for a slot its own comment calls "the first argument", and `it("maps an x64 home slot to the index of the register that owns it")` justified itself with "a spilled argument" over a fixture that spilled nothing. `it("does not number slots of a frame established with lea")` was the old rule as a title, on a fabricated shape; it is now the real `sub_1400027C8` geometry asserting `arg_4`.
- **…and being inside the argument area is not being an argument: on x64 the home space is four slots the CALLER reserves and the CALLEE may spend on anything.** `peek-a-bin-sx57` stopped *naming* an unfilled home slot positionally and left `isParam` alone, so all 16 per x64 binary were still recorded as parameters and reached the emitted signature under their offset name — `void sub_1400027C8(int64_t arg_0x30, int64_t arg_0x38, int64_t arg_0x40, int64_t arg_0x48, …) { arg_0x30 = rbx; arg_0x38 = rsi; … }`, four declared arguments that a callee-saved register save overwrites at entry, and `int sub_140001150(int64_t arg_0x38, …) { arg_0x38 = 0; sub_140002100(rbp + 0x38, …); }`, where the *address* of the "argument" is spelled off `rbp` two lines under the parameter that names the same bytes. `inUnfilledHomeSpace` (`stack.ts`) is the one declaration of the rule and `analyzeStackFrame`'s `isArgumentSlot` and `argSlotName` are its two callers — splitting it between them is exactly how the slot came to be correctly *named* and wrongly *recorded*. Seven things (`peek-a-bin-g186`):
  - **The evidence was already computed.** `homed` — `inlineFrameGeometry`'s second answer, the indices whose home slot the prologue fills with `homeRegs[N]` itself while `argsPristine` holds — is precisely "this home slot holds its own argument", so no new grammar was written and no new measurement supports the discriminator that did not already support sx57's.
  - **It is a WITHDRAWAL, not a re-labelling, and the alternative does not work.** The slot is not a local either — the caller owns the storage — and `promote.ts` resolves a positive `bp:` key in `paramLookup` and nowhere else, so a `var_` name recorded here would land in `varLookup` while every site looks in `paramLookup`: a declared local with no site to be promoted at. (`matchStackAccess` used to reach that outcome by re-deriving the threshold itself, from a hard-coded `is64 ? 0x10 : 0x8`; `peek-a-bin-s7hl` removed the constant, and the conclusion is unchanged because the two maps partition the key space.) Not recording is the same answer `stack.ts` has always given an offset *below* the argument area, and it leaves a plain deref.
  - **It cannot withdraw a positional name, by construction rather than by measurement.** Everything the predicate answers true for is exactly what `argSlotName`'s home-space fallback already spelled by offset, so no slot that reaches `arg_<N>` is reachable by it — sx57's six spilled home slots (`sub_1400080E0` arg_0/arg_1, `sub_14000B094` arg_0/arg_2, `sub_14000EE7C` arg_3, `sub_140006804` arg_0) keep their indices as a property. `argSlotName`'s home-space arm is now unreachable and kept as defence in depth, exactly as its `delta === null` arm already was.
  - **Containment, not slot alignment.** A byte inside a home slot the callee never filled is not part of an argument either, so the predicate tests the interval rather than divisibility; `argSlotName` still needs exact alignment to *spell* an index, which is the separate question. 0 such offsets occur in the corpus — the two readings are measurably indistinguishable here — so it is pinned by two tests rather than measured, in both directions (a sub-slot byte of a *filled* home slot is still `arg_0x12`).
  - **`offsetNamedArgs` reaching 0/0/0/0 is a TARGET AND NOT THE RESULT, and CLAUDE.md already records the wrong change that also gets there.** sx57 measured a variant naming all 35 slots: it takes the same row to 0 and moves nothing else in the report, while printing four declared parameters immediately overwritten. So do not read the row. The instrument that separates the two is the *declared* parameter list read against `objdump`: 16 slots over 8 functions per x64 binary, all 16 read by hand, going to 0 declared `arg_0x*` parameters — against the naming variant's 0 in the row and 35 declared. The row's own docstring in `corpus/emitAudits.ts` still describes the x64 population as live and should be re-read by whoever next touches that file.
  - **The 8 functions now depend on `stackDerivedBases`' ASSIGNMENT channel alone, and the risk is named rather than merely absent.** Withdrawing every stack parameter from `sub_1400027C8` silences the `^arg_(\d+)$` name channel there, so what keeps its four withdrawn slots from becoming a fabricated 4-field struct on the frame register is only the surviving `rbp_1 = rsp + 0x30` / `rbp = rbp_1`. All 8 carry such an assignment (verified in the artifacts, one per function), and the whole corpus's `offsetof` artifacts are **byte-identical** — not one struct gained, lost or renumbered. Negative-controlled: withholding the assignment channel for the frame register moves `offsetof` to **311/56 and 313/55** on the x64 pair *identically before and after this change*, and none of the 8 casts the frame pointer to a struct even then — so the withdrawn slots contribute nothing to that delta. A future change that folds `mov rbp, rsp` away would nevertheless fabricate those shapes, and `offsetof`'s ratio would stay 1.00 through it (CLAUDE.md's standing warning: 1.00 sees a self-consistent declaration, never a wrong identity).
  - **The helper path gets stricter for free.** `frameGeometry`'s `__SEH_prolog4` arm reports `homed: NO_HOMED`, so on x64 a helper-framed function's home slots now yield no parameter rather than an offset-named one. It is the same conservatism one storey up, it fires 0 times on both x64 binaries, and it is pinned rather than measured.

  Measured at base `16f1633`, both sides pinned: **`offsetNamedArgs` 0/49/49/0 → 0/0/0/0** over 0/8/8/0 → 0/0/0/0 functions and 0/9/9/0 → 0 distinct names; **t32 and w32 byte-identical** (`homeRegs` is `[]` on x86, so the predicate is false before any arithmetic — both PE32 binaries are the untouched control); emitted C changed in exactly **8 of 279 and 8 of 275** functions, all 16 slots read against `objdump -d -M intel`. Every other row in the report is byte-identical: gcc 288/279/275/285 clean, `offsetof` 346/301/303/354 fields over 55/54/53/59 definitions with the artifacts byte-identical, polarity 517/574/497/441 anchor-A all correct with 0 inverted and 0 mismatch, `guards audited` exactly flat at 919/824/821/729 with `CHANGED 0 / only-base 0 / only-change 0`, unrecovered values 38/12/11/34, loop shape `for` 6/6/6/6 and `while` 111/106/99/98 and `do/while` 78/77/76/77 and `if` 1987/1938/1719/1759, invented prelude declarations 1524/2505/2327/1480, and 0 on staleReads, staleGuards, popReads, lostDefs, armExits, unencodableNames, wildBranches, arity over, callees lost, loops short, throws. **Eight tests are replaced and one preamble corrected**: four asserted `["arg_0x10"]` for shapes that are now no parameter at all — correct about naming, and the reason the record decision went unexamined — one asserted `arg_0x1A40` for what its own arithmetic makes argument 0's *home* slot in `t64!sub_14000D8C4`, and `pipeline.test.ts`'s "does not synthesise a struct out of a verified frame pointer" had to spill RCX into `[rbp+0x10]` before that slot is an argument to test with.
- **Recursing into a statement's nested bodies is `ir.ts`'s `bodiesOf` / `rewriteBodies`, and there is exactly one declaration of each.** `rewriteBodies` was a *verbatim* copy — docstring included — in both `structure.ts` and `cleanup.ts`, and `cleanup.ts` held two more specialisations of it under other names (`repairStmt` = it with `giveTrailingLabelsAStatement`, `cleanupStmt` = it with `cleanupPass`): four hand-synced switches over `IRStmt` for one traversal, the shape `sections.ts`, `ripRelative.ts`, `funcInsns.ts` and `stackIdiom.ts` each exist to end. All four ended in `default:`, so a new statement kind carrying a body would be returned unrecursed in every one of them — silently, and the copy you had not noticed is the one that bites. Both now end in an exhaustive `never` instead, which is a deliberate second decision and not part of the de-duplication: it names all twelve body-less kinds explicitly so the *next* body-carrying kind is a build error. Verified both directions — the merge is output-neutral (all 1127 emitted functions byte-identical across t32/t64/w64/w32, `compare.mjs` CHANGED 0 of 3144 guards, at `e22ba6e`), and a probe kind added to the union does fail the build at both switches. **`for`'s `init` and `update` are single statements, not lists, so neither function reaches inside them** — true of the copies too, and a caller needing that wants `foldStmt`'s shape (peek-a-bin-svwt).
- **`+`, `-` and `*` do not model wraparound at the operand's width, and the site they are folded at has NO width evidence to key one on.** `peek-a-bin-8fv` put the width-sensitive operators (the bitwise ops, shifts, `/`, `%`) through `fold64` at 64 bits and made these three merely *refuse* a result outside the exactly-representable double range. So `0x7FFFFFFF + 1` folds to `0x80000000`, where a 32-bit `add` produces the same bit pattern under a signed reading of `-0x80000000`. Censused at `bd73798` and **the class does not occur**: over the whole corpus these fold **33/42/40/30 `+` and 0/29/24/0 `-` and 0/0/0/0 `*`** (t32/t64/w64/w32) and **0 of them leave the int32 range** — 30/68/61/27 have `|result| <= 0xff`, and the largest anywhere is 4271612 (`0x412830 + 12`, an image address plus a field offset), about 500× below the boundary. Four things make this worth reading before touching it (`peek-a-bin-ivj5`):
  - **`knownWidth` returns `null` for an `IRConst`, deliberately**, so in the const-const branch — the only place these three fold — *both* operands report no width. The bead proposing the fix said "the width is in the IR (`IRConst.size`)", and that is the one thing it is not: `IRConst.size` is the CPU **mode**, 8 for every immediate in a 64-bit binary, which is exactly what `needs64`'s own comment warns against keying on (`or esi, 0xffffffff` would print 0xFFFFFFFF where −1 is the 32-bit register's value). There is no width to consult at that site.
  - **The tempting fix INTRODUCES a defect.** `(l + r) | 0` and `Math.imul` are what the *bitwise* arms of the same switch already do, by JavaScript's own semantics — which is where the asymmetry comes from and why it looks like an oversight. But `+`/`-`/`*` are not in `WIDTH_SENSITIVE`, so they never reach `fold64`, and wrapping would turn a correct 64-bit `add rax, 1` over the boundary into a negative constant. The 32-bit case it would fix is only a re-spelling of a bit pattern that is already right.
  - **Nothing here can see either direction.** With the wrap applied, `npm run corpus` is **byte-identical on all four binaries** (`CHANGED 0`, no moved rows, verdict "no regression"), so every one of its gates is structurally blind, and the *only* failing test was the 2^53 refusal one — catching it incidentally, for a different stated reason. `fold.test.ts` now carries `does not wrap an arithmetic fold to int32` so that trap fails for its own reason; negative-controlled.
  - **`is64` is the only sound evidence, and `fold.ts` does not have it** — a PE32 image has no 64-bit arithmetic, so there the wrap would be provably right. Threading it is the fix if a real occurrence is ever seen; it would be a no-op on this corpus, and `fold.ts` importing only `ir.ts` is deliberate.

- **`Ordinal_<n>` is a WIRE FORMAT, not a label, and the two ends of it lived in different files with nothing connecting them.** `parsePE` writes `Ordinal_<n>` into `PEFile.imports[].functions` for an import that names no function; `computeImphash` **parses it back out** with `startsWith("Ordinal_")` to decide whether an entry is an ordinal at all. Neither file pointed at the other and no type related them, so **respelling the parser's output changed every affected imphash silently**: the entry falls through imphash's ordinal branch into its by-name branch and hashes the literal display text, e.g. `somelib.ord_42` where pefile says `somelib.ord42`. **A hash has no runtime symptom** — it is only ever compared with another tool's answer — so the failure mode is a corpus match that stops matching, months later, with nothing on screen to notice. `ORDINAL_IMPORT_PREFIX`, `formatOrdinalImport`, `parseOrdinalImport` and `resolveOrdinal` in `pe/ordinalTables.ts` are the one declaration now, and the property is asserted rather than described: `pe/__tests__/ordinalImports.test.ts` builds its fixtures **through** `formatOrdinalImport`, so moving the prefix moves both ends together and the digests must not move — verified by doing it (prefix `Ordinal_` → `Ord_`: the invariance suite stays green while the tests that pin the literal spelling go red, which is the correct division). Two smaller rules fell out of it. `parseOrdinalImport` answers **null**, never `NaN`, for a malformed tail — callers read null as "this is a name", where `NaN` would make `ord${NaN}` a plausible-looking imphash component; the old inline `parseInt` needed an `isNaN` guard three lines further down to avoid exactly that, and the guard is now unnecessary rather than merely present. And `resolveOrdinal` is keyed by the library name **with** its extension, lowercased, because `ws2_32.dll` and `wsock32.dll` disagree on ordinals 10, 11, 12 and 24 — a key that dropped the extension would merge two tables that are deliberately separate. **The view now resolves through that same function**, which is the user-visible half: the Imports tab shows `WSAStartup` for `ws2_32!115` instead of `Ordinal_115`, resolved in the memo *above* the filter so the name on screen is the name that can be searched for, and marked `#115` because it is inferred from a table rather than read out of the file — an import by ordinal and one by name are different facts about a binary, and a packer may have chosen the ordinal precisely to keep the name out of it. An ordinal the tables do not cover keeps `Ordinal_<n>`, which is the control: resolving everything and resolving nothing are each one assertion away from passing a suite that only checked the other. **`npm run corpus` is byte-identical over four binaries**, and here that is evidence rather than a formality — the PE parser is in the corpus import graph and is checked differentially against an independently written from-spec reader. (`peek-a-bin-p0qw`)

- **A CodeView PDB GUID is a `GUID` STRUCT, not sixteen bytes — and the suite had pinned the wrong reading as the rule.** `IMAGE_DEBUG_TYPE_CODEVIEW`'s payload is `CV_INFO_PDB70 { DWORD CvSignature; GUID Signature; DWORD Age; BYTE PdbFileName[]; }`, and `GUID` is `{ DWORD Data1; WORD Data2; WORD Data3; BYTE Data4[8]; }` laid out in **native** byte order. The canonical text form (`StringFromGUID2`, RFC 4122, and the Microsoft symbol-server path) prints the first three fields as *integers* and the last as a *byte string* — so on a little-endian host the first three groups are byte-swapped relative to the file and the fourth is not. `parseDebugDirectory` hex-joined all sixteen bytes in file order and sliced them 8/4/4/4/12, which is right for `Data4` and wrong for everything before it.

  **The class, and why it is the `Ordinal_<n>` class rather than the `>>> 0` class.** Nothing inside this tool ever compares a PDB GUID with anything. Its entire purpose is to be read *out*: the symbol-server path for a PDB is `<name>.pdb/<GUID><Age>/<name>.pdb`, so what the panel prints is a lookup key for another system. A wrong spelling is therefore not malformed, not empty and not obviously odd — it is a well-formed GUID that matches nothing, which is exactly the failure mode `ORDINAL_IMPORT_PREFIX` exists to prevent. It reached the page on every binary built with symbols, which for MSVC output is essentially all of them: all five corpus binaries carry a CodeView record.

  **THE ORACLE IS REAL MSVC OUTPUT, and it is decisive.** `CoCreateGuid` mints RFC 4122 version-4 (random) UUIDs. The version nibble is the **first hex digit of the third group** — `Data3`'s high nibble — which is precisely one of the fields this swap moves, and the variant bits are the top two bits of `Data4[0]`, which it does not move. Read out of the six corpus binaries — the integrator re-derived this independently with a from-spec reader, and got the **two ARM64 launchers as well**, which the original table omitted:

  | binary | file-order reading | ver | corrected reading | ver | variant |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `t32.exe` | `A1235908-ABB7-ED44-B16B-45E583405715` | **E** | `085923A1-B7AB-44ED-B16B-45E583405715` | 4 | `10` |
  | `t64.exe` | `957C2BBD-DDC8-4745-99F6-0DBBFEDF5A30` | 4 | `BD2B7C95-C8DD-4547-99F6-0DBBFEDF5A30` | 4 | `10` |
  | `w64.exe` | `C58155E6-0226-7B41-ACDE-82D805DC896F` | **7** | `E65581C5-2602-417B-ACDE-82D805DC896F` | 4 | `10` |
  | `w32.exe` | `2E033976-4827-7948-8FD8-0F9F61D5371B` | **7** | `7639032E-2748-4879-8FD8-0F9F61D5371B` | 4 | `10` |
  | `t64-arm.exe` | `3FE59A8C-6B46-B44E-9D1B-1B5473B1D0C6` | **B** | `8C9AE53F-466B-4EB4-9D1B-1B5473B1D0C6` | 4 | `10` |
  | `w64-arm.exe` | `C09CAAE8-8C3D-1449-8BF1-87D7A41B552B` | **1** | `E8AA9CC0-3D8C-4914-8BF1-87D7A41B552B` | 4 | `10` |
  | `t64-arm.exe` | `3FE59A8C-6B46-B44E-9D1B-1B5473B1D0C6` | **B** | `8C9AE53F-466B-4EB4-9D1B-1B5473B1D0C6` | 4 | `10` |

  Five for five under the corrected reading, one for five under the old one — and that one is the 1-in-16 coincidence. The variant bits agree either way, which is the control: they are in the field that does *not* move, so a reading that also disturbed `Data4` would have broken them.

  **THE SUITE HAD PINNED THE DEFECT AS THE RULE.** `pe/__tests__/metadata.test.ts` asserted `01020304-0506-0708-090A-0B0C0D0E0F10` for bytes `01`…`10`, under the comment *"Bytes 01..10 in file order, formatted as a GUID string"* — a restatement of what the code does rather than of what the format says, which is the recurring shape in this repo (`test` clearing SF, `detectForLoop`'s id ordering, `wroteAnyAlias`). The replacement pins the corrected string and states the oracle in the comment; a second case pins a GUID whose four fields are each distinguishable (`AABBCCDD-1122-3344-5566-778899AABBCC` on disk), so a swap applied to the wrong field, or to all sixteen bytes, cannot pass.

  **What could see it, and what could not.** Not `typecheck` — both spellings are `string`. Not any corpus gate — a debug record reaches neither the emitted C nor the IR, so gcc, polarity, `offsetof`, arity and every stale-read gate draw from an empty population, exactly as they do for `Instruction.comment`. Not the existing unit test, which asserted the defect. The instrument is `HeaderView.dom.test.tsx`, rendering the Debug Info block for the first time: it derives the expected text from the fixture's own bytes with an **independently written** swap (`guidTextOf`) and pins the literal beside it, so a change to either cannot quietly make the other agree.

  **Controls.** Reverting to the file-order read reddens 3 rows (1 render, 2 unit); swapping `Data2`/`Data3`'s offsets reddens the same 3; reading `PointerToRawData` from the `AddressOfRawData` field 4 bytes earlier reddens 3; a debug entry stride of 24 instead of 28 reddens 2. **Two controls came back INERT and are recorded rather than tuned away**: zeroing `SizeOfData` moves nothing, because `parseDebugDirectory` never reads it; and misaligning the certificate blob by one byte moves nothing, because `parseSecurityDirectory` takes the directory's offset verbatim.

  **A one-character neighbour, fixed with it.** `parseRichHeader`'s `useCount` was `view.getUint32(i + 4, true) ^ xorKey`, and `^` is an **int32** operator in JS — so the XOR of two `uint32`s comes back signed and a stored `0xFFFFFFFF` reached the Rich Header table as `-1`. `toolId` and `buildId` are masked back to 16 bits out of the same word and were never affected, which is why only the count was wrong. **Unfalsifiable on real output**: all 36 Rich entries across the four x86 corpus binaries have counts under 150 (maxima 121, 118, 118, 115), so the population is packed and corrupted headers — where a negative count reads as a parse failure rather than as the value the file holds. Kept anyway because the fix is `>>> 0` and the alternative is a row nobody can interpret.

  **Two things deliberately NOT changed, and both are judgements rather than oversights.** (1) `parseDebugDirectory` never consults `SizeOfData`, so the PDB path scan runs from `pointerToRawData + 24` to the next NUL **anywhere in the file** — on a hostile image with no NUL after the record that is a `TextDecoder` over the remaining megabytes, during a render. Bounding it is not obviously right: `SizeOfData` is attacker-controlled too and a zero or understated value would silently truncate a valid path, so the bound to pick (declared size, an absolute cap, or both with a fallback) is a decision to take with an instrument, not inside this change. (2) The Data Directories table heads the Certificate Table's first column **"RVA"** when the format says that field is a file offset. `dumpbin` prints it in the same column with the same heading, and the fix is a per-row heading exception in a table whose whole point is uniformity. Both were left as bead text.

  **The fixture work this rests on.** `src/pe/__tests__/fixtures.ts` gained three opt-in structures, none of which changes a byte for any existing caller: an `IMAGE_DEBUG_DIRECTORY` with a real RSDS payload (whose `PointerToRawData` is a **file offset**, so it is emitted as a deferred fixup — nothing inside the directory-section builder knows where its own section will land); a `Rich` header, which **moves `e_lfanew`** past 0x80 because that is where `parseRichHeader` starts looking, so the whole layout is re-derived from it; and a `WIN_CERTIFICATE` past the last section carrying a hand-built PKCS#7 blob, since the security directory's first field is a file offset and the blob therefore cannot live in a section. Each is controlled from both directions: making the builder ignore the option reddens exactly the rows that read it (2, 2 and 4 respectively).

- **`>>> 0` on a value that can exceed 2^32 is a TRUNCATION, not a respelling, and `HeaderView`'s `CopyableHex` is where that bit.** `(value >>> 0).toString(16)` is the idiomatic way to print a value read as a signed int32 unsigned, and it is correct for exactly that: it is why `SectionTable` uses it on a section's `characteristics`. It is also **ToUint32**, i.e. a silent reduction modulo 2^32, and the Headers tab is handed 64-bit quantities — a PE32+ `ImageBase` is 0x140000000 for an MSVC x64 EXE and 0x180000000 for a DLL, and every field in the TLS block is an image-based VA. **What was on screen**: `Image Base 0x0000000040000000`, two rows under an `Entry Point` of `0x140001000` the same panel spelled correctly, on essentially every 64-bit binary the tool opens. Live and ungated — `App.tsx`'s `tabComponents` mounts `HeaderView` for the `headers` tab and `VIEW_TABS` routes to it with nothing in front. **The fix is `value < 0 ? value >>> 0 : value`**, which keeps the one case the operator was there for; reverting it reddens exactly two tests (the Image Base row, and the TLS VAs), and the integrator re-derived that independently rather than taking it on report. **Why nothing static could see it**: `value` is `number` under either spelling so `typecheck` is silent, `>>> 0` beside a `padStart(16)` reads as deliberate, no lint rule models arithmetic range, and the corpus never renders — no gate in this repo reads a formatter's output. The instrument is `HeaderView.dom.test.tsx`, and the property that makes it one is that every assertion about a value is written against the bytes the fixture actually contains rather than against "a table appeared": a hex/decimal slip, an endianness slip, a flag-table entry on the wrong bit and a truncating formatter all fail. It was found on that suite's first run. (`peek-a-bin-p0qw`)

- **The Data Directories table's column heading is right about fifteen rows and wrong about one, and the correction belongs on the ROW.** Directory 4, the Certificate Table, is the one directory in the PE format whose address field is a raw **file offset** rather than an RVA — the attribute certificates sit outside every section, so a signing tool can append or strip them without touching the section table. `pe/authenticode.ts`'s `parseSecurityDirectory` already reads it as one and says so in a comment; only `HeaderView`'s table disagreed, and only on that row, heading every row's first column "RVA". So the number was correctly read and incorrectly labelled — the same family as the `>>> 0` entry above (a formatter stating something the parser knows to be otherwise), and, like it, invisible to `typecheck`, to lint and to every corpus gate. **What was refused, and why the refusal is the interesting half.** Heading the column **"RVA / offset"** was the obvious move and is worse: a heading applies to all sixteen rows, so it makes fifteen correct rows ambiguous in order to disambiguate one, and a reader coming from `dumpbin` — which prints this value under a plain "RVA" heading too — ends up with less information rather than more. A **second column**, blank on fifteen rows, widens the table permanently for one row's sake. A **tooltip alone** leaves the false text on screen, is invisible until hovered and unreachable on touch. What landed is a muted parenthetical `(file offset)` beside the number on that row only, which is the panel's **own existing idiom** for "what this number actually is" — `0x014C (x86)`, `0x010B (PE32)`, `(RVA: 0x…)`, `(Unknown)` — so no footnote mechanism is invented for a single row; the explanation is in a `title` on the parenthetical as a supplement, never as the whole fix. **It is shown UNCONDITIONALLY, including where the directory is empty** (0/0, i.e. every unsigned binary): the statement is about the *field*, not about the value, and suppressing it when zero would make it read as a property of this file while leaving the table saying "RVA" over that row on nearly every binary anyone opens. The row is keyed on `IMAGE_DIRECTORY_ENTRY_SECURITY`, never on the directory's display name. Pinned in `HeaderView.dom.test.tsx` in both directions and with the two halves of "and only that row" in **separate tests on purpose** — within one `it` the first failing assertion hides the second, so a control that marks every row would only ever be seen to redden whichever came first. (`peek-a-bin-xnne`)

- **The declared data directory count and the table the parser actually read are two true facts that mislead as a pair, and the clamp is DERIVED rather than published.** `numberOfRvaAndSizes` is attacker-controlled — `parseDataDirectories` says so in as many words — so it reads `Math.min(count, 16, fits)` entries, while `optionalHeader.numberOfRvaAndSizes` keeps the raw value. `HeaderView` printed both with nothing connecting them, so **a PE32+ declaring 40 rendered `Number of RVA and Sizes: 40` directly above a table of SIXTEEN rows**. Measured by building exactly that image and rendering it, not reasoned. **Why it is worse than a cosmetic mismatch**: this is the adversarial-input direction of `peek-a-bin-xnne`'s class. Every real linker writes exactly 16, so a declared 40 is a crafted-PE tell; the parser noticed it deliberately, and the panel — the one surface a human reads — was the single place it disappeared. Neither number is false alone, which is what makes the pair the defect. **THE DECISION, and the half worth keeping.** Three routes were on the table: a parenthetical in the panel's own idiom, a parser-level field, and an anomaly. Routes one and three both landed, because they answer different readers: the parenthetical serves the reader already looking at the count, and `analysis/anomalies.ts` serves the analyst who wants to be told what is wrong with a file. (That reader opened the **Anomalies tab** when this was written; the tab was removed at `peek-a-bin-1xc5` and the same findings now render as dismissible banners on the Headers tab, plus MCP and the Markdown export. The three-route argument is unaffected — only the surface changed.) **Route two was REFUSED on the precedent it would have copied.** `PEFile.importsTruncated` exists because the import walk's truncation is *not recoverable from its output* — a list cut short at a bound is shaped exactly like a complete short list, so unless the parser publishes the fact nothing downstream can tell.
