That is the `ORDINAL_IMPORT_PREFIX` / `TRUNCATION_MARKER` / `pe/sections.ts` class, and the drift here is worse than usual because a wrong epoch renders as a **confident expiry claim**: neither spelling is ill-typed and nothing cross-checks them. `parseUTCTime` and `parseGeneralizedTime` return a `DERTime` (`{ text, ms }`) from one reading of the bytes, `CertificateInfo.notAfterMs` carries it, and the pivot is applied **once** with both halves reading the same `fullYear`, so a 1997 certificate cannot print one year and compare as another. `notBefore` gets **no** epoch: nothing reads one, a not-yet-valid certificate is a real thing the panel makes no claim about, and an unpublished field would be a second reading with no reader to keep it honest.
  - **`certificateValidityState(cert, nowMs)` HAS THREE STATES AND THE THIRD IS THE POINT.** `"expired" | "current" | "unknown"`. Folding an unreadable date in with `"current"` would make the tool assert a validity it has no evidence for — the same defect as the green pill, one level down — so `"unknown"` renders **neither** claim: an amber row saying the date above is not a calendar time this tool can compare against now. `nowMs` is a **parameter** rather than a `Date.now()` inside, and that signature is what makes every arm reachable from a test **without touching the clock**: `vi.useFakeTimers()` deadlocks `waitFor` and `userEvent` in this repo, and a suite pinning "expired" against the real clock flips arm as the calendar turns. The comparison is `nowMs > notAfterMs`, since `notAfter` names the **last second** the certificate is valid, and both sides of that boundary are pinned — a `>=` would call a live certificate dead for its final second and no other row could see it.
  - **`null` AND A NON-NULL `notAfter` HOLD AT ONCE, AND THAT PAIRING IS WHAT THE ROUND-TRIP CHECK BUYS.** `isDigits` promises only that the field is ASCII digits, so `241301000000Z` reaches the formatter with a thirteenth month and prints `2024-13-01 00:00:00 UTC`. **`Date.UTC` does not reject it — it rolls over to 2025-01-01** — so an unchecked epoch would silently disagree with the text printed beside it, which is *worse* than no epoch: a rolled-over date is a confident answer about the wrong instant, and in this case one in the past, i.e. a tool asserting that a certificate denoting nothing has expired. `epochFor` builds the instant and **reads it back component by component**, which is one comparison per field rather than six range tables and is exactly right by construction — an instant that reads back as the components it was built from is precisely one the components name. February 30th and a leap second (`sec` 60) are refused by the same test, correctly: there is no such instant in the `Date` model, so nothing here can compare it. February 29th of a leap year is the control that the check rejects impossible dates without rejecting unusual valid ones.
  - **THE FIXTURE'S DEFAULT `notAfter` IS HISTORICAL AND EVERY CALLER INHERITING IT IS NOW IN THE EXPIRED ARM.** `buildPKCS7`'s default is `260115085959Z` — 2026-01-15, in the future when it was written and in the past from 2026-01-15 onward. It is deliberately **not** moved (changing it would silently change what ~10 callers of that builder assert, and the `23`/`26` pair is what pins the `UTCTime` pivot in `HeaderView.dom.test.tsx`'s rows); instead both the field and the default carry a comment saying so, and every new expiry assertion passes an **explicit unambiguous** date — `010101000000Z` for expired, `491231235959Z` for current, the latter being the last year the pivot puts in the future. A suite that inherited a moving default would flip arm on a date.
  - **Three negative controls, all discriminating, none inert — and TWO OF THE BEAD'S PREDICTIONS WERE LOW, which is recorded rather than tuned away.** (1) Delete the scope sentence → **4 red** (the bead predicted "exactly one row"), every one in the new `says what it did not verify` block and **no pre-existing row moved** — the blast-radius half of the prediction, which held; the count is 4 because the sentence is asserted at four sites, three of them as a liveness check beside another claim (the unparsable-type arm, the not-expired arm, the no-date arm), which is deliberate: a sentence that renders on only *some* signed files is the defect returning. (2) `certificateValidityState` returning `"current"` for a `null` epoch → **4 red** as predicted, 3 node and 1 DOM, the DOM one being the row that asserts an unreadable date renders neither claim — *the row that stops the fix from claiming a validity it cannot know*. (3) Hardcode the epoch in `parseUTCTime` instead of deriving it → **8 red** (predicted 5), 5 node and 3 DOM. **The instructive part of (3): the suite's exhaustive `toEqual` is NOT what catches it.** Hardcoding the epoch to that fixture's own instant leaves the happy-path row green, so every discriminating row is one that derives the expected instant from bytes the parser was not handed — the `HeaderView.dom.test.tsx` house pattern earning its keep rather than being a style rule.
  - **FIXTURE-VERIFIED ONLY, and that is a property of the machine rather than of the change: NO BINARY HERE IS SIGNED.** All six corpus images report no certificate at all, so `npm run corpus:parserdiff` has nothing to say about any of this and was not run for it (nothing under `src/disasm/` moved either). The `authenticode.ts` bounds work above records the same gap. What that leaves unverified is narrow and worth stating: no real Authenticode `SignedData` has ever reached this code, so a genuine countersigned Microsoft signature has never been rendered by the changed block; and jsdom performs no layout, so the neutral chip and the two amber rows are asserted as text and class names and **no human has seen either on screen** (`peek-a-bin-v2u`).
  - **DELIBERATELY OUT OF SCOPE, and the split was the user's choice rather than an omission**: computing the Authenticode PE hash (the special-cased checksum, security directory and certificate-table exclusions) and comparing it against the digest in `SpcIndirectDataContent` is a **separate bead**. That is the tier that would let the pill make a positive claim; until it lands, the honest pill is a neutral one. Nothing here reaches MCP output or the markdown report either — `parseAdmissions` covers the *unreadable* certificate and not the unverified one, and an unverified signature is the ordinary case rather than an admission about a narrowing.
- **…AND THE ONE PLACE THE WHOLE CLASS HAD NEVER BEEN APPLIED WAS A VIEW-SIDE SCAN: the hex tab's byte search.** Every cap in `src/pe/` renders an admission — `TRUNCATION_MARKER` (`peek-a-bin-nygv`), `ImportEntry.truncated` (`peek-a-bin-tmo9`), `ResourceTree.incomplete` (`peek-a-bin-dhcx`), `PEFile.stringScan` (`peek-a-bin-2py5`), `DebugDirectory.truncated` (`peek-a-bin-wo8g`) — and then the same class sat open in a component, where nobody had thought to look because the class had been read as a *parser* problem. `findBytePatternMatches` (`components/HexView.tsx`) broke at a bare literal `1000` and returned a `number[]`; the toolbar printed `{matchCount} matches`. So a search for `00` over a real `.rdata` reported **"1000 matches" as a fact about the section**, with the count indistinguishable from a complete one and nothing anywhere saying the scan had stopped. It needs no crafted file — one common byte in an ordinary section reaches it, which puts it beside `2py5` rather than beside the crafted-input entries above (`peek-a-bin-v3uh.7`).
  - **`truncated` IS DECIDED EXACTLY, AND THE STRUCTURE OF THE LOOP IS THE WHOLE OF WHY.** The break is taken **on the match that would exceed the cap**, never on the one that fills it: `if (offsets.length === MAX_BYTE_PATTERN_MATCHES) return { offsets, truncated: true }` *above* the `push`. So `truncated` means "a match beyond the cap exists" and a result of exactly `MAX_BYTE_PATTERN_MATCHES` is a **whole** answer that is not marked. The tempting spelling — `truncated: offsets.length >= MAX_BYTE_PATTERN_MATCHES`, with the push and break left where they were — is precisely the off-by-one `dhcx` found one reader over in `ResourceTree`, where a directory holding **exactly** its budget claimed to be short over a complete answer, and it is the first negative control here. It costs at most one further walk over bytes the loop was already covering: the cap was never an asymptotic bound, it bounds the RESULT (the offsets array and the highlight `Set` built from it).
  - **THE ADMISSION GOES ON THE COUNT LINE, NOT ON A ROW, and that is `ResourceTree.incomplete`'s half of the choice.** The bound is global to the scan, so no single match is "the incomplete one" — the same reason `Budget` is threaded by reference through every `walkDirectory` frame and leaves no row to hang the flag on. `ImportEntry.truncated` is per-library for the opposite reason: each descriptor has its own thunk walk. The reading is `1000+ matches in .rdata (search stopped at 1000)`, which says it twice on purpose — the `+` marks the VALUE as a floor (`TRUNCATION_MARKER`'s model, the admission spelled into the thing a reader's eye lands on) and the parenthetical names what happened, for a reader who wonders why. `matchSummary` is the one declaration of that sentence, so the `+` and the parenthetical cannot come apart.
  - **THE SECOND HALF IS THE SCOPE, and it is the same falsehood one step out.** The scan runs over `sectionBytes` — ONE section — so `"1000+ matches"` unqualified reads as a claim about the FILE, and `"No matches"` (the *stronger* falsehood: the file lacks it) even more so. `2py5`'s `stringScan` shape at much smaller stakes. Both sentences now name the section, and the input carries a matching `title`/`aria-label`; `searchScope` is derived from `sectionInfo?.name`, the same value the section `<select>` shows, so the sentence cannot name a section other than the one being searched. Naming it in the affordance ALONE would not have been enough — a `title` is invisible until hovered, and the count line is where the claim is actually made.
  - **Three negative controls, all discriminating, none inert.** (1) `truncated` from `offsets.length >= MAX` reddens **exactly one** row, the exactly-at-the-cap one, while the over-the-cap row stays green — which is what shows the boundary fixture is real rather than a restatement of the implementation. (2) Dropping the `+` and the parenthetical from `matchSummary` reddens **exactly one** row, the over-the-cap one. (3) Removing the input's `aria-label` reddens the two scope rows and nothing else.
  - **The fixture is the load-bearing part and it rests on one layout fact.** `buildMinimalPE64` writes `sizeOfRawData` as the section's own `data.length`, not the file-aligned length, so `HexView`'s `sectionBytes` window is *exactly* the array the test handed it and the zero padding up to file alignment is outside anything the scan sees. A section of `MAX_BYTE_PATTERN_MATCHES` copies of `0xAA` therefore has exactly that many matches for a one-byte `AA` pattern, by construction rather than by counting a fixture nobody controls — and without that, neither side of the boundary could be pinned. Both sides are pinned, per `dhcx`.
  - **Nothing was verified about the scan's COST and the cap was not raised.** The scan is still main-thread on every keystroke with no debounce, and the offsets are still discarded rather than kept for next/prev navigation. Both are held out deliberately: the debounce brings the two *measured* jsdom traps (`waitFor` and `userEvent` deadlock under `vi.useFakeTimers()`; and a single advance past the boundary cannot tell a 250 ms debounce from no debounce — that control came back inert twice), and next/prev is a new affordance rather than a repair. This entry is an honesty change and claims nothing else.
- **…AND DEBOUNCING THAT SCAN CREATED A NEW INSTANCE OF THE SAME CLASS, IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION: A SENTENCE ABOUT A SCAN THAT HAD NOT HAPPENED.** The entry above left the scan main-thread on every keystroke and the offsets discarded; `peek-a-bin-f4gz` adds a 150 ms debounce and next/prev. **The debounce is not a free performance change, and the hazard is intrinsic to it rather than a slip in this implementation.** The moment the typed input and the scanned query are two pieces of state, there is a window in which the toolbar describes the OLD scan while the box shows the NEW query — and one of the things it says there is `No matches in .rdata`, **which is a positive claim about the section**. Type over a query that found nothing and that sentence stands for a further 150 ms beside a pattern nothing has looked for yet: the narrower-answer-wearing-a-complete-one's-shape rule (`ImportEntry.truncated`, `ResourceTree.incomplete`, `PEFile.stringScan`) in its most literal form, since the narrower answer here is *no answer at all*. **The first sketch of this change had exactly that defect and shipped no test that could see it** — it gated the toolbar on `activeSearch`, the query the SCAN keys on, which by construction still equals the result's own query throughout the wait, so the stale sentence is judged current (`peek-a-bin-f4gz`).
  - **THE FIX IS TO CARRY THE QUESTION WITH THE ANSWER, so that "not scanned yet" and "scanned, found nothing" are two states rather than one.** `ByteSearchResult` holds `{ query, data, offsets, truncated, highlighted }`, and `searchSettled` is `result.query === byteSearch && result.data === sectionBytes` — compared against **what is in the box**, not against `activeSearch`. Every sentence and every control the search offers is gated on it, and while a *parseable* query is unsettled the toolbar prints a neutral `Searching…`, which is not a claim about the section. Gating on `activeSearch` instead reddens both pending rows and two of the debounce rows. The `data` half is the **second axis**: switching sections changes neither the box nor the debounce, so a query-only test would have reported one section's count under another section's name.
  - **The pending marker is gated on the query PARSING, and that is a third state and not a refinement of the second.** Gibberish is not a scan in flight, so `zz` gets silence rather than a `Searching…` that never resolves — the same distinction the entry above draws between "no matches" and "not a byte pattern". One control, discriminating.
  - **TWO CONTROLS CAME BACK INERT AND BOTH ARE REPORTED RATHER THAN TUNED AWAY, and each names a real limit of what jsdom can judge here.** (1) **Failing to CANCEL the pending timer on a keystroke** — a delay rather than a debounce — moves no row. The reason is a consequence of the fix: a stale timer's result arrives stamped with the superseded query, `searchSettled` judges it unsettled, and the toolbar says nothing about it, so the only cost left is the wasted walk — and counting walks would need a seam in `HexView` existing solely for a test. The test is therefore named for what it pins (`reports nothing from a query superseded before the wait elapsed`) rather than for what it does not. (2) **The `data === sectionBytes` half of `searchSettled`** cannot be reddened at all: its window is one frame, and testing-library flushes passive effects inside the same `act`, so the frame in which the old section's count sits under the new section's name is never observable. It is kept because the behaviour is right in a browser. (3) A third, smaller one: **making an emptied box wait out the debounce** also moves nothing, since with an empty box every sentence is already gone and the only thing the delay holds back is the HIGHLIGHTS — which jsdom cannot render, the grid having no rows.
  - **BOTH SIDES OF THE DEBOUNCE BOUNDARY ARE PINNED, because one side is not evidence.** A single advance past the boundary cannot tell a 150 ms debounce from no debounce — "the answer is on screen now" is equally true of a scan that ran synchronously — and that control has come back inert twice in this repo. So each row asserts at `BYTE_SEARCH_DEBOUNCE_MS - 1` (no count, and **no `No matches` either**) and then at the boundary; and one row keeps the total elapsed time well past 150 ms while no single gap reaches it. `userEvent` and `waitFor` both deadlock under `vi.useFakeTimers()`, hence `fireEvent.change` and an explicit `act`.
  - **NEXT/PREV IS ASSERTED AS A REQUEST, AND NOTHING HAS BEEN SEEN TO SCROLL.** `virtual-core` measures the scroll element with `offsetHeight`, which is 0 here, so the grid renders no rows and a `scrollToIndex` moves nothing that exists. The tests record the index the view was ASKED to bring into range, through a `vi.mock` of `@tanstack/react-virtual` that wraps the instance's `scrollToIndex` — an instance property assigned in the constructor, so it cannot be spied on the prototype, and guarded by a `WeakSet` because the instance is stable across renders and would otherwise be wrapped once per render. The mount's own scroll (row 0, from `currentRowIdx`) is dropped in the helper, or the record would not be a record of the buttons. Beside it, the `SET_ADDRESS` dispatch is asserted as the observable half — that is what makes the address bar and the data inspector agree with the row the grid was told to show.
  - **`matchIdx === -1` is a third state and not a zero.** Forward from it is the FIRST match and backward from it is the LAST, which plain modular arithmetic gets wrong in the backward direction (it lands one short of the end); and no position is printed at all until a step has happened, since `1/40` over a view that has not moved is a claim about where the grid is. A new scan resets it, or a stale index points into a list it did not come from. **One of those controls was inert until the FIXTURE was sharpened**: the second query in the reset row matched nothing, so the whole navigator was unrendered and a carried-over cursor was hidden rather than caught — a second needle byte fixes it, and the inert reading is recorded in the test.
  - **ONE ROW GOES THROUGH THE REAL `appReducer`, AND IT IS THE ONLY ONE THAT CAN SEE THE MEMO ARGUMENT THE NAVIGATOR RESTS ON.** Every other row uses a `vi.fn()` dispatch, so `currentAddress` never moves and the chain behind `sectionBytes` is stable for free. In the app it is not free: a step dispatches `SET_ADDRESS`, which re-runs the `sectionInfo` memo, and were that to return a fresh object the `sectionBytes` memo below it would too — re-running the scan effect, which resets `matchIdx`, so every second press would be a first press forever. What holds it is that `sectionInfo` returns an element OF `pe.sections`. Spelling it `{ ...sec }` reddens that row and nothing else.
  - **Left out deliberately and claiming nothing**: whole-file scope (which would have to move the scan to the METRICS worker — the disasm worker is serial and would queue behind a disassembly) and an ASCII/UTF-16 query mode. Neither falls out of this change, and the cap was not raised.

- **THE `.pdata` LANGUAGE-SPECIFIC DATA IS NOT SELF-DESCRIBING, AND THE FORMAT'S OWN REDUNDANCY IS WHAT MAKES READING IT DECIDABLE WITH NO SYMBOLS.** `parsePdata` read an x64 `UNWIND_INFO`'s handler RVA and stopped there, so the `__try`/`__except`/`__finally` regions MSVC records for C SEH — the one place the image states its exception structure outright — were never read (`peek-a-bin-j4uk.4`). `UNWIND_INFO` says only *that* a handler exists and gives its RVA; the bytes after it are whatever **that handler's** convention says, and the handler is a linker-chosen address with no symbol in a stripped image. Three conventions share the slot in ordinary MSVC output — `__C_specific_handler` (a `Count` plus `Count` 16-byte `{Begin, End, Handler, JumpTarget}` records), `__GSHandlerCheck` (a single `uint32` stack-cookie frame offset) and `__CxxFrameHandler3` (a single `uint32` `FuncInfo` RVA) — with the combining forms (`__GSHandlerCheck_SEH`, `__GSHandlerCheck_EH`) putting a scope table *before* the cookie offset. So reading the first word as a `Count` unconditionally hands a caller a cookie offset like 0x30 as "48 guarded regions" and a `FuncInfo` RVA as ~16,000 of them.
  - **`readScopeTable` (`pe/pdata.ts`) therefore publishes a table only behind a FOUR-PART STRUCTURAL CHECK, and the check is an oracle rather than a plausibility test.** (1) `Count >= 1`, at or under `MAX_SCOPE_TABLE_ENTRIES`, and `4 + Count * 16` fitting inside both the containing section's raw extent and the buffer. (2) Every region satisfies `begin < end` and lies inside `[rf.beginAddress, rf.endAddress)` — the extent of the very record the table hangs off. (3) `begin` non-decreasing across entries. (4) `handler` is 0, 1, or an RVA the section table resolves, and `jumpTarget` is 0 or inside the function. Check (2) is the one that makes a wrong reading nearly impossible to pass: a `__try` region is lexically inside its function **by construction**, so four RVAs read out of the wrong bytes would all have to land inside one ~100-byte extent, in order, with a resolvable third field.
  - **`undefined` MEANS "THE RECORD DID NOT SAY", NEVER "THERE ARE NO REGIONS", and a failure yields NOTHING rather than a short table.** The first half is `arm64Frame`'s rule restated for the same reason — a consumer reading absence as an empty region list reports a guarded function as unguarded. The second is `peek-a-bin-tmo9`'s lesson pointed the other way: a walk cut short at a bound is shaped exactly like a complete short list, so a caller cannot tell a two-region table from the first two regions of a five-region one and would draw the `__try` around the wrong span. There is consequently no `TRUNCATION_MARKER`-style admission either — there is no value to spell it into, and `undefined` is already a claim a caller can act on honestly.
  - **THE BOUND IS `peek-a-bin-tmo9`'s PRODUCT CLASS ARRIVING IN THE FILE THAT HAS ALREADY BEEN BITTEN BY IT.** `Count` is a `uint32` from the file multiplied by 16 bytes, evaluated once per handler-bearing record, and the record count is itself file-supplied with every entry free to name the same `UNWIND_INFO` — so the cost is `records x Count x 16` unless both factors are bounded. `MAX_PDATA_ENTRIES` bounds the first. **The primary bound on the second is the SECTION** (`sectionRawLimitForRva` over `unwindInfoAddress` — deliberately *not* `parseX64Pdata`'s `limit`, which bounds `.pdata` while the record lives in `.xdata`), with `MAX_SCOPE_TABLE_ENTRIES` (2^16) as the backstop on `MAX_PDATA_ENTRIES`' model. Measured: 20,000 records each claiming 60,000 individually-valid regions is a 2.4 MB file that, with the section bound removed, admits 1.2 **billion** `ScopeTableEntry` objects in `parsePE` on the main thread and does not finish inside a 60 s timeout.

- **THE `.pdata` SCOPE-TABLE CENSUS (0870e14) — the number stage A1's rule has to be written against.** Over the two x64 corpus binaries, of the handler-bearing records (`handlerFlags & 0x3` and not `CHAININFO`):

  | | t64 | w64 |
  |---|---|---|
  | `.pdata` rows | 240 | 235 |
  | handler-bearing records | **50** | **46** |
  | scope table VALIDATES | **30** | **28** |
  | UHANDLER-only (`0x2` without `0x1`) | 2 | 2 |
  | EHANDLER, no valid table — **the `/GS` population** | **18** | **16** |
  | entries in the valid tables | 34 | 32 |
  | largest single table | 2 | 2 |
  | tables of 1 / of 2 | 26 / 4 | 24 / 4 |
  | entries that are `__finally` (`jumpTarget == 0`) | 31 | 29 |
  | entries that are `__except` | 3 | 3 |
  | entries with `handler == 1` (`EXCEPTION_EXECUTE_HANDLER`) | 1 | 1 |

  The partition is exact (30 + 2 + 18 = 50; 28 + 2 + 16 = 46), and **it corroborates against two independently taken measurements**: the 50 and 46 handler-bearing records are precisely the 50 and 46 functions that currently emit `__try`, and the 18 refusals on t64 are precisely the 18 t64 functions an earlier survey found carrying a visible `/GS` cookie xor. A refusal count landing on an independently counted population is the strongest evidence available here that the check is refusing the right records rather than an arbitrary subset. **The dominant kind is `__finally`, by an order of magnitude** — 31 of 34 entries on t64 — which is the fact A1's rule has to be shaped around; only 3 entries per binary are `__except`, and exactly one of those needs no filter funclet.

  - **THE TWO CORPUS-LEVEL NEGATIVE CONTROLS ARE INERT, AND THE MECHANISM IS REDUNDANCY RATHER THAN AN IDLE CHECK.** Reported, not tuned away. `peek-a-bin-j4uk.4`'s brief predicted that removing check (1) would make the `/GS` records "start producing enormous bogus tables" and jump the validated count by roughly 18; **measured, the census does not move at all**, and neither does it for check (2). Instrumenting which check refuses each record explains it: of t64's 20 refusals, **12 are refused by check (1)'s extent test, 6 by check (2), and 2 by check (3)** — so each check *is* reached and used, but every refused record fails several of them, and removing one lets the next catch it. With check (2) removed the exact 6 records it had refused are caught by check (4)'s handler test instead, 12 + 6 + 2 = 20 either way. The `/GS` population splits by cookie-offset magnitude: a large offset (t64 carries 1072, 1504, 1936, 2096, 2784) claims a table tens of times larger than `.xdata` and dies at the extent test, while a small one (0x30 = 48 entries) fits and is refused for its regions.
  - **So the discriminating form of every control is a unit test over a crafted fixture, and all twelve discriminate.** `src/pe/__tests__/pdata.test.ts`'s fixture puts `.xdata` in a buffer four times its own length precisely so the *section* bound can be shown to be the operative one. Removing check (1) entirely reddens 2 rows; its extent half alone 1; its `Count >= 1` half alone 1; check (2) 2; its containment half alone 1; check (3) 1; making check (3) **strict** 1 (nested `__try` blocks sharing a `begin` are ordinary output); check (4)'s handler test 1; dropping check (4)'s `handler == 1` exemption 1; its `jumpTarget` test 1; replacing the section bound with the buffer 1; and publishing `[]` instead of `undefined` on refusal reddens **11**, which is the semantic inversion the field's docstring exists to forbid. In `malformed.test.ts`, dropping `MAX_SCOPE_TABLE_ENTRIES` reddens the cap row and dropping the section bound **hangs the run**.
  - **TWO CONTROLS CAME BACK INERT DURING CONSTRUCTION AND THE FIXTURES WERE SHARPENED RATHER THAN THE CONTROLS DROPPED.** The extent test's first fixture filled its overrun with `0x1010` words, so the degenerate-region test refused it too and removing the extent test moved nothing — the fixture now writes `Count` regions that each pass checks (2), (3) and (4), so only the extent can refuse. And the hostile fixture first sized its buffer to `.xdata`, so the buffer refused the same read and the section bound could have been deleted with every row green; the buffer now holds the whole claimed table while `xdataBytes` sizes only the section. A third, separate trap worth recording: at 100,000 records a fixed `.pdata` RVA of 0x100000 has a 1.2 MB virtual extent that **swallows** an `.xdata` at 0x200000, so the record's RVA resolved inside `.pdata` and the `UNWIND_INFO` read the test was about never happened. The RVAs are derived from the sizes now.
  - **Output is byte-identical BY CONSTRUCTION: nothing consumes `scopeTable`.** That is the deliverable rather than a weak result — it is what lets the census be trusted and lets the consumer land as its own change. `npm run corpus` 27/27 gates over four binaries with emitted C identical for every function (t32 260/260, t64 279/279, w32 258/258, w64 275/275) and `guards: 733 -> 733, CHANGED 0`; `corpus:parserdiff` 98/118 with 20 vacuous and 0 red.
  - **What a validated table does NOT establish.** It is *structurally* a `__C_specific_handler` table, not provably one: nothing here reads the handler's code, and a `__GSHandlerCheck_SEH` record carries a genuine scope table followed by a cookie offset the check neither reaches nor needs. Conversely a withheld table is not evidence that a function has no `__try` — it is the ordinary answer for every `/GS`-only and C++ record in the image, which is 18 of t64's 50. The ARM64 path is untouched: an ARM64 `.xdata` record has one "has a handler" bit and no `__C_specific_handler` convention behind it, so `scopeTable` is x64's alone.
- **A CARET THAT OPENS ONTO NOTHING, A BLOB URL NOBODY REVOKES, AND A COLUMN DRESSED AS A LINK — three independent defects in `ResourcesView.tsx`, each landable alone and each with a control that reddens exactly its own rows.** They share only a file. The reason they are one entry is that all three are the same *kind* of defect one level below the parser-admission family above: the pane's OUTPUT is honest and its CONTROLS lie about what they will do (`peek-a-bin-v3uh.14`).
  - **THE CARET. Every leaf row renders an expand control and `ExpandedLeaf` had arms for THREE of the ~20 types the format defines** — RT_VERSION, RT_GROUP_ICON, RT_MANIFEST — and `return null`ed the rest. So clicking the caret on an RT_BITMAP, RT_STRING, RT_DIALOG or RT_RCDATA flipped the arrow to its open state and rendered an empty `<tr><td colSpan={5}>`: the control moved, nothing appeared, and the reader has no way to tell "this type has no preview" from "the pane is broken". Those four are most of what an ordinary binary carries, so the *unhandled* case is the common one. The fallback is a 16-byte-per-row hex/ASCII dump with resource-relative offsets — resource-relative because nothing else on this tab shows a file offset and the RVA beside it is what the Hex tab wants.
  - **IT GOES THROUGH `resourceBytes`, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE OF WHY THE `RangeError` CANNOT COME BACK.** `peek-a-bin-p0qw` found `rvaToFileOffset` answering an offset past the end on a truncated image — it resolves against the SECTION TABLE and never sees the buffer — so `Math.min(size, byteLength - fileOff)` goes negative and `new Uint8Array` throws, taking the pane into its `ErrorBoundary` on a click while the tree itself walks fine. The fallback is the one arm every unhandled type reaches, so a hand-rolled bound here would have reintroduced that defect across the whole population instead of one type. A row drives a genuinely truncated fixture — the file cut so the section table still maps the leaf and the buffer does not contain its first byte — through the real component and asserts the sentence, not merely the absence of a throw.
  - **THE CAP IS ADMITTED ON THE COUNT LINE, never a silent clip.** 256 bytes, printed as `first 256 of 300 bytes` and as a plain `256 bytes` at exactly the cap — both sides of the boundary pinned, on `peek-a-bin-6qx9`'s model, since the off-by-one that reports a complete answer as short is the same defect mirrored. A preview that just stopped would read as a 256-byte resource, which the Size column two cells away contradicts. A THIRD number is printed where the cut lands *inside* a leaf: `resourceBytes` clamps to the buffer, so `bytes.length` and the directory's declared `size` are two different true facts and the line says both.
  - **AND `resourceBytes`' REFUSAL NOW PRINTS A SENTENCE, WITH THE TWO REASONS HELD APART.** Its return became `{ bytes } | { bytes: null, reason }` — `unmapped` (the RVA falls in no section: a malformed directory) versus `past-end` (the section table maps it and the FILE is short of it: a truncated image). Two facts, and the old bare `null` collapsed them into the same empty row an *unhandled type* produced, so one blank `<tr>` stood for three unrelated things. The reason is carried out of the GUARD rather than re-derived at the call site, because a second `rvaToFileOffset` comparison elsewhere is a second declaration of the bound and is exactly the copy that drifts back into the `RangeError`. `UNREADABLE_SENTENCE` is a `Record` over the union, on `DETECT_PASS_LABELS`' model, so a third reason fails the build.
  - **The guard sits ABOVE RT_VERSION deliberately.** `parseVersionInfo` bounds its own reads, so on an unreachable resource it answered `{}` and the arm printed "No version strings found" — a positive claim about the RESOURCE resting on the tool's failure to reach its bytes, which is `peek-a-bin-wo8g`'s class one level down from the directory. The RT_GROUP_ICON arm stays OUTSIDE it, and that asymmetry is the recorded one: `buffer.slice` CLAMPS where `new Uint8Array(buf, off, len)` throws, so a past-the-end group icon yields an empty buffer and `reconstructIcon` answers null — the honest "Could not reconstruct icon" rather than the truncation sentence. A row asserts it, so routing that arm through `resourceBytes` "for consistency" fails.
  - **THE BLOB URL. `URL.createObjectURL` was called in the middle of RENDER and `URL.revokeObjectURL` appeared nowhere in the file** — so every render of an expanded RT_GROUP_ICON pinned another blob for the lifetime of the document, and this pane re-renders on every collapse, expand and download click. Minting during render is wrong on its own terms too: a render React throws away (a concurrent retry, StrictMode's double invoke) leaks a URL no cleanup can ever see, because no effect ever ran for it. `GroupIconPreview` is its own component precisely so it has a cleanup: it memoises the reconstruction (so the effect key is stable across parent re-renders that change no bytes), mints in a `useEffect` keyed on those bytes, and revokes in the return.
  - **jsdom implements NEITHER function, which is exactly why the arm had never been rendered by any test** — the suite's own docblock had named `RT_GROUP_ICON` as a gap for that reason. Both are installed file-wide as counting stand-ins, and **the instrument is the PAIRING, not either count**: one revoke per create across expand → collapse → unmount, two cycles, because a create count alone passes against the defect (which minted one on the first render) and a revoke count alone passes against a component revoking a URL it never minted. A separate row pins that a re-render mints nothing further, which is the leak itself.
  - **NOTHING PAINTS, AND THE CONTROL CONFIRMS IT RATHER THAN THE DOCSTRING ASSERTING IT.** Replacing the blob's contents with a single 0x00 byte of `text/plain` — not an icon by any reading — leaves every row in the file green. jsdom decodes no image and has no 2D context, so `<img src="blob:…">` is an element with an attribute; whether the reconstructed `.ico` is a valid icon is measured by `resources.test.ts` over the bytes and by nothing here. The human check stays in `peek-a-bin-v2u`.
  - **THE RVA COLUMN was `text-blue-400` monospace inside a plain `<td>`,** and had been since the tab was written. Every other blue address in this app is clickable — `SectionTable`'s rows, `HeaderView`'s `CopyableHex`, every operand in the listing — so this one taught a reader that the colour means nothing. An affordance that is not one is worse than plain text. It dispatches `SET_ADDRESS` to `imageBase + rva` and then `SET_TAB "hex"`, address first because `HexView` derives its section from `state.currentAddress` alone, so the tab that mounts already has what it needs and its own scroll-to-offset effect does the rest. **The Hex tab and not the disassembly**: `.rsrc` is data, and a linear sweep of resource bytes is invented code.
  - **Five controls, four discriminating and ONE INERT AND REPORTED.** Restore `return null` for unhandled types → 9 rows, all in the hex-fallback family. Restore `return null` for the unreadable arms → 4 rows, all unreadable-arm rows (two of them pre-existing rows rewritten by this change). Remove the effect cleanup → 1 row, the pairing; restore the whole original defect (mint during render, never revoke) → 2, the pairing plus the re-render row. Revert the RVA cell to a `<td>` → 3 rows, all `getByRole("button")` on that cell. **INERT: anything asserting the icon PAINTS**, above. No control crossed from one sub-change into another, which is the property that makes the three separately landable.
  - **Two rows had PINNED THE DEFECT AS THE RULE**, both `expect(rows[1].textContent).toBe("")` — one for an unhandled type ("renders no preview at all for a type with no preview arm") and one for the truncated leaf `p0qw` found, whose comment described the empty row as the fix. A third asserted the empty box for an RVA in no section. All three now assert the sentence.
  - **Out of scope and left alone, deliberately**: this tab has no filter box where Imports, Exports and Strings all have one, and RT_STRING string tables are not decoded (the fallback prints their bytes, which is honest but is not the resource). Neither is a defect in what the pane says; both are filed rather than done.

- **A `__try` IS NOW EMITTED ONLY WHERE THE IMAGE SAYS ONE EXISTS, AND ALL THREE OF THE THINGS THE OLD WRAPPER SAID WERE UNREAD.** `wrapExceptionRegions` (`disasm/decompile/pipeline.ts`) fired on any record `funcExceptionRecord` selected and printed a whole-function `__try` with `__except(EXCEPTION_EXECUTE_HANDLER)` — three claims, none of them read out of the file (`peek-a-bin-j4uk.5`). Now that `peek-a-bin-j4uk.4` reads the scope table, each can be replaced by a reading or withdrawn. Measured at `4167aa3`: **t64's `__try` count goes 50 → 3 and w64's 46 → 3.**
  - **Claim 1, "a try region exists", fired on `EHANDLER | UHANDLER`.** UHANDLER is `__finally`, and `__GSHandlerCheck` — a stack-cookie check with **no `__try` in the source at all** — sets EHANDLER. The rule is now a validated scope table holding at least one `__except` entry. **18 of t64's 50 handler-bearing records are that `/GS` shape** and 2 more carry language-specific data no table could be read out of; all 20 were wrapped, and all 20 now emit nothing (w64: 16 + 2 of 46).
  - **A record with no validated table produces NOTHING, NOT EVEN AN ADMISSION, and that asymmetry is the one judgement here that runs against the house habit.** Everywhere else in this file a narrowed answer must say so. It must not here, because a withheld table is the **ordinary** answer for a `/GS` or `__CxxFrameHandler3` record — `readScopeTable`'s own docstring says so — and those functions have no guarded region to admit. "A guarded region here could not be read" would be the fabrication all over again, wearing an admission's clothes.
  - **Claim 2, "the region covers the whole function", is not merely unproven — it is WRONG IN 100% OF CASES, and that is measured rather than argued.** Not one of t64's 34 scope entries or w64's 32 covers its record's extent; every single one is strictly narrower (e.g. `sub_140004104` spans `0x140004104-0x14000427B` and guards `0x1400041B8-0x140004257`). Placing the extent onto statements is a separate piece of work — a region need not be a contiguous run of top-level statements — so the wrap is still the whole body, but the braces no longer *imply* the extent: the regions are named in the body and a line beside them says the braces are not the region. **The `__except` body is itself inside those braces** in every corpus case, `jumpTarget` being the region's own `end`, which is exactly why the admission is needed rather than nice to have.
  - **Claim 3, "the filter is EXCEPTION_EXECUTE_HANDLER", was `emit.ts`'s unconditional fallback, and NOTHING IN PRODUCTION HAS EVER CONSTRUCTED A `filterExpr`** — all nine `src/` references are pass-through copies or reads, and the only constructors are in tests. So that literal was printed on every `__except` the tool had ever emitted. It is now printed for exactly one reason, `handler === 1`, the format's own spelling of it, and the emitted C says where it came from; **that is 1 entry per binary out of 34 and 32**, so the path is real and tiny. Everything else is `unrecoveredValue`'s ordinary `__unrecovered_N` admission, naming the filter *routine* where the table gave one — knowing where a filter lives is not knowing what it returns, and nothing here decompiles the funclet. **Absence of filter information is deliberately the unrecovered case and never the constant**, or the fallback is back.
  - **`__finally` IS RECORDED AND NOT SPELLED, AND IT IS THE DOMINANT KIND BY AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE — 31 of t64's 34 entries, 29 of w64's 32.** A UHANDLER-only record used to print `__except(EXCEPTION_EXECUTE_HANDLER)`, a different construct with different control flow. The IR has no `__finally` statement kind and adding one is the whole "Adding new IRExpr / IRStmt kinds" checklist (`peek-a-bin-fcgu`), so such a record gets a leading `/* .pdata: __finally region 0x… - 0x…; termination handler at 0x… */` and no construct. That is `peek-a-bin-wo8g`'s move — say what was read without claiming what was not — and **any design that handles only `__except` is a design for 9% of the population**. A `handler` of 0 or 1 on a `__finally` entry names no funclet and the clause is omitted rather than resolved.
  - **A CORROBORATION THE A0 CENSUS DID NOT STATE, AND IT IS THE STRONGEST EVIDENCE HERE THAT THE TABLE IS BEING READ CORRECTLY: the `UNWIND_INFO` FLAGS BYTE AND THE SCOPE TABLE AGREE EXACTLY.** Re-derived at `4167aa3`: over all 30 validating t64 records and all 28 w64 ones, "UHANDLER without EHANDLER" and "every entry has `jumpTarget === 0`" are the **same set, with zero disagreements**. Those are two independent readings — one bit in the unwind header, one field in language-specific data the header does not describe — so their agreement is not something a mis-parse would produce. (A0's census rows are a *partition*, not orthogonal counts: its "UHANDLER-only 2" is the 2 UHANDLER-only records that did **not** validate; the other 27 are inside its "validates 30". Counted orthogonally t64 has 29 UHANDLER-only records. Both readings are right; the labels invite the mistake.)
  - **A FIFTH UNREAD CLAIM, found while writing the fourth: the handler comment named the CRT dispatcher.** The old handler body printed `Exception handler at 0x…` from `rf.handlerAddress` — which is the **language-specific handler**, i.e. `__C_specific_handler` itself. Measured: **50 t64 records carry just 2 distinct handler RVAs** (`0x1400043DC`, `0x140007C00`; w64 `0x14000476C`, `0x1400074CC`). It is not a fact about the function, so it is no longer printed; the handler block names the `__except` body instead. The RVA→VA rebase that `peek-a-bin-yrh` exists for is unaffected and still pinned — every scope-table address goes through the same recovered image base, and the fixtures assert it.
  - **CORPUS, at `4167aa3` against `base-0870e14`: t32 and w32 BYTE-IDENTICAL THROUGHOUT** (PE32 has no `.pdata`, so they are the control), **t64 `emitted C identical 229/279` and w64 `229/275`** — exactly the 50 and 46 handler-bearing records and no other function. **Every gate flat on all four**: `functions` 260/279/258/275, `instructions` 18025/16844/16601/15111, `jumpTables`, `throws`, `polarity inverted`, `polarity mismatch`, `loops short of machine`, `distinct callees lost`, `arity over` all unmoved at 0, `gcc clean` 260/260, 279/279, 258/258, 275/275, `offsetof` 393/282/309/287 unmoved. **`guard lines unparsed` 0 and — the row `peek-a-bin-vwr5` exists for — `polarity guards audited` EXACTLY FLAT at 521/577/445/500 with `braced` 1532/1500/1348/1312, `brace-less` 572/550/515/512 and `do/while tails` 78/77/77/76 all unmoved, and `guards … CHANGED 0, only-base 0, only-change 0`.** `corpus/sweep.ts`'s `OPENER` and `corpus/guardShape.ts`'s `BODY_IS_OPENER` both name `__try`, so removing 47 of them per binary could have taken guards out of the audited population with the numerator unmoved; it did not, and the flat denominator is what says so.
  - **EXACTLY THREE ROWS MOVE ON EACH x64 BINARY AND ZERO ON EACH PE32 ONE** (counted mechanically over the whole comparison, not spot-checked): `unrecovered values` **+2** (t64 12 → 14, w64 11 → 13), its `token occurrences` **+4** (24 → 28, 22 → 26 — two tokens, each printed at its use and at its declaration), and `emitted lines scanned` **−148 / −134**, which is the net of 47 removed wrapper line-pairs against the region comments added. **The first is flagged `<<< REGRESSION` by `compare.mjs` mechanically.** It is the opposite: CLAUDE.md's standing reading of that row is that "a rise can be a refusal replacing a confident wrong answer", and **the rise is arithmetically predicted** — 3 `__except` records per binary minus the 1 whose filter the table actually spelled. `of which branch guards` is unmoved at 12, so the new tokens are correctly not counted as guards. Emitted-C evidence agrees exactly with the census on every count: **3 `__try` blocks, 30 functions carrying a region comment, 31 `__finally` + 3 `__except` regions = the 34 entries** on t64 (w64: 3 / 28 / 29 + 3 = 32), and 0 of everything on the PE32 pair.
  - **gcc HAS NEVER CHECKED THIS CONSTRUCT AND STILL DOES NOT — measured, not reasoned, and DO NOT CITE "all of them compile" AS EVIDENCE ABOUT ANYTHING HERE.** `corpus/emitAudits.ts`'s `CC_HEADER` is `#define __try` and `#define __except(x) if (0)`, so the try block compiles as a bare compound statement and the handler as `if (0)` — and the macro **discards its argument**, so nothing inside `__except(...)` reaches the compiler at all. Verified directly against gcc 15.2.0: a totally undeclared identifier, and an argument consisting only of a comment, both compile silently under `-std=gnu89 -fsyntax-only` **with no diagnostic even without `-w`**. Two consequences. The bead predicted a comment-only argument would be a gnu89 pedwarn: it is not. And it predicted an `__unrecovered_filter` identifier would show up in the **invented prelude declarations** instrument: it would not, because `preludeFor` reads gcc's "undeclared" diagnostics and gcc never sees the identifier — measured, that row is unmoved at 1460/2456/1417/2278. Using `unrecoveredValue` instead puts the admission in the **unrecovered-values** row, which is a landed instrument built for exactly this reading, and declares it as a real local so the C stays valid.
  - **SEVEN NEGATIVE CONTROLS, ALL SEVEN DISCRIMINATING — none inert, which was NOT the expected texture.** `peek-a-bin-j4uk.4`'s two corpus-level controls came back inert because its four structural checks are mutually redundant on well-formed output, and the same was expected here. It did not happen, because these are unit tests over crafted fixtures where each rule is the only thing standing between the fixture and the assertion. (1) Wrap any selected record with no table → the headline row alone. (2) Wrap a `__finally`-only record → 2 rows. (3) Restore the `EXCEPTION_EXECUTE_HANDLER` fallback → **8 rows**. (4) Drop the extent admission → 1. (5) Drop the `handler === 1` exemption and resolve 1 as an RVA → 1. (6) Make one pass rebuild `IRTry` by enumerating its fields instead of `...stmt`, dropping `filterSource` → **6 rows**, which is the control that protects the plumbing rule. (7) Treat every entry as `__except` → 3 rows.
  - **`corpus/decompileRpcCost.ts`'s liveness half had to be re-based, and NOT the way the bead suggested.** `peek-a-bin-qmlz`'s equivalence census counted functions emitting a `__try` — 50 and 46, which was also the handler-bearing row count — precisely because that was the whole observable effect of `runtimeFunctions`. At 3 per binary the row is too thin to protect anything, and it is now every function carrying **any** `.pdata`-derived text (`__try` or a region comment): **30 on t64, 28 on w64, equal under both payloads, 0 and labelled vacuous on the PE32 pair**, with `EQUIVALENCE 0 of 279` and `0 of 275` unchanged. The bead's suggestion — re-base it on the handler-bearing **row** count — was **refused**: that is a property of the image, and a liveness half for an *emitted-C* equivalence census has to be a property of the emitted C, or it stays comfortably non-zero while saying nothing about whether the slice preserved anything.
  - **A SECOND LIVENESS HALF WENT VACUOUS AND THE SUITE CAUGHT IT, WHICH IS THE SYSTEM WORKING.** `src/workers/__tests__/disasmClient.test.ts`'s "gives the same emitted C as the whole table did, and there is a `__try` to keep" is `peek-a-bin-qmlz`'s non-vacuity assertion in unit form, and its fixture rows carried a handler flag and no scope table — the `/GS` shape — so it went red the moment the new rule landed. That is the assertion doing its job: without a `__try` on either side the equivalence it checks would have been between two empty answers. A's row now carries a validated one-entry `__except` scope table, and the docstring says a handler flag alone is no longer enough.
  - **DO NOT MOVE ANY OF THIS INTO `funcExceptionRecord`, AND DO NOT "TIDY" ITS `& 0x3` FILTER TO MATCH THE NEW RULE.** That filter is a **selector**, and `peek-a-bin-qmlz`'s soundness argument is that the client and the worker apply the *same* rule and that it is idempotent — narrowing it re-opens that for no gain, since the consumer is what decides what may be claimed. Selecting the record and judging it are two questions. `wrapExceptionRegions`' docstring says so at the top, because the invitation to tidy is strong.
  - **Still not done, and named rather than implied.** The extent is not placed onto statements. `__finally` has no spelling (`peek-a-bin-fcgu`). A record carrying two `__except` entries gets one `__try` driven by the first in address order with every region named — no such record exists in this corpus, so that path is fixture-only. Nothing renders any of this, and the emitted `__try` has never been read by a human in the browser panel.

- **A FUNCTION'S RETURN TYPE IS DECIDED OVER THE WHOLE STRUCTURED TREE, AND THE HAND-WRITTEN TRAVERSAL THAT DECIDED IT COULD NOT SEE INSIDE A `__try`.** `promote.ts:859` was `hasReturnValue(finalBody) ? "int" : "void"`, and `hasReturnValue` recursed into `if` (`thenBody`/`elseBody`), `while` and `do_while` — and nothing else. A valued `return` inside a `for`, a `switch` arm, a `switch` default, a `__try` body or its handler was invisible, so the header said `void` above a body that said `return rax;` (`peek-a-bin-n9cl.2`). The fix is the one already prescribed by this file's `bodiesOf` entry: iterate `ir.ts`'s exhaustive declaration of which statement kinds hold nested lists, so a new body-carrying kind fails the build instead of silently narrowing the traversal.
  - **The population at 6299113 is 3/3, NOT the 48/44 the bead recorded, and the difference is a fixed defect, not a miscount.** The bead's figure was taken when `wrapExceptionRegions` wrapped every handler-bearing `.pdata` record (48 t64 / 44 w64 emitted `__try`s); `peek-a-bin-j4uk.5` narrowed that to records with a validated `__except` scope entry, which is exactly 3 per x64 binary here — and all 3 have a valued return, so the void-header count and the `__try` count are the same 3. Both halves measured over `funcs_*.jsonl` at the base run `s29-base-6299113`: void headers with a valued `return` 0/3/3/0 on t32/t64/w64/w32, of 260/279/275/258 functions; t64 has 9 void headers in all. After: 0/0/0/0. Liveness: non-void headers with a valued return 253/270/266/250 → 253/273/269/250.
  - **gcc saw none of it.** `corpus/emitAudits.ts` runs `gcc -std=gnu89 -fsyntax-only`, and a valued return in a void function is a *warning* under gnu89, not an error; the `cc` gate reads `260/260 clean` on both sides. `voidReturnsValue` (built in `peek-a-bin-n9cl.1`, in parallel) is the gate that can see it.
  - **`emit.ts`'s `headerReturnType` widens the `int`, and the whole of its evidence is LOCAL.** After `foldReturnedCallResults` — `emitFunction`'s first act, so `eax = GetLastError(); return eax;` has already become `return GetLastError();` — if EVERY valued `return` in the tree is a call whose callee is in `API_TYPES` and every such signature spells the same `typeToString`, the header spells that type. Anything else refuses back to `int`: a returned register, a returned `sub_…()`, two API callees disagreeing, or an API whose declared type is `void` (`return free(p);` under a `void` header is the header/body disagreement this entry exists to end, one level up). Corpus: 6 headers widened, one per binary plus one more on each x64 binary, **every one `return TerminateProcess(…)` → `BOOL`**, which is the right answer (`t32!sub_402C35`, `t64!sub_14000307C`, `t64!sub_140004290`, `w64!sub_1400033E8`, `w64!sub_140004620`, `w32!sub_402E89`).
  - **Two inferences REFUSED, on evidence rather than taste.** Width: `lifter.ts` names the returned accumulator at `is64 ? 8 : 4`, so on x64 every returned register reads 8 bytes whatever the function returns — telling `int` from `int64_t` needs the SSA definition widths, a different piece of work. Pointer-ness from call sites: interprocedural and flow-insensitive after `destroySSA`. Both are follow-ons, recorded here so the next reader does not spend a session rediscovering that the local evidence is not there.
  - **Four negative controls, all four discriminating.** Reverting `hasReturnValue` to the three-kind recursion reddens exactly 4 rows — the three new `promote.test.ts` rows (for/switch/try; default arm and handler; after a label and three constructs deep) and `pipeline.test.ts`'s end-to-end `__try` row — and nothing else. Deleting `headerReturnType`'s agreement check reddens exactly the two disagreement rows (`emit.test.ts` and the end-to-end two-API row). The `promote.test.ts` row that had pinned the defect as a KNOWN BUG (`toBe("void") // should be int`) is the same fixture with the expectation corrected, so the suite carried the oracle before the fix did.
  - **Corpus at 6299113, `s29-a1b1-6299113` against `s29-base-6299113`: 18 functions changed, every one read.** 6 are this entry's `void` → `int` headers (t64 `sub_140004104`, `sub_14000B050`, `sub_14000CFA8`; w64 `sub_140004470`, `sub_140005144`, `sub_140005A20`), 6 are the `int` → `BOOL` headers above, 6 are the thunk bodies of the next entry. Every change is a header line or a thunk body; `compare.mjs` reads **VERDICT: no regression**, guards `CHANGED 0` on all four binaries, and no numeric pair in the comparison differs — checked mechanically over every `a -> b` in `compare.mjs`'s output, not spot-read.
- **AN IMPORT THUNK IS NAMED AFTER ITS CALLEE, AND THE LIFTER THEN CALLED THE FUNCTION BY THAT NAME — `RtlVirtualUnwind() { return RtlVirtualUnwind(); }`.** `functionDetect.ts:2703-2735` renames a ≤16-byte function whose single meaningful instruction is `jmp [IAT slot]` to `iat.func` and sets `fn.isThunk`; the lifter's tail-jmp path (`liftBlock`, via `resolveNamedTarget`) resolves the same RIP-relative slot through the same `iatMap` to the same name. Both readings are right in isolation. The collision is naming the function after its callee, and it is **not** an ordering defect in `resolveCallTarget` — reordering the lookups changes nothing, because there is only one name to find. 3 per x64 binary at 6299113 (`RtlVirtualUnwind`, `RtlLookupFunctionEntry`, `RtlUnwindEx`, all `KERNEL32.dll`; 0 on either PE32 binary at this baseline), none of which recurses (`peek-a-bin-n9cl.2`).
  - **The fix spells what the instruction does.** `jmp [slot]` is an indirect transfer through the slot's contents, and `emit.ts`'s `calleeText` already had the spelling for a call through a named value: a target of `(*__imp_X)` prints as `((intptr_t (*)())__imp_X)(…)`. `importThunkTransfer` (`lifter.ts`, beside `resolveNamedTarget`) hands the tail path that target plus a `comment` statement — `import thunk: jmp through the IAT slot for KERNEL32.dll!RtlVirtualUnwind` — so the body reads `return ((intptr_t (*)())__imp_RtlVirtualUnwind)();` under `int RtlVirtualUnwind() {`. `display` is left unset on that call deliberately: `emitExpr` prefers `display`'s `!`-suffix as the callee name, so carrying `lib!func` there would print the self-call back.
  - **Two admissions, either sufficient, and BOTH require the target to be an IAT slot.** `self.isThunk === true` (detection's word) or `tail.name === self.name` (the collision itself), where `self` is the function `liftBlock` now receives as its eleventh parameter (`pipeline.ts` passes `func`; every other caller and every fixture passes nothing, and nothing changes for them). The slot requirement is what `resolveNamedTarget`'s new `imported` field carries: a direct `jmp 0x…` to a same-named `sub_…` has no slot to spell and stays the call it was — pinned by the `helper` row. Widening the admission to *every* IAT tail jmp was run as a control and reddens 2 rows (`names the import a tail jmp through the IAT reaches`, `leaves a differently named, unmarked function's tail jmp as the call it is`); the fix absent reddens 3 (both admissions and the 32-bit `jmp dword ptr [slot]` form). Both controls discriminate.
  - **`__imp_<name>` IS ONE DECLARATION, in `lifter.ts`: `IMPORT_SLOT_PREFIX` and `importSlotName(func)`.** It is also what MSVC's linker calls the same slot. Epic `peek-a-bin-n9cl`'s B2 (globals and imports) adopts it for every IAT-slot LOAD — `mov rax, [rip + X]` with `X` in `iatMap` — and must spell it through this function rather than a second template; it lives in the lifter because that is where both readers of the slot are. `corpus/sweep.ts`'s `emittedCallees` imports the prefix and credits `__imp_X` as a mention of `X` — the ONLY standing gate this change touches, and in that one way: its `CALL_RE` is `ident\s*\(`, and `__imp_X)(` has a `)` between the name and the `(`, so without the credit `distinct callees lost` would report 3 red rows per x64 binary for callees that are on the page. With it: `0 of 947 / 879 / 842 / 912` on t32/t64/w64/w32, denominators unmoved. `corpus/arity.ts` is unaffected for the same reason in the other direction — `intptr_t (` matches its regex but is not in `API_TYPES`, and the thunk's *callers* still print `RtlVirtualUnwind(...)`, so its population is unchanged.
  - **A `comment` statement emitted by the LIFTER is new, and it survived every pass unchanged** — the previous constructors were all post-structuring (`pipeline.ts`'s `wrapExceptionRegions`, `structure.ts`'s lost-branch note). `ssa.ts` and `ssadestroy.ts` list the kind explicitly; `ssaopt.ts` and `fold.ts` never name it and treat it as they treat every kind they do not name, which the end-to-end rows confirm is pass-through. `raw` was the alternative and was refused: it prints `/* unlifted: … */;`, and the instruction here IS lifted. Measured: `self-recursive thunks` (one-statement body `return <own name>(`) 0/3/3/0 → 0/0/0/0; `__imp_`-bearing functions 0/0/0/0 → 0/3/3/0; each thunk grows by exactly the comment line (3 → 4 lines).
  - **Not done, and named.** No caller of a thunk is told it is calling through an import — they print `RtlVirtualUnwind(...)` exactly as before, which is right (the name resolves to the thunk, which resolves to the import). The header's return type for a thunk stays `int`: the callee text is `(*__imp_X)`, not an `API_TYPES` name, so the previous entry's widening does not fire on it, and `RtlVirtualUnwind` is not in `API_TYPES` in any case. Whether a thunk should be emitted at all — rather than folded into its callers as a name — is epic B2's question.
- **REGISTERS ARE DECLARED VARIABLES — one C variable per canonical register per function, at emit, and every alias is spelled through it (`peek-a-bin-n9cl.4`).** Every register, split repair (`ecx_3`), `clobbered_<reg>_<n>`, `tmp_xchg`, `st0` and `stk_<addr>` used to reach the page undeclared, one free variable per NAME: `dx` and `edx` were two C variables for one register, t64's `wcslen` tested a name nothing assigned (`peek-a-bin-uxm`), and `cc` read 1072/1072 clean only because `preludeFor` invented a `long` per name — 979/1072 functions, 7,519 pairs. `registerVariables` (`emit.ts`) declares the widest alias mentioned, **capped at `regAtSize(canon, 4)` on PE32** (the cap is what keeps `unencodableNames` at 0 — a red row there is the cap catching a leak), typed from `typeInfer` only when the widths agree so no register is ever a pointer. Reads: `(uint16_t)edx`, `(uint8_t)(eax >> 8)`. Writes put the truncation IN the expression: `rax = (uint32_t)e` (x64, 32-bit — zero-extension), `eax = (eax & ~0xFF) | (uint8_t)e`. `IRFunction.is64` comes from `promoteVars`, never inferred from the body (`peek-a-bin-0s6e`). Residue counted, not declared: `stk_`, `tmp_xchg`, `st0`, `xmm`. Gate: `undeclaredIdentifiers.register + minted === 0` — 1460/2443/2265/1417 → 0 at `2328657`, control 1518/2612/2419/1471. Full record, hand-readings and the `<<<` adjudication in `docs/decompiler-ir.md`.
  - **It broke exactly one thing, and the thing it broke was a spelling device doing a variable's job.** `peek-a-bin-pzws` had kept `r9` and `r9d` apart as two C variables for two live ranges; one variable per register makes the 32-bit range's lowered phi copy `r9 = (uint32_t)r14d_1` a write of the pointer six stores read. `corpus/staleReads.ts` — `writes` now by canonical register, the only correct test when the identifier IS the register — went red at 6/6 per x64 binary. Fixed where the audit always pointed: `splitStaleReads` now attributes a phi's write to each **predecessor** where `destroySSA` emits the copy (`defBlocks`) and hands a predecessor's still-entry exit state the phi's version for its non-phi successors (`phiCopiesOut`), so the version-0 rule takes its one entry copy (`r9_0 = r9;`). Both halves negative-controlled in `pipeline.test.ts`; one new entry copy per affected function corpus-wide.
  - **The declaration block is not statements.** Three text scans counted `int64_t rsp;` / `int64_t clobbered_rcx_4;` / a declaration line as a read or a statement; `stripDeclarationBlock` (`corpus/emitAudits.ts`) is the one declaration of the block's shape, recognised by BOTH halves (every line declaration-shaped AND a blank line closing it).
  - **Not taken on, measured**: width-blind constant/copy propagation puts `mov al, 5` on the page as `*(int32_t*)(edx) = 5` before emit sees a register — 100/105/94/91 sub-register writes beside a wider alias. Its own bead.
- **`regSize()` is not a membership test.** It falls back to `4` for any unrecognised name, so `regSize(x) > 0` is true for every string. Use `isKnownRegister()` (`decompile/ir.ts`). This exact mistake made `lifter.ts`'s `isRegister()` a no-op that lifted immediates as registers.
- **`RegState.defs` is keyed by the literal operand text, and that is deliberate — ask `wroteAnyAlias` instead of canonicalising the map.** The map stores the *expression* last written, and that expression has the operand's width, so a key of `rcx` would record `mov cl, 2`'s one byte as if it were eight; on x86-64 a byte write does not even clear the upper bits a 32-bit write does. But *arity* is a width-blind question — `mov ecx, 1 / call f` passes an argument in RCX exactly as `mov rcx, 1` does — and `collectArgs64` probed the literal 64-bit name, missed every sub-width setup, and broke out of the loop there. With no argument in the IR the write had no reader and DCE deleted it: `ExitProcess()` with the exit code gone, in valid well-typed C, 5 times in each x64 binary. `wroteAnyAlias` answers the width-blind question over the width-exact map (same shape as `invalidateCallerSaved`), and returns a boolean so the recorded expression can never be substituted at the call site — `peek-a-bin-urs` cannot come back through it. **The suite had pinned the defect as the rule** (`it("misses x64 arguments set up through 32-bit sub-registers")`, asserting `[]` under a KNOWN BUG comment), which is why nothing failed for as long as it stood. Measured: 156/279 t64 and 154/275 w64 functions changed emitted text, t32/w32 byte-identical (`collectArgs32` reads pushes, not `RegState`), and against `apitypes.ts`'s declared signatures — the only oracle here that can see arity, since `gcc -std=gnu89` accepts an implicit declaration at any arity — exact-arity calls went 64→90 and 70→96 with over-count unmoved at 3 (`peek-a-bin-qb2x`). **Those four figures are stamped at the qb2x measurement and detection has moved since; the audit is now `corpus/arity.ts` and HEAD's numbers are in the Verification section.** The instrument that produced 64→90 was lost with a scratch worktree and the claim was unrepeatable for a while (`peek-a-bin-02fa`) — when you build an oracle to verify a change, land the oracle.
- **A written fastcall register is not an argument if the block already SPENT it as an address index — and base and index are different evidence.** `collectArgs64` asks `RegState` only *whether* the block wrote RCX/RDX/R8/R9, which is equally true of a register the block computed for its own addressing: t64 0x14000B34B does `imul rcx, rcx, 0x58` / `and BYTE PTR [rax+rcx*1+8], 0xfe` / `call GetLastError`, and the emitted C passed an argument to an API declaring none. The discriminator is that the value has been **spent as an index**: a subscript, scaled by an element size, whose access has already been emitted. An address **base** proves nothing and must not be treated the same — `lea rdx, [rcx+0x10]` / `f(rcx, rdx)` is a pointer that *is* the argument. `liftBlock` notes index reads via `noteIndexReads` before dispatching each instruction, so a read-modify-write clears its own mark when it calls `regState.set`; `RegState.consumed` holds them and `invalidateCallerSaved` drops the volatile ones. **One exemption, and it is the prefix property rather than a patch:** an index read does not spend the register when the instruction's destination is a fastcall register *later* in the argument order — `mov rdx, QWORD PTR [rdx+rcx*8]` is argument two derived from argument one, and since arity is counted as a prefix, RDX being an argument already implies RCX is. Without it t64!sub_14000FCE7 emitted `sub_14000278C()` for a callee that reads both ECX and RDX, and the statements computing them were then deleted as dead — `peek-a-bin-qb2x`'s failure mode. **Four rules refuted by this same corpus, none of which may be re-tried:** *distance* and *dominance* from the write to the call (the filed hypothesis, refuted by every one of the six rows — `RegState` is per-block so the write always dominates, and it is two instructions from the call at 0x14000B35F and 0x14000369C); *any* read spends it (t64 0x14000FAF0 `mov DWORD PTR [rsp+0x20], r8d` spills R8 to the outgoing stack-argument area **because** it is also the register argument, and `CreateFileW` loses two of four); and any read from inside a memory operand spends it (t64 0x14000BD6E `lea edx, [r9+0x8]` is MSVC computing the constant 9 from the 1 it just put in R9 — arithmetic wearing an address's clothes — and R9 is argument four of the `MultiByteToWideChar` two instructions later). Measured on base `7082e66`: arity over **3→0 on both x64 binaries**, exact 90→93/127 and 96→99/133, **under and both ceiling splits unmoved at 34 (26/8)**, x86 emitted C byte-identical, four functions changed per x64 binary, and every other audit — gcc, offsetof, polarity, loop exits, callees lost, statement drops, unrecovered values, stale reads, stale guards — unmoved. **The best evidence is again a callee `apitypes.ts` cannot see**: t64's `sub_140007808(expr, rdx)` and `sub_140007908(expr, rdx)` became one-argument calls, and both callees begin `movsxd r, ecx` and never read RDX. **Never answer this question from `apitypes.ts`** — same reason as below. `qb2x` is untouched: `wroteAnyAlias` still answers width-blind, and the 5 `ExitProcess` and 4 `Sleep` calls per x64 binary keep their arguments (`peek-a-bin-7r1l`).
- **`collectArgs32`'s backwards push-walk stops dead at a call whose result feeds a *following* call, and the marker is AFTER the call, not before it.** The shape is `call inner` / `push eax` / `call outer`: the inner call's result is pushed, so the inner call is an argument expression of the outer one and the pushes *above* it are the outer call's. Verified on t32.exe at 0x40e08b (`push ebx` / `push 0x8` / `call GetProcessHeap` / `push eax` / `call HeapAlloc`) and 0x402c4a (`push esi` / `call GetCurrentProcess` / `push eax` / `call TerminateProcess`) — **there is no call *between* the pushes and the inner call**, which is why the tempting rule "stop the walk at an intervening call boundary" would never fire once. 19 such marker sites on t32 and 18 on w32; ~7 and ~6 emitted calls actually lose arguments, the rest having had none to steal. It is deliberately an **admitted under-count, not a re-attribution**: handing the pushes to the outer call instead is a guess in the *over*-count direction, and an invented argument is the one error this codebase will not trade for a recovered one. In this corpus every callee the rule fires on takes zero arguments, so all of them land on `exact` — arity over went **8→4 on t32 and 10→6 on w32, exact 70→74/105 and 73→77/111**, x64 byte-identical (`collectArgs32` is the x86 path only). **The best evidence is a callee `apitypes.ts` cannot see**: t32's `sub_402283(edi, esi)` became `sub_402283()`, and that function is called with no preceding pushes at 10 of its 13 call sites, so it provably takes none — the two arguments were invented and no oracle here would ever have reported it. Costs, both benign and both measured: two struct shapes on each x86 binary stop merging (`distinctDefs` 57→59 and 56→58, per-preamble `fields` 358→348 and 413→403, **every layout still correct in both runs**), which renumbers `struct_N` across ~43 t32 and ~41 w32 functions whose text is otherwise unchanged (`peek-a-bin-f51x`).
- **A `push` of a callee-saved register the function has not yet written is a register SAVE, and that — not the register, not the position — is the whole discriminator.** `collectArgs32`'s backwards walk had no way to stop before the prologue, so it emitted `GetModuleHandleW("KERNEL32.DLL", edi)` for an API declaring one parameter and `GetCommandLineW(edi, esi, ebx)` for one declaring none. **Two tempting rules are refuted by this same corpus and neither may be re-tried.** "A push of ebx/esi/edi is a save" dies at t32 0x402c3f, where `push esi` *is* an argument. "A save is identifiable by position plus a matching `pop` before the `ret`" dies twice over: `push imm8 / pop reg` is a pervasive MSVC size idiom, so in t32's `sub_401A4E` — itself an over-counting site — *every* `pop ebx`/`pop edi` in the function is `mov reg, imm`; and the saves are often not at the entry, sitting six instructions in behind the allocation and the GS cookie at 0x401a4e, or *sunk* to a mid-function block leader reached by `jne` at 0x4086f1. What separates them is whether the pushed value is the register's **entry** value, decided at t32 0x402c35 where the same register in the same block is both — `push esi` (save) / `mov esi, 0xc0000417` / `push esi` (argument). `firstCalleeSavedWrites` (`lifter.ts`) precomputes the lowest address at which each of ebx/esi/edi/ebp is written and `decompileFunction` threads it through `liftBlock`; a push below its register's first write ends the walk. Three constraints are load-bearing: the scope is **function-wide** (w32 0x40104d is a genuine `push esi` argument at a block *leader*, with esi written in an earlier block), **`mov X, X` is not a definition** but `xor X, X` very much is (the hot-patch pad is the entry instruction of two of the four t32 rows; generalising the self-move test past `mov` withdrew t32 0x4068c6's zeroing and turned four real `Sleep(esi)` calls per x86 binary into `Sleep()`), and the rule is **restricted to the four callee-saved registers** — on cdecl and stdcall every incoming argument arrives on the stack, so their entry values are opaque to the callee and forwarding one is meaningless, which is what makes the rule sound rather than empirical on ten rows. The address-order approximation is deliberate and one-directional: a write laid out after a push that dynamically precedes it drops an argument, never invents one. Measured on base `91cca4f`: arity over **4→0 on t32 and 6→0 on w32**, exact 75→79/105 and 79→85/111, **under unmoved at 26 on both**, x64 emitted C byte-identical, and every other audit — gcc, offsetof fields *and* distinct definitions, polarity, loop exits, callees lost, statement drops, unrecovered values, stale reads — unmoved (`peek-a-bin-6lmh`). **Never answer this question from `apitypes.ts`.** It would drive over to 0 for exactly the callees `corpus/arity.ts` can see, making the audit measure its own input and blinding the only oracle here that can see call arity at all.
- **A DEFAULT PARAMETER IS A PLACE TWO CALLERS CAN DISAGREE, AND `layoutCFG(blocks, fontSize = 12)` IS WHERE THEY DID — a bug wearing a performance fix's clothes** (`peek-a-bin-v3uh.4`).
  - **The defect.** `src/disasm/cfg.ts`'s `layoutCFG` takes a font size with a default of 12 and sizes every dagre node from `getCfgLayout(fontSize)`: `BLOCK_WIDTH` is `round(320 * fontSize / 12)`, `INSN_HEIGHT` `round(14 * scale)`, `BLOCK_HEADER` `round(22 * scale)`, and a block's height is `max(BLOCK_MIN_HEIGHT, insns * INSN_HEIGHT + BLOCK_HEADER + 4)`. `CFGView` passed the real `loadFontSize()`. `DisassemblyView`'s sidebar-minimap memo — one screen away, on the same blocks, for the same function — **passed nothing**. So at any `--mono-font-size` other than 12 (the setting's range is 10-16) the `GraphOverviewData` published to the sidebar described a graph the panel was not drawing, and the pan/viewport arithmetic the overview canvas computes against those boxes was arithmetic over the wrong boxes. Nothing static could see it: both calls typecheck, both are one line, and no harness under `corpus/` renders React.
  - **The repair is the sharing, and the sharing CHANGES OUTPUT.** `DisassemblyView` now holds the single `graphLayout` memo, `layoutCFG(cfg, fontSize)`, gated on `viewMode === "graph" && currentFunc`, and `CFGView` takes `layout` as a prop. Minimap geometry at a non-default font size therefore **differs from before** — which is why the CHANGELOG files it under `### Fixed`, separately from the `### Changed` entry for the sharing itself. A change that alters what a user sees is not a refactor however it was reached.
  - **THE INSTRUMENT MUST BE PARAMETERISED OVER TWO FONT SIZES, and that is measured, not reasoned.** `layoutCFG(cfg)` and `layoutCFG(cfg, 12)` are the same call. Restoring the defect (drop the second argument) leaves a row asked at 12 **green** and reddens only the row asked at 16 — run and confirmed. A test at the default alone would be a test that passes by asking at the one value where the defect and the fix agree. Both rows sit in a `for` loop in `DisassemblyPanel.dom.test.tsx`, each with a liveness half asserting every published block's width equals `getCfgLayout(fontSize).BLOCK_WIDTH`, plus a third row asserting 12 and 16 produce different geometry — without which both rows could be agreeing about a layout that never consulted the font size.
  - **WHAT IS ASSERTABLE IS THE NUMBERS, NEVER THE PICTURE, and this control is EXPECTED INERT.** Any assertion that the minimap *looks* like the graph is unaskable here and was not written. jsdom performs no layout; `DisassemblyMinimap` is already named in CLAUDE.md's verification section as a component that "mounts and never paints"; and `DisassemblyPanel.dom.test.tsx` sets `HTMLCanvasElement.prototype.getContext = () => null` outright, so a paint assertion is vacuous **by construction of the file it would live in**. What the suite compares is the four numbers handed to `setGraphOverview` against the four inline styles `CFGBlock` writes — the same claim, one level below the pixels. **That a human sees them agree belongs in `peek-a-bin-v2u`**, alongside the other unseen surfaces.
  - **FOUR `buildCFG` CALL SITES, AND THE FOURTH IS THE ONE AN INSTRUMENT MISSES.** All four passed identical arguments: `useDisassemblyRows`' loop memo, `DisassemblyView`'s minimap memo, `CFGView`'s own layout memo, and `DisassemblyView`'s **`buildCFGForNav`** — a `useCallback` invoked from inside the arrow/Tab branch of `useDisassemblyKeyboard`, i.e. **once per keypress in graph mode**. A mount-only count cannot see it, so the count row drives a graph-mode mount **plus three arrow presses**. Reverting `buildCFGForNav` to its own `buildCFG` takes the reading from 1 to 5 (three presses plus the two builds the un-shared minimap and `CFGView` memos each did); reverting only the `graphLayout` memo to its own `buildCFG` takes it from 1 to 4. Both controls run and discriminating.
  - **The count row withholds the xref reply, and that is about DETERMINISM rather than coverage.** `instructions` and `typedXrefMap` land from two separate worker round trips, so the shared memo's inputs change once or twice depending on whether the two resolutions batch — measured at 2 running the file alone and 1 under a full `--dir src` run, and worse, the second change can land *during* the keypresses and be miscounted as a keypress build. With the xref pass held open there is exactly one input change and both readings are exact. The window it holds open is a real one every load passes through: `buildAllXrefs` is the last stage.
  - **THE `typedXrefMap.size === 0` GUARD MUST NOT BE INHERITED ONTO THE SHARED BUILD.** The `loops` memo keeps it — loop markers legitimately do not appear in the linear view until the xref pass lands, and dropping it changes that view. But `CFGView` no longer builds its own CFG, so putting that guard on the shared memo makes **graph mode render nothing** for the whole window between the disassembly arriving and `buildAllXrefs` finishing. The shared guard is `!currentFunc || instructions.length === 0` and nothing else; the control (adding the xref clause back) reddens its own row and was run.
  - **`CFGView` went from 23 props to 21**, dropping `func`/`instructions`/`typedXrefMap`/`jumpTables` for `layout`/`funcAddress`. Verified before the change that those four were read at exactly three lines (the `buildCFG` call, the `layoutCFG` call, and the memo's dependency array) plus `func.address` as a change key in the auto-centre effect — `funcAddress` is all of `func` that survived, and it is still a change key the body never reads. `CFGView.dom.test.tsx` now renders a **scripted** layout (round coordinates dagre would never assign, in an order that is not block order) and asserts the drawn boxes carry those numbers verbatim; the control — having `CFGView` re-run `layoutCFG(layout.blocks, fontSize)` — reddens both rows of that describe, the edge row included, since a recomputed edge set reclassifies a `nop`-terminated block's successor as a fallthrough.
  - **NOTHING HERE CLAIMS ANYTHING GOT FASTER.** Render *count* is measured in this repo and render *cost* is not; dagre's wall clock on a real function in a real browser has never been taken, and this change is recorded as one build and one layout where there were four and two, not as a speed-up.

- **The CSP is generated, not hand-written.** Edit `build/csp.ts`, never `nginx.conf`'s header or `index.html` directly — `build/csp.test.ts` fails on drift. A meta CSP cannot be committed into `index.html` because it is also the dev entry point and Vite injects an inline React Refresh preamble there. Note the shipped `connect-src` omits non-localhost plain `http:`, so a LAN Ghidra server is blocked on the HTTP nginx deployment.
- **Do not re-add a plugin that copies `capstone.wasm`.** Rollup already rewrites `new URL("capstone.wasm", import.meta.url)` to its hashed asset; a manual copy is pure duplication and was 1.7 MiB of the PWA precache. `capstone-wasm-guard` in `vite.config.ts` fails the build if more than one WASM asset is emitted.
- **`tools.ts` and `resources.ts` must only *type*-import `./session`.** A value import pulls in `./disasm`, which loads Capstone WASM at module scope, and both MCP suites become slow and fragile. `src/mcp/__tests__/importGraph.test.ts` enforces this.
